# Furphy Addon Manager - changelog

## Round 21 (design/UX pass)

- **Fixed** - the My Addons freshness headline no longer freezes on stale text for the whole duration of a Check now/Update all job - starting a job now re-fetches `/api/state` right away (`App.onJobStarted`), so the headline correctly flips to "Checking…" instead of showing a contradictory, out-of-date update count next to the job panel's own live progress.
- **Fixed** - "Update all" no longer silently updates an addon the player marked "Ignore updates" - its scope, the "Update all (N)" button's own count, and the "Update" status pill (which was unreachable for an ignored-but-updatable addon) now all agree that an ignored addon stays ignored unless updated individually via its own row.
- **Fixed** - Settings > Advanced's "Save addon list"/"Load addon list" feature no longer shows "Exported"/"Import addon list?"/"Import" in its toast and confirm dialog - now "Saved"/"Load addon list?"/"Load", matching the renamed buttons; the job-progress label for this same action now also reads "Loading addon list"/"Loaded addon list" instead of "Importing"/"Imported".
- **Fixed** - the Add-addon dialog now tells a player who pastes a curseforge.com page link where to find the numeric ID instead (a new hint line, plus a specific rejection message for an actual CurseForge link) - CurseForge-URL resolution itself stays removed per Eric's Round 16 request.
- **Fixed** - removed the pink update-count badge on the My Addons nav item, a third always-visible rendering of the same "updates available" number Components.Freshness already shows (sidebar dot + My Addons headline) - left over from before the Round 14 freshness redesign and never reconciled with UX-SPEC.md's "one fact, one place" principle; `SPEC.md`'s older nav layout line updated to match.
- **Fixed (accessibility)** - Dark and Light themes' `--text-faint` token (secondary text: addon author, placeholders, freshness clause, status lines, drawer/About meta, and more) failed WCAG AA contrast against `--bg-1`/`--bg-3` in both legacy themes; both values corrected and verified at ≥4.5:1 against both surfaces.
- **Fixed** - Lofi Night's brand-cat decoration no longer truncates the sidebar wordmark ("Furphy Addon Ma…") at the app's default window size - it's now positioned as a corner badge instead of sitting inside the wordmark's own flex row.
- **Fixed** - the sidebar's "Update & Open Battle.net Classic"/"...Classic Era" CTA no longer visibly overflows its own pill once a non-Retail flavour is active - it now wraps to two lines instead of spilling text past the button's rounded edge.
- **Fixed** - the tray's right-click context menu item read "Check for updates now", a stale hybrid of the pre-rename copy; it now reads "Check now", matching the toolbar button and UX-SPEC's copy table so the action has one name everywhere.
- **Fixed** - the multi-flavour tray balloon/tooltip said "N updates ready" for a background cycle that had already installed those updates - present tense implying pending action for a completed event. It now reads "Updated N at HH:MM (Retail: 2, Classic: 1)", matching the single-flavour balloon's past tense and Settings' "Running - updated N at HH:MM" status line; FLAVORS-SPEC.md section 5.6's example text corrected to match.
- **Fixed** - `install.ps1`'s final install-complete console message still advertised the CurseForge API key feature removed entirely in Round 16 ("An optional key in Settings adds official CurseForge metadata...") and called the search screen "Browse", its pre-Round-15 name; collapsed to one accurate line: "No CurseForge API key needed - Get new addons and installs both work out of the box."
- **Fixed** - `install.ps1`'s console narration during the take-over scan (first run / re-run) still said "adopt"/"Adopting"/"adoption" in every printed line, while the app itself renamed this action to "Take over" everywhere visible back in section 2.4; all seven console lines now say "take over"/"Taking over"/"taking over" to match, and the line listing folders left unmanaged no longer prints the raw ".toc" file-extension jargon (a banned term).
- **Fixed** - `README.txt` (copied into every install's own folder by `install.ps1`/`package.ps1`/`deploy.ps1`) still documented the removed CurseForge API key feature, the pre-Round-15 "Browse" screen with categories/sorting, the banned terms "untracked"/"adopt", and the old "Check for updates" toolbar label; rewrote the "NO API KEY NEEDED", "THE APP", and "FILES" sections to match the app's current keyless Get new addons screen, "Check now" button, and "doesn't manage yet"/"take over" wording.

## Round 20 (adversarial bug pass)

- **Fixed** - `staging\` is now nested under `flavours\<id>\` (like `addons.json`/`state.json`/`backups\` already were) instead of living once at the install root. Two flavours syncing at the same time - the "Update All" button and the tray's scheduled update-all-flavours cycle both do this by design - no longer wipe and recreate the same folder out from under each other or extract the same CurseForge/Wago project id into the same path, so a job for one flavour can no longer fail with "Download did not produce a file" / "directory is not empty", and one flavour can no longer end up with another flavour's extracted files. `addon-sync.ps1` only; `addon-server.ps1` never referenced `staging\` directly, so no other component's contract changed.
- **Fixed (security)** - the listener now binds `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` instead of a bare `http://localhost:<port>/`, which did not actually restrict it to loopback - a LAN client could reach every API endpoint by connecting to this machine's real IP with a spoofed `Host: localhost` header. `?host=webview2`/browser-launch URLs are unaffected.
- **Fixed (security)** - PUT/POST endpoints now reject cross-origin and non-JSON-content-type requests (a new same-origin check plus a stricter Content-Type check on request bodies), closing a CSRF hole where any web page open in the user's browser could silently install/remove addons, rewrite settings, or register Windows startup persistence just by having the server running - the SPA's own same-origin calls are unaffected.
- **Fixed (security)** - `addon-sync.ps1` child-process arguments (project ids, file ids, paths) are now uniformly quoted/escaped before being handed to `Start-Process`, closing a command-line injection hole where a value containing a literal space (e.g. a crafted projectId of `"1 -Remove 999"`) could smuggle an extra CLI flag into the child process and trigger an operation the caller never asked for.
- **Fixed (security)** - `POST /api/open {what:"url"}` now validates the target URL structurally (parsed scheme + exact host match, same as the existing `cf-window` path) instead of a plain string-prefix check, closing a hole where a URL with an embedded space could inject extra `msedge.exe` command-line switches past the curseforge.com/wago.io allowlist.
- **Fixed** - `PUT /api/settings` now returns a clean 400 (e.g. "port must be a number") instead of a raw 500 with an internal .NET exception message when `port`/`releaseType`/`backgroundIntervalMinutes` are sent as a non-numeric value.
- **Fixed** - `PUT /api/settings` boolean fields (`adFilter`, `cfFocus`, `autoUpdateOnLaunch`, `backgroundUpdates`, `runAtStartup`, `showTestRealms`) no longer flip ON when sent the literal string `"false"` (or `"0"`/`"no"`/`"off"`/empty) - those now correctly save as off, matching what any reasonable caller would expect.
- **Fixed** - the idle auto-shutdown timer now checks whether any flavour still has a job running (same check `POST /api/shutdown` already does) before exiting, so a long sync left unattended past `-IdleMinutes` no longer gets the whole server torn down mid-job and the CLI child process orphaned.
- **Fixed** - `GET /api/jobs/{id}` no longer 404s a job that is still genuinely running just because 20+ other jobs completed on other flavours and rotated it out of the short rolling history - it now falls back to the same live per-flavour job map `GET /api/jobs` and `POST /api/shutdown` already consult.
- **Fixed** - `host\FurphyHost.cs`'s tray, which POSTs `/api/jobs` directly (not through the browser), now sets a `Referer` header on that call so it still passes the new same-origin check above - without this it would have been rejected as a foreign-origin request the same as an actual attacker.
- **Fixed** - Wago search on the Get new addons screen now ignores a search response that comes back after the user has already changed the search box text, instead of letting a slower response for an old query silently overwrite the results shown for the query currently typed.
- **Fixed** - turning off Settings > Browsing "Filter ads and trackers" now actually stops hiding ad elements in the CurseForge pane right away, instead of the ad-hiding style staying stuck on for the rest of that app session once it had ever been turned on.
- **Fixed** - the host window now responds to Windows moving it to a monitor with a different display scaling (DPI), resizing itself to the size Windows suggests for the new monitor instead of keeping its old pixel size; the CurseForge pane resyncs through its normal resize-driven path once that happens.
- **Fixed (security)** - `install.ps1 -Uninstall` now skips real-Desktop shortcut removal with a warning (instead of deleting them) when the target `-WowPath`/install folder looks like a scratch or test root (under `%TEMP%`, a `\scratch\` folder, or `fixtures\wowroot`) and `-NoShortcuts` was not passed - `-NoShortcuts` is still the normal opt-out, but a caller who simply forgets it against a scratch root can no longer reproduce the earlier incident that deleted the real Desktop shortcuts; a genuine production install is unaffected.
- **Fixed** - `install.ps1 -Uninstall` no longer crashes and aborts partway through if a file it's removing is locked by another process (antivirus, search indexer, OneDrive, an open editor, etc.) - it now skips the locked item, keeps removing everything else, and finishes with a clear "completed with warnings" message and exit code 0 instead of a raw .NET exception and exit code 1, so the app folder is never left half-installed/half-uninstalled.
- **Fixed** - the repo mirror's `.gitignore` now also excludes `tray-state.json`, `server.pid`, and any `*.WebView2\` runtime-cache folder, matching the other per-install-state entries already ignored there, so a manual dev run inside the repo checkout can't accidentally get these committed.

- **Fixed (follow-up to security-2)** - `curseforge-handler.vbs` and `deploy.ps1` now send a same-origin `Referer`/`Origin` header on their POSTs; without it the new CSRF guard rejected the protocol handler's install jobs and the deploy script's shutdown call. Any non-browser client of the local API must send `Origin: http://localhost:<port>` (or a same-origin `Referer`) on non-GET requests.

- **Fixed (follow-up to security-1)** - the listener now binds both loopback literals, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` and `http://[::1]:<port>/`. Binding only IPv4 made every `localhost` client wait out a refused IPv6 connect first (1-2 s per connection; short-timeout health checks failed). The LAN remains unreachable.

## Round 19 (multi-version: Retail, Classic, Classic Era)

Furphy now manages addons for Classic and Classic Era, not just Retail, alongside detected-but-hidden-by-default PTR/XPTR/Beta support - one app, one server, one port, one `curseforge://` registration, exactly as before. `FLAVORS-SPEC.md` (repo root) is the authoritative design doc (principles, detection rules, the full data model/migration, every CLI/server/UI/launcher change, the synthetic test fixture, and the seven ordered change sets, CS-F1 through CS-F7, that implemented it) - this entry summarizes what shipped in each and records this round's (CS-F7's) own doc/fixture work and fresh acceptance-checklist re-run. `SPEC.md`'s new "E27" section documents the additive API/settings contract; `UX-SPEC.md` documents the switcher/badges/copy. **Load-bearing promise, proven throughout:** on a machine with exactly one installed flavour - including this one - every file path, API shape, and UI pixel is byte-identical to before this round shipped.

### addon-sync.ps1 (CS-F1)

- **Added** - `Get-InstalledFlavours -WowRoot <path> [-ScriptRoot <path>]`, detecting up to six known WoW client folders (`_retail_`/`_classic_`/`_classic_era_`/`_ptr_`/`_xptr_`/`_beta_`) in a fixed order, each corroborated (not gated) by a `.build.info` row match; a generic fallback surfaces an unrecognized-but-plausible 7th folder as an "Unknown flavour", disabled by default.
- **Added** - `Resolve-ClassicProgressionTypeId -Interface <int>` - the single appendable table resolving Classic's rolling progression era (Vanilla through Mists Classic, live as of 2026-09-04) from the installed client's own Interface number to a CurseForge `gameVersionTypeId`/Wago `game_version`/`.toc` suffix, live-re-derivable with zero code changes when Blizzard ships the next expansion.
- **Added** - `-Flavor <id>` (validated against `Get-InstalledFlavours`, defaults to `retail` when installed - byte-identical to every existing invocation) and `-WowRoot <path>` (overrides normal WoW-root auto-detection, never touches the real WoW folder) params, threaded through `Resolve-AddonsPath`, `Get-ClientBuildInfo`, `Select-CfFile`/`Test-FileHasGameVersionPrefix`, `Select-WagoRelease`, `Get-PrimaryTocFile`, `Get-TocInterfaceValues`, and every downstream toc-derived helper.
- **Fixed** - `Get-PrimaryTocFile`'s bare-`<Folder>.toc` fallback ownership rule: a first draft incorrectly reserved bare-name files for Retail unconditionally; the corrected 3-group (Retail / Classic Era / Classic-progression-collapsed) rule claims the bare file for whichever single group has no suffixed sibling present, defaulting to Retail only when that isn't exactly one group - verified byte-identical to pre-change output against real Details/WeakAuras/AngryKeystones samples that ship Classic/TBC/Wrath/Cata/Mists siblings alongside the bare Retail file.
- **Added** - `Invoke-FlavourMigration -RootPath <path>` - the zero-data-loss, copy-first, idempotent, crash-retry-safe migration moving an existing top-level `addons.json`/`state.json`/`backups\` into `flavours\retail\`, called once at CLI startup before any path resolution.
- Every `-Json` output gains two additive top-level fields: `flavour` (resolved id) and `installedFlavours` (string[]).

### addon-server.ps1 (CS-F2, CS-F3)

- **Added (CS-F2)** - the CLI's detection/resolution helpers duplicated verbatim (existing established pattern); `-WowRoot` param; a per-request flavour-resolution layer (`Resolve-RequestFlavour`/`Set-CurrentFlavourContext`, called once per dispatched request by `Invoke-Route`) backing `$Script:FlavourScopedEndpoints` - the enumerated addon-scoped endpoint list a startup self-check asserts against every registered route; per-flavour `Test-JobBusy` scoping (a Retail sync and a Classic Era sync now run concurrently; two Retail syncs still can't); `job.flavour` on every job object; the `{kind:"update-all-flavours"}` bulk job kind (fans out one sync per installed, non-hidden flavour); `/api/state`'s additive `installedFlavours`/`activeFlavour`/`flavour` fields; `settings.json`'s `schemaVersion`/`activeFlavour`/`showTestRealms` keys.
- **Fixed (CS-F2)** - `POST /api/jobs` was initially placed in the router-level flavour-scoped list, which 400'd `update-all-flavours` before its own request body (naming the one job kind that doesn't need a specific flavour) was ever read; moved that enforcement into `Handle-JobsPost` itself instead.
- **Added (CS-F3)** - `Resolve-CfInstallFlavour` (S5.5's install-flavour resolution): a single-target CurseForge add/install with no explicit `?flavour=` on a multi-flavour machine now auto-targets the one matching flavour silently, fails immediately with a plain no-support message on zero matches, or creates a new `awaiting_flavour` job carrying `.choices` on multiple matches (resumed by re-posting the identical body with `?flavour=` now explicit).
- Verified live against three real, stable CurseForge projects spanning all three cases (retail-only, multi-flavour via file-list union, zero WoW-flavour match) - see the acceptance re-run below for a fresh confirmation this round.

### ui/app.js, ui/index.html (CS-F4)

- **Added** - the flavour switcher (`Components.Switcher`) - a compact pill row mounted after a stable `#flavour-switcher-anchor`, inserted/removed **wholesale** (never `display:none`) below two visible flavours; an "Update All" button alongside it; per-flavour badges on the job panel, About panel, and Settings > Game folders; "Update & Play [Label]"/"Update & Open Battle.net [Label]" button text and toast wording per flavour; the CS-F3 install-flavour picker restyled to reuse the switcher's own pill component; Wago search's `game_version` now follows the active flavour automatically; "Retail Patches" renamed "Patches" in the Wago versions table header (touched regardless of flavour count, since the data source was touched anyway).
- **Added** - `Store.hasMultipleFlavours()` (raw installed count, matches the server's own `?flavour=`-required gate) kept deliberately distinct from `Store.visibleFlavours()` (test-realms-filtered, drives everything UI-facing) - a Retail-plus-hidden-PTR machine must still send `?flavour=` even though the switcher itself shows nothing.
- **Fixed** - a first Mock (`?mock=1&flavours=N`) draft returned `activeFlavour` from the persisted setting instead of the per-request resolved flavour, diverging from the real server's contract and causing a just-clicked pill's own addon list to update while its highlight lagged; corrected to match the real contract exactly.

### install.ps1, deploy.ps1, package.ps1 (CS-F5)

- **Added** - `Find-WowRoot` accepts any known WoW flavour folder, not just `_retail_` (unblocks a Classic-only machine); the app's home roots at Retail when present (byte-identical upgrade path) else the first-detected flavour in fixed order; a launcher `.cmd`/`.vbs` pair is written into every installed first-class flavour's own folder with that flavour's `-Flavor` id, Battle.net product code, and an honest non-Retail launch-reliability comment; one desktop shortcut per first-class flavour (today's exact unlabeled shortcut at n=1); the "adopt existing folders" step now loops over every installed flavour, passing both `-Flavor` and `-AddonsPath` so each lands in its own `flavours\<id>\` rather than being misfiled under `flavours\retail\`.
- **Fixed** - `-Uninstall`'s `$keepDirs` allow-list had not been updated to include `flavours\`, which would have silently deleted every installed flavour's `addons.json`/`state.json`/`backups` on uninstall.
- **Fixed** - the adopt-loop's "could not read scan results" check treated a successfully-parsed-but-genuinely-empty untracked array as a parse failure (a PowerShell falsy-empty-collection gotcha), surfaced live against the fixture's empty `_ptr_` AddOns folder.
- **Added** - `deploy.ps1`'s backup-zip tar excludes extended to skip each flavour's own re-downloadable `backups\` and the migration safety-copy folder, while still capturing every flavour's `addons.json`/`state.json`; `package.ps1` needed no code change (its allow-list already excludes `flavours\` by construction) - a comment was added warning against ever adding a generic root-copy step without excluding it.
- **Incident, disclosed and resolved:** a CS-F5 verification pass running `-Uninstall` against a scratch fixture without `-NoShortcuts` deleted this machine's two real desktop shortcuts ("Furphy Addon Manager.lnk", "WoW (auto-update addons).lnk") - the shortcut-removal step is not scoped to `-WowPath`/`-WowPath`, a pre-existing design choice, not something CS-F5 introduced. The live install itself was untouched (confirmed via file mtimes); the two shortcuts were manually recreated after the incident. **Lesson recorded for every future session:** always pass `-NoShortcuts` when testing `-Uninstall` against any fixture copy.

### host\FurphyHost.cs (CS-F6)

- **Added** - the tray's scheduled sync now POSTs a single `{"kind":"update-all-flavours"}` job instead of a bare `{"kind":"sync"}`, fanning out to one job per installed, non-hidden flavour via addon-server.ps1's own CS-F2 fan-out; new `FlavourJobResult`/`FlavourLabels`/`ExtractFlavourJobs`/`ApplyFinishedJob`/`CompleteMultiFlavourCycle` types classify and aggregate the results into one tooltip line per FLAVORS-SPEC.md section 5.6 (single flavour: byte-identical to the pre-round tooltip text; multiple flavours, all up to date: still one combined line, never once per flavour; mixed/failed: aggregated counts across every flavour; updates ready: a "Label: count" breakdown per flavour with updates) - the polling loop iterates every flavour's own job under one shared 15-minute cycle cap. The `--tray-selftest` marker gained a `flavourJobs` array (one row per flavour job) for harness assertions.
- `WowDetector.KnownWowNames` was reviewed and found already complete (`Wow`, `Wow-64`, `WowClassic`, `WowClassicT`, `WowClassicB`, `WowT`, `WowB`) - left unchanged, since no recon source in this repo documents anything beyond what it already covers.
- **Filed, not fixed here (task_d2b170d3):** a pre-existing concurrency bug in `addon-sync.ps1`'s shared (non-flavour-scoped) `staging\` folder makes concurrent per-flavour syncs intermittently race on `Remove-Item`, occasionally failing one flavour's job - reproduced live twice during CS-F6's own verification; out of that change set's file scope (`host\FurphyHost.cs` only), flagged for a future round to fix in `addon-sync.ps1`/`addon-server.ps1`.

### fixtures\wowroot\, docs (CS-F7, this round's own entry)

- **Added** - `fixtures\README.md`, explaining the fixture's tree, what each addon folder proves (the `FakeAddon`/`MultiFlavourAddon` bare-file-ownership and X-Flavor-tag regression cases specifically), the `.build.info` field-count gotcha in `FLAVORS-SPEC.md` section 8's own literal sample text (a stray double-pipe there produces 16 fields against a 15-field header if copied verbatim - this fixture's checked-in file already uses the corrected single-pipe form), and exactly how to point `-WowRoot`/`-WowPath` at it from the CLI, server, and installer without ever touching a real WoW folder.
- **Confirmed** - `fixtures\wowroot\` already matched FLAVORS-SPEC.md section 8's tree exactly (built by CS-F1, maintained pristine by every later change set) - re-verified file-by-file this round (12 files: 4x `Wow.exe` stub, 1x `.build.info`, 7x `.toc`, including the empty `_ptr_\Interface\AddOns\` proving PTR is detected-but-empty-by-default) and left unmodified.
- **Added** - `SPEC.md`'s new "E27" section (settings shape, `?flavour=` API contract, `/api/state` shape, the migration algorithm, and the rollback instructions - see the callout box below); `UX-SPEC.md` gained the switcher (§2.5), job-panel badge (§4.3), install-flavour picker (§5.3), per-flavour Game-folders/About rows and two new Settings fields (§6.2), 19 new copy-table rows (§7), a do-not-change bullet (§9), and three new acceptance-checklist items (§11); `README.md` gained a "Multiple WoW versions" paragraph; `ROADMAP.md`'s E27 entry marks the item DONE and updates "Up next" to reflect it (the item was previously listed there as "not started").
- **Hygiene, this round:** `node --check ui\app.js` clean; `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` on `addon-sync.ps1` and `addon-server.ps1` both zero errors; byte-scanned every `.ps1`/`.cs` file touched by CS-F1 through CS-F6 (none touched by CS-F7 itself) - zero bytes above 127 confirmed still true.

### Migration and rollback (FLAVORS-SPEC.md section 3.3, S3.3 point 7 - verbatim)

Runs once, automatically, at CLI and server startup, before any path resolution (`Invoke-FlavourMigration`). A pre-flavour install (`settings.json` has no `schemaVersion`, or it's `1`) has its existing top-level `addons.json`/`state.json`/`backups\` **copied** (not moved) into `flavours\_migration-backup-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>\` first - cheap (same volume, same parent) and it is the manual undo path - then **moved** into `flavours\retail\`, each item guarded individually by its own `Test-Path` check so a crash-and-retry mid-migration never re-copies the backup or overwrites an already-landed file with stale content. `schemaVersion: 2` is set in `settings.json` only after every move succeeds. The migration-backup folder is never auto-deleted, and is listed (never hidden) in Settings > Advanced > Troubleshooting alongside "Open logs folder."

**Documented rollback:** reinstalling an older Furphy build over an already-migrated folder will not find `addons.json`/`state.json` at the top level (an old build doesn't know about `flavours\`) - so the rollback is: **restore from `flavours\_migration-backup-<timestamp>\`** - copy that folder's contents back up to the AddonSync folder's top level, then delete the `flavours\` directory entirely. This restores the exact pre-migration file layout an older build expects, and is always available for as long as the migration-backup folder is left in place (it never is auto-deleted).

### Acceptance checklist (FLAVORS-SPEC.md section 8) - re-run fresh this round (CS-F7)

Every checkbox re-verified against the checked-in `fixtures\wowroot\` (or scratch copies of it, never the fixture itself, and never a real WoW folder), using throwaway scratch copies of `addon-sync.ps1`/`addon-server.ps1`/`install.ps1` (never the build root's own copies) on port 47899, freed after every run. The fixture was confirmed pristine (12 files, unchanged) before and after this entire pass.

- [x] `Get-InstalledFlavours -WowRoot <fixture>` returns exactly `{retail, classic, classic_era, ptr}`, section 2.1 order, none `buildInfoMissing` - confirmed via `-Status -Json` for all four flavours: `installedFlavours` list identical and correctly ordered on every call.
- [x] Each entry's `clientBuild` matches its `.build.info` row's `Version` string exactly - `retail` 12.1.0.69587/120100, `classic` 5.5.4.61180/50504, `classic_era` 1.15.9.60546/11509, `ptr` 12.1.0.69588/120100, all confirmed byte-for-byte against the fixture's own `.build.info`.
- [x] `classic`'s resolved progression era = Mists, derived from Interface 50504, re-derivable with zero code change - confirmed via a direct `Resolve-ClassicProgressionTypeId -Interface 50504` call (`{EraKey:"mists", TypeId:79434, WagoValue:"mop"}`), then edited a scratch copy's `.build.info` `wow_classic` row to a Cataclysm-range version (4.4.2.55555, Interface 40402) and re-ran `-Status -Json`: `clientInterface` correctly flipped to 40402 and a direct `Resolve-ClassicProgressionTypeId -Interface 40402` call returned `{EraKey:"cata", TypeId:77522, WagoValue:"cata"}` - zero code touched between the two calls.
- [x] `-Flavor retail -Scan` finds `SingleFlavourAddon` only; `-Flavor classic_era -Scan` finds `MultiFlavourAddon` (title read as "Multi Flavour Addon (Vanilla)", proving the Vanilla `.toc` was selected) and `PreExistingEraAddon`; `-Flavor classic -Scan` finds `FakeAddon` (title read as "Fake Addon (Mists)", proving the decoy bare file was correctly skipped) - all three confirmed live this round with real `-Json` output.
- [x] `Get-PrimaryTocFile -Flavor classic_era` on `MultiFlavourAddon` picks `MultiFlavourAddon_Vanilla.toc` (X-Flavor: Vanilla), not the bare Mainline-tagged file - proven by the `-Scan` title above.
- [x] `Get-PrimaryTocFile -Flavor classic` on `FakeAddon` picks `FakeAddon_Mists.toc`, not the bare decoy - proven by the `-Scan` title above.
- [x] `Get-PrimaryTocFile -Flavor retail` against real pre-existing fixtures (`servertest\AddOns\Details`, `servertest\AddOns\WeakAuras`) is byte-identical to pre-change output - re-confirmed this round via a functions-only dot-sourced extract: both correctly still pick their bare `Details.toc`/`WeakAuras.toc` despite Classic/TBC/Wrath/Cata/Mists siblings present in the same folders.
- [x] Deleting `_classic_\` from a scratch copy and re-running `Get-InstalledFlavours` drops it from the result with no error - confirmed this round (`installedFlavours` correctly became `{retail, classic_era, ptr}`, `-Status -Json` for `-Flavor retail` still succeeded).
- [x] `_ptr_` is detected but absent from the switcher/tray-sync list by default; `showTestRealms: true` surfaces it - detection re-confirmed this round (`ptr` present in every `installedFlavours` list); the UI/tray visibility gating itself was verified live by CS-F4 (`?mock=1&flavours=4`, PTR appears only after toggling the setting) and CS-F6 (`--tray-selftest`, PTR excluded from `flavourJobs` with `showTestRealms` off) in their own sessions - not re-run live this round (Browser pane was unavailable this session; see below), but reconfirmed by static code inspection of `Store.visibleFlavours()`/`Components.Switcher.render()` in `ui\app.js`.
- [x] Running `Invoke-FlavourMigration` against a copy of this build root's own real top-level `addons.json`/`state.json`/`backups\` produces `flavours\retail\addons.json`/`state.json` byte-identical to the originals (re-confirmed via checksum this round) plus an untouched `flavours\_migration-backup-<timestamp>\` holding the same content, `schemaVersion: 2` set, and `backups\<id>\<id>.zip` files present unmoved until the move step.
- [x] Re-running the migration after simulating a crash mid-move (hand-populated `flavours\retail\addons.json` with placeholder content before re-running) does not overwrite the already-landed file with stale backup content, and does not create a second backup folder - re-confirmed this round: placeholder content survived verbatim, `state.json`/`backups\` still moved correctly, exactly one backup folder existed.
- [x] `install.ps1` against a fixture copy with `_retail_` deleted still succeeds, home-rooting at `_classic_`/`_classic_era_` per section 2.1 order - re-confirmed this round against a classic+classic_era+ptr copy (no retail): installer correctly rooted home at Classic, wrote launcher pairs for Classic and Classic Era (PTR excluded), with zero desktop/shortcut/protocol interaction (`-NoShortcuts -NoProtocol -SkipAdopt`).
- [x] A CurseForge test file whose `gameVersionTypeIds` matches only `classic_era` auto-targets it with no prompt; one matching both `retail` and `classic_era` produces an `awaiting_flavour` job; one matching neither fails with the plain no-support message - re-confirmed this round via a direct `Resolve-CfInstallFlavour` call (functions-only extract, real live CurseForge data, not mocked) against three real, stable projects: 1521253/BonusRollConfirm -> `{retail}` only; 65387/WeakAuras -> `{retail, classic, classic_era}`; 318098 (a non-WoW project) -> `{}` (zero matches) - all three exactly as CS-F3's own original live verification found.
- [x] Two concurrent mocked jobs, one `?flavour=retail` one `?flavour=classic_era`, run without blocking each other; a request to a flavour-scoped endpoint missing `?flavour=` on the multi-flavour fixture returns 400, the same request against a single-flavour state succeeds without the param - re-confirmed this round against a real scratch `addon-server.ps1` on port 47899 pointed at the fixture: `GET /api/state` with no `?flavour=` -> 400; `?flavour=retail`/`?flavour=classic_era` both succeeded with correct, distinct `clientBuild`/`clientInterface`; `POST /api/jobs?flavour=retail {kind:sync}` -> 202, an immediate second same-flavour POST -> 409 busy, an immediate `?flavour=classic_era` POST -> 202 and ran to completion (`state:"done"`, `exitCode:0`, `flavour:"retail"`/`"classic_era"` respectively) concurrently with the first.
- [x] `?mock=1` UI test: switcher shows exactly the mocked flavours in section 2.1 order; switching pills swaps My Addons' list/freshness without a full reload; on a mocked single-flavour response the switcher DOM is entirely absent (inspected directly, not just visually hidden) - **not re-run live this round**: the Browser pane tool was unavailable in this session (`navigate`/`tabs_create` both denied). Re-confirmed instead by (a) reading `Components.Switcher.render()` in `ui\app.js` directly - it calls `node.remove()` (wholesale DOM removal) when `Store.visibleFlavours().length <= 1`, matching the acceptance bar exactly, not a `style.display` toggle - and (b) citing CS-F4's own session, which drove this exact check live via a Chrome DevTools Protocol headless-Edge session and confirmed `document.getElementById("flavour-switcher")` returns `null` at n<=1 and exactly 3/4 pills render in order at n=3/4 with correct pill-switch behavior (see CS-F4's own entry above for that session's full detail).

All checklist items pass. The two items not re-run live this round (both UI-visibility checks that need a live browser) were re-confirmed by direct source inspection of the exact code path the acceptance bar tests, plus a citation of the change set that already drove them live end-to-end - flagged explicitly rather than silently reused, per this round's own task brief asking for the checklist to be run and its results pasted.

## Round 18 (background tray - stage B, host fixes, and a 14-theme picker)

Stage A (the actual `--tray` background-updater process in `host\FurphyHost.cs` - NotifyIcon, scheduling loop, WoW-running skip, `tray-state.json`, the Mutex/Event pair, HKCU Run string) shipped earlier this same day as part of v1.4.0 and is documented in `OVERNIGHT-REPORT.md`'s "Rounds 16.5 + 17 + tray stage A" entry (no dedicated CHANGELOG section existed for it - a gap, not backfilled here; this round covers stage B only). Full design lives in `SPEC.md`'s new "E25" section (settings keys, all six endpoints, the exact HKCU Run value string, `tray-state.json`'s shape, and the "one action" independence rule); this round's own task brief is the tray contract referenced throughout.

### addon-server.ps1

- **Added** - three new `settings.json` keys, round-tripped by `Get-DefaultSettings`/`Get-Settings`/`Get-SettingsView`/`Handle-SettingsPut` exactly the way `adFilter`/`cfFocus` already are: `backgroundUpdates` (bool, default `false`), `backgroundIntervalMinutes` (int, default `120`, CLAMPED - not rejected - to 30..1440 on every read and every write), `runAtStartup` (bool, default `false`). Same defaults/clamp `host\FurphyHost.cs`'s own `TraySettingsReader` (stage A) already uses, so both readers of `settings.json` agree.
- **Added** - a new "Tray + start-with-Windows" section with six routes: `GET /api/tray/status` (`{running, state, startupRegistered}` - `running` is computed, not read verbatim off `state.running`, so a stale `tray-state.json` left by a crash can never report a phantom "running": true only when the state file says running AND that pid is a live process AND it's actually named `FurphyHost`), `POST /api/tray/start` (`Start-Process host\bin\FurphyHost.exe --tray`; `202` on success, `409` if already running, `501` if the exe was never built), `POST /api/tray/stop` (signals the `"FurphyAddonManager.TrayStop"` EventWaitHandle; `200 {ok:true}` or `200 {ok:false, reason:"not running"}` - never a 500 for "nothing to stop"), `POST /api/startup/register` / `POST /api/startup/unregister` (write/remove the HKCU Run value, byte-identical to stage A's own `StartupRegistry.BuildRunValue` format), `GET /api/startup/status` (`{registered}`).

### install.ps1

- **Fixed** - `-Uninstall` previously deleted `host\` (including a possibly-still-running `FurphyHost.exe --tray`) without ever stopping it or removing the Run value first. Now: removes the HKCU Run value first (so a logon mid-uninstall can't relaunch the tray), signals `"FurphyAddonManager.TrayStop"` (try/catch - no error if no tray is running), then polls for up to 10 seconds for the real `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe` process to exit before the existing file-removal pass runs - a warning, not a hard failure, if it's still running past that window, so uninstall always completes. A normal install/upgrade path (no `-Uninstall`) is untouched and never starts the tray.

### ui/index.html, ui/app.js

- **Added** - Settings > Updates gains three rows below the existing "Update addons before WoW starts" toggle, never hidden (a plain Windows background process, not tied to the native host's own CurseForge pane): "Update addons in the background" (`#toggle-background-updates`), an interval `<select>` shown only while that's on ("Every hour"/"Every 2 hours"/"Every 4 hours"/"Every 8 hours"/"Once a day" -> 60/120/240/480/1440), and "Start with Windows" (`#toggle-run-at-startup`) - plus one muted status line (`#updates-background-status`) fed by `GET /api/tray/status`: "Background updates off" / "Running - last check HH:MM: everything up to date" / "Running - updated N at HH:MM" / "Running - N failed at HH:MM" / "Waiting - WoW is running" (plus two same-style fallbacks for shapes outside those five). Fed by `Store.state.trayStatus`, refreshed immediately on entering Settings and by the normal 5s idle poll while Settings stays active - no extra request on any other screen.
- **Added** - `Actions.setBackgroundUpdates`/`Actions.setRunAtStartup`: the background-updates toggle does one settings save then starts/stops the tray to match. The startup toggle keeps OFF fully independent (unregisters only), but turning it ON while background updates is currently off does the task brief's "one action, plainly labelled" - a single `PUT /api/settings` carrying both flags, one "Settings saved." toast, then register + tray-start, rather than two separate saves.
- **Added** - `ui\app.js`'s dev-only `Mock` (`?mock=1`) gains all three settings keys plus a fake in-memory `mockTray`/`mockStartupRegistered` backing `GET/POST /api/tray/*` and `POST/GET /api/startup/*` - starting fabricates one plausible completed `up_to_date` cycle immediately (no real process, no timer), so the whole flow is exercisable offline.

### host\FurphyHost.cs

- **Fixed (high)** - `settings.json`'s `cfFocus` is now AUTHORITATIVE over the CurseForge pane's own `localStorage['cfFocusEnabled']` cache. Previously the per-document injected script's `readStoredEnabled()` let a stale localStorage value (left over from an earlier in-app toggle, a leftover test profile, etc.) silently win forever, because the host only ever re-pushed the setting into the page when its OWN in-memory `_cfFocusEnabled` happened to change (`CfWebView_NavigationStarting`'s `freshCfFocus != _cfFocusEnabled` check) - never merely because the page's own cached value might already disagree with settings.json without the host's own view ever having changed. New `SyncCfFocusToPage(context)` re-reads `ReadCfFocusSetting()` and unconditionally pushes it into the page (`PushCfFocusLive` - writes `localStorage['cfFocusEnabled']` and calls the page's exposed `window.__cfFocusSetEnabled` if already booted) on every `CfWebView_NavigationCompleted`, plus once right after `CfWebView_InitCompleted`'s `EnsureCfFocusInfra()` call - idempotent, so a normal run where nothing ever drifted does the same no-op writes it always did. Logs one `host.log` line only when the freshly-read value differs from the host's own last-known `_cfFocusEnabled`, so a steady-state run stays silent on every navigation. The live in-app toggle path (`HandleCfFocusMessage` -> `ApplyCfFocusToggle`) and `NavigationStarting`'s own out-of-band resync are both unchanged and still work exactly as before.
- **Changed** - `InitializeDefaultTheme()`'s built-in cold-start palette is now Vaporwave (`bg0 #12081f`, `bg1 #1a0b2e`, `bg2 #241640`, `bg3 #2d1b4e`, `border #3a2a5c`, `text #f3e9ff`, `muted #b9a6d6`, `accent #ff71ce`, `_themeName = "vaporwave"`) instead of Lofi Night, matching `THEMES-SPEC.md` section 2's "Vaporwave's 8-color host palette" verbatim (byte-confirmed against `ui\style.css`'s own unmodified `:root[data-theme="vaporwave"]` block) - so a brand-new profile with no persisted `hostTheme` paints the right title bar color before the page ever reports in, matching round 12's SPA-side default flip. `LoadPersistedTheme()` is untouched: an existing user's persisted `hostTheme` (Lofi Night or otherwise) still overlays and wins exactly as before - this only changes what a fresh profile seeds with. Two stale comments elsewhere in the file that described the built-in default as "Lofi Night" are updated to match.

### ui/style.css (Set A - theme expansion, ten new themes)

- **Added** - the ten new Appearance options chosen and specced end-to-end in `THEMES-SPEC.md` section 1 (judge-scored, contrast-verified), bringing the total to 14 alongside the existing Dark/Light/Vaporwave/Lofi Night (which are byte-for-byte unchanged, confirmed by diffing this round's file against the pre-round copy outside the two new insertion points). Each is a complete `:root[data-theme="X"]` token block (every token Dark defines, plus whatever extra tokens - `--secondary`, `--accent-active`, geometry - that theme's own brief calls for) copied verbatim from the spec, its own `.select` dropdown-chevron override, and a `.theme-swatch[data-theme-value="X"]` swatch-preview rule colocated with the block per section 3.2 (inert until the picker markup itself lands in a later change set - THEMES-SPEC.md's own Set B - but keeping the palette and its swatch preview as one source of truth from day one): **Terminal Green** (`terminal-green` - a CRT glowing alone in a dark server room), **Arctic Ice** (`arctic-ice` - a glacier field at noon, the calm motion-free anchor of the set), **Art Deco** (`art-deco-gold` - a 1920s lobby at closing time, one disciplined gold accent), **Alpine Dawn** (`alpine-dawn` - blue hour on a snowfield, five minutes before sunrise), **Matcha** (`matcha` - a quiet tea-ceremony room at midday, the antidote to Vaporwave's neon buzz), **Desert Night** (`desert-night` - a cold star-strung desert well after sunset), **Tokyo Rain** (`tokyo-rain` - a crosswalk at 1am, magenta signage over wet asphalt), **Brushed Steel** (`brushed-steel` - a machine-shop workbench at night, industrial and precise), **Aurora Sky** (`aurora-sky` - a black arctic sky torn open by ribbons of green and violet light), **Strawberry Cream** (`strawberry-cream` - a dessert-shop counter, soft and sweet without tipping into pastel-illegible).
- **Added** - each theme's own signature-touch CSS (its "personality on top" layer, same discipline as Vaporwave's perspective grid / Lofi Night's cityscape already in this file): CRT scanlines + phosphor glow + gated flicker (Terminal Green), static frost-glow corners with zero motion (Arctic Ice), a diagonal gold hairline lattice plus a double-hairline frame on the active nav item and settings cards (Art Deco), a static ridgeline silhouette with a first-light band (Alpine Dawn), a small teacup with breathing steam low in the sidebar (Matcha), a dune-ridge silhouette with a gated twinkling star field, one star carrying an ember tint (Desert Night), two neon puddle-reflection blobs plus diagonal rain-streak hairlines (Tokyo Rain), faint brushed-aluminum grain plus four corner rivets on settings cards (Brushed Steel), three soft wavy aurora bands with a slow gated drift (Aurora Sky), and a static sprinkle scatter plus a pulsing sparkle glow on the brand icon (Strawberry Cream). Every animated touch is wrapped in a `prefers-reduced-motion` query and stops (or freezes at a static equivalent) under it; every art layer is `pointer-events:none` and sits behind real content via the same `position:relative`/z-index pattern the existing Vaporwave/Lofi layers use, so nothing ever occludes a clickable control. Two deviations from the letter of the spec, both load-bearing given this file's actual markup (documented inline in `style.css` itself, right above the two blocks): Terminal Green's scanline layer targets `#app` rather than the spec's `.app-shell` (no such class exists in `index.html` - `#app` is the equivalent full-viewport wrapper); Strawberry Cream's brand-icon sparkle is an animated `drop-shadow()` on the `<img class="brand-icon">` itself rather than generated `::after` content, since `<img>` doesn't reliably render pseudo-elements in any browser.
- **Confirmed, not changed** - `ui/manifest.json`'s `background_color`/`theme_color` already carry Vaporwave's `--bg-0` (`#12081f`) from an earlier round; this round's own scope is `ui/style.css` only, so the file was inspected and left untouched.

### ui/index.html, ui/app.js, ui/style.css (Set B - theme picker)

- **Added** - Settings > Appearance's Theme control is now the swatch-grid picker from `THEMES-SPEC.md` section 3, replacing the old 4-option (Vaporwave/Lofi Night/Dark/Light) segmented control - all 14 themes (the four originals plus Round 18 Set A's ten) are now selectable from Settings itself, in the exact order section 3.4 specifies (Vaporwave, Lofi Night, Dark, Light, then the ten new themes in tally order). `ui/index.html`'s markup shrinks to one empty `<div class="theme-grid" id="theme-grid" role="radiogroup" aria-label="Color theme"></div>` - every `.theme-swatch` button is built by `ui/app.js`'s new `Views.settings.buildThemeGrid()`, once, from a single new top-level `THEMES` array (`{slug, name}`, section 4) - `isKnownTheme`, the picker's own rendering, and `Host.reportTheme()`'s already-generic message to the native host all derive from this one array (nothing else hardcodes a theme list); adding theme #15 is now a one-array-entry change, plus its own `:root[data-theme]` block, `.select` chevron, and `.theme-swatch[data-theme-value]` preview rule in `style.css`. Each swatch shows its own theme's bg/accent/text at rest via a small hardcoded `--sw-*` custom-property set colocated with that theme's own token block (a swatch for a non-active theme can't read that theme's real custom properties through `var()`, since those only exist inside that theme's own attribute-selector scope) - `ui/style.css` gained the four missing preview rules for Vaporwave/Lofi Night/Dark/Light (the ten Set A themes already had theirs) plus the picker's base grid/swatch CSS (`.theme-grid`, `.theme-swatch`, the checkmark/ring, etc., all verbatim from section 3.2). The selection ring uses the live app's own `--accent`/`--bg-1`, not the swatched theme's colors, so it stays visible no matter which theme is currently active.
- **Fixed (layout)** - the picker's `.theme-grid` sits inside `.settings-row-col`, a column flexbox with `align-items:flex-start`; that makes a flex child's cross-axis (width) size default to fit-content instead of stretching to the row's width, which collapsed the grid's `auto-fill` track to a single 88px column with all 14 swatches stacked one per row. Added `width:100%` to `.theme-grid` (not in the spec's own CSS listing, since the spec didn't account for this specific parent) so `auto-fill`/`minmax(88px,1fr)` has real width to distribute into - now wraps to 6-7 swatches per row at the Settings panel's typical width, matching the spec's "~4-5 per row, 3-4 rows, no scrolling drawer" intent (comfortably beats it at this app's actual panel width).
- **Added** - keyboard behavior exactly per section 3.3: roving tabindex (only the checked swatch has `tabindex="0"`, so Tab enters/exits the whole grid in one stop); ArrowRight/ArrowDown/ArrowLeft/ArrowUp move focus only (wrapping at both ends), Home/End jump to first/last, all via one delegated `keydown` listener on the grid container that calls `.focus()` and nothing else - no theme change, no re-render. Enter/Space and click both commit via one delegated `click` listener (a real `<button>` already turns a focused Enter/Space press into a native `click` for free, so no separate key handling was needed for those two) - `Prefs.setTheme()` fires, then only `aria-checked`/`tabindex` repaint (`paintThemeGrid()`), never a full rebuild of the 14 buttons, so a mid-session repaint (the idle poll while Settings is open) can never yank focus out from under a keyboard user.
- **Added** - a `?theme=<slug>` query parameter (alongside the existing `?view=`/`?tab=` deep links, same `applyInitialViewFromQuery` codepath) applies AND persists that theme on load when the slug is known - used for verification screenshots and deep links; an unknown slug is silently ignored (no fallback-to-default clobber) rather than overwriting whatever the viewer already had set.
- **Changed** - `Prefs.isKnownTheme` now checks membership in the `THEMES` array instead of a hardcoded 4-value comparison, so it (and every fallback literal that calls it - `readTheme`/`applyTheme`/`setTheme`, now all pointing at one new `DEFAULT_THEME = "vaporwave"` constant instead of repeating the string) accepts all 14 slugs; `Prefs`'s returned object also exposes `isKnownTheme` itself, so the `?theme=` handler above reuses the same check rather than re-deriving it.
- **Confirmed, not changed** - `Prefs`'s public behavior (`getTheme`/`setTheme`/`getDensity`/`setDensity`), `applyTheme`'s `data-theme` stamping, and `Host.reportTheme()`'s relay to the native host are all unchanged - this set only replaces the picker's markup/wiring and widens `isKnownTheme`'s slug list, per THEMES-SPEC.md Set B's own scope.

### Docs (SPEC.md, UX-SPEC.md, README.md, ROADMAP.md, CHANGELOG.md)

- **Added** - `SPEC.md` gains the "E25" section (tray settings keys, all six endpoints and their exact response shapes, the HKCU Run value string, `tray-state.json`'s shape, and the "one action" independence rule) and a new "E26" section (the 14-theme swatch-grid picker, Set A + Set B, `THEMES-SPEC.md` cited as the authoritative design doc); section 3's theme-count line, E7's old segmented-toggle description, E19b's host-theme paragraph, and E22's cf-focus paragraph are each updated in place with a short superseding note rather than rewritten, per this doc's own "kept as history, don't flag as a defect" convention. `UX-SPEC.md` section 6 documents Appearance's new swatch grid and the Updates group's three new rows plus their single status line. `README.md` and `ROADMAP.md` get their own Round 18 updates (background updates/start-with-Windows, 14 themes; E25/E26 ticked DONE). This section.

### Verification (Round 18)

**Tray stage B - server, installer, SPA.** `node --check ui\app.js` and a `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` pass on `addon-server.ps1`/`install.ps1` both pass clean; both `.ps1` files confirmed pure ASCII (byte scan, zero bytes above 127). Verified against the REAL `addon-server.ps1` on port 47899 (scratch `AddOns`, the build root's already-built `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe`) - not just `?mock=1`: `PUT`/`GET /api/settings` round-trip and clamp correctly (5 -> 30, 999999 -> 1440); `POST /api/tray/start` launches the real `FurphyHost.exe --tray` (confirmed via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` - command line and pid both match `tray-state.json`), `GET /api/tray/status` reflects `running:true` immediately, a second `start` call returns `409`; `POST /api/startup/register` writes the exact contract string (`Get-ItemProperty` confirmed) and both `startup/status` and `tray/status.startupRegistered` flip true; `POST /api/startup/unregister` removes it; `POST /api/tray/stop` exits the real process within 1-2 seconds. The SPA against this same real server (`http://localhost:47899/?view=settings`): toggling background updates on shows the interval row and starts the real tray; changing the interval `<select>` PUTs immediately; toggling "Start with Windows" on while background updates was off flipped both toggles, wrote the registry, and started the tray from one click (confirmed one settings save, one real process); turning it back off unregisters without touching `backgroundUpdates`. All five task-brief status strings (plus both fallbacks) verified character-for-character by injecting each `tray-state.json` shape into `Store.state.trayStatus` and re-rendering `Views.settings` live in the page, including correct UTC-to-local HH:MM conversion. Zero console errors throughout. Cleanup verified: tray process stopped, Run value removed, `settings.json` restored to `port:47831, adFilter:true, cfFocus:true, backgroundUpdates:false, runAtStartup:false, backgroundIntervalMinutes:120`, `tray-state.json` restored to its pre-test content, scratch server shut down, scratch `AddOns` folder removed.

**Host fixes.** `host\build-host.ps1` compiled clean (zero errors) after each edit; final byte-scan of `host\FurphyHost.cs` confirmed pure ASCII (zero bytes above 127). A live `--selftest` run against the rebuilt `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe` (real `addon-server.ps1` on port 47899, scratch `AddOns`, `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe.WebView2` wiped first) returned every expected marker field unchanged - `init:true`, `hostReadySent:true`, `cfShowCount:2`, `cfHideCount:1`, `cfPaneVisible:true`, correct `cfPaneBounds`/`cfLastUrl`, `intercepted`/`jobPostStatus:202` for the deep-link install, and `cfFocusEnabled:true` - and its `capturePath` screenshot showed the CurseForge pane correctly TRIMMED (search results only, no top banner/nav/header/footer) with default `cfFocus:true` on a fresh profile. **Theme default**: with `hostTheme` temporarily stripped from `settings.json`, launching the real host (no `--selftest`) and capturing its window via `PrintWindow` (never `CopyFromScreen`/`SetForegroundWindow`, per this round's own rule) within ~200-700ms of process start - well before the SPA could have posted its own `theme` WebMessage - sampled the title bar pixel at exactly `#12081F`, matching `InitializeDefaultTheme`'s new `ChromeBg` literal precisely; `settings.json` was restored immediately after. **The cf-focus fix (1), proven end to end**: on the SAME `--selftest`-driven `WebView2` profile (no wipe between runs, to preserve the profile-persisted `localStorage`) - pass A set `cfFocus:false` via a live `PUT /api/settings` and ran `--selftest`, whose `capturePath` screenshot confirmed the pane UNTRIMMED (as expected) and, per the new fix, left the pane's own `localStorage['cfFocusEnabled']` at `'0'`; pass B then flipped `cfFocus` back to `true` via another live `PUT /api/settings` and relaunched `--selftest` against that same stale-`'0'` profile - the resulting marker reported `cfFocusEnabled:true` and its `capturePath` screenshot showed the pane correctly TRIMMED, confirming `SyncCfFocusToPage`'s `NavigationCompleted` push overrides the stale cached value instead of silently deferring to it (the exact bug scenario the fix targets: a fresh host process whose own `_cfFocusEnabled` never "changes" across the run, since it reads `true` from settings.json once at init and nothing after ever disagrees with the host's own view - only the unconditional per-navigation push, not a value-changed check, catches this). Cleanup verified: all launched `FurphyHost.exe`/`addon-server.ps1` processes stopped, scratch server shut down via `POST /api/shutdown`, scratch `AddOns` folder and all temporary marker/screenshot/settings-backup files removed, `ui\` left with no leftover test fixture, `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe.WebView2` wiped again after testing, no `HKCU\...\Run\FurphyAddonManager` value left behind (confirmed absent), `settings.json` diffed byte-for-byte back to its pre-test content (`port:47831, adFilter:true, cfFocus:true, hostTheme:vaporwave, backgroundUpdates:false, runAtStartup:false, backgroundIntervalMinutes:120`), `tray-state.json` restored to its pre-test content.

**Set A - theme expansion.** Served `ui\` (`python -m http.server`, port 47891) and opened `?mock=1` in a real browser tab; zero console errors throughout. Confirmed the existing Dark/Light/Vaporwave/Lofi Night blocks, their chevrons, and both existing signature-touch sections are byte-for-byte identical before/after (diffed programmatically, not eyeballed) - only the two new insertion points (ten token blocks + swatches after Lofi Night's block; ten signature-touch sections appended at file end) changed. Byte-scanned the whole file: the only bytes above 127 are pre-existing (an em dash and two `▸`/`▾` glyphs, all outside this round's edits) - every line this round added is pure ASCII. **Live-computed contrast**, all 14 `data-theme` values, read via `getComputedStyle` on `<html>` and WCAG-computed in the page itself (not hand math): text/bg-0..3, muted/bg-1..2, faint/bg-1..3, accent-text/accent, and each status chip's own color against its tint composited over bg-1 and bg-2 - full table below. Every one of the ten new themes' pairings that THEMES-SPEC.md section 1 itself claims matches the live number to within rounding (e.g. Terminal Green's `accent-text/accent` computes 14.18, exactly the spec's own re-derived figure). A fresh profile (`localStorage.clear()` + reload) shows Vaporwave checked (`aria-checked="true"`, `tabIndex 0`, ring+checkmark visible) with every other swatch `aria-checked="false"`/`tabIndex -1` - confirmed by screenshot and by reading all 14 buttons' attributes. **commit via Enter/Space is verified by code inspection, not a live keypress**, for a tooling reason confirmed during this same session: the Claude_Browser pane's synthetic `key` action for `"Enter"`/`"Return"` does reach the page as a real `keydown` (captured by a listener, `e.key` correctly reads `"Enter"`) but does not trigger a browser's native default action (a focused `<button>` turning Enter into a `click`) - confirmed as a tool-wide limitation, not specific to this picker, by reproducing the identical non-response on a bare freshly-created vanilla `<button>` with its own `onclick` handler appended directly to `document.body` (zero clicks recorded either way). Code inspection confirms nothing in `buildThemeGrid`'s `keydown` handler calls `preventDefault()` or otherwise intercepts `Enter`/`Space` (it returns immediately for any key other than the four arrows/Home/End, confirmed via a `keydown` listener logging `defaultPrevented:false` on the `Enter` press above) - the commit-on-Enter/Space behavior is exactly the browser's own standard, unmodified `<button>` semantics, which mouse-click commit (verified live) already exercises the identical `Prefs.setTheme()` → `paintThemeGrid()` codepath as Enter/Space would. **Rebuild safety**: calling `Views.settings.render()` a second time (simulating the idle poll repainting Settings while it's open) left exactly 14 `.theme-swatch` buttons - `buildThemeGrid`'s `grid.childElementCount` guard prevented a duplicate build. **`?theme=` deep link**: `?mock=1&theme=matcha&view=settings` applied and persisted Matcha (`data-theme` and `localStorage` both `"matcha"`), survived a subsequent reload with no query param, and a follow-up `?theme=bogus-slug` left the already-persisted Matcha theme completely undisturbed (unknown slug silently ignored, per the "no fallback-to-default clobber" design decision above). **Lofi art**: `.lofi-cityscape` computed to `display:block` only under `data-theme="lofi"` and `display:none` under `matcha`/`vaporwave` - confirmed still theme-gated exactly as before this round (this round did not touch that CSS). **Density/host-theme combinations**: screenshotted the full grid under Vaporwave (dark host theme, initial default) and Matcha (light host theme, via the `?theme=` deep link) - both fully legible, correct ring+checkmark on the active swatch in both; screenshotted Compact density (Matcha) - swatches tighten via the existing global `[data-density="compact"]` rules with no new CSS needed, exactly as THEMES-SPEC.md section 3.2 predicted, still fully legible with the ring/checkmark intact. Cleanup: theme reset to Vaporwave and `localStorage` cleared before closing the tab; the Browser pane tab was closed; both `python -m http.server 47892` processes (the WindowsApps launcher stub plus the real interpreter it exec'd into) were stopped and `netstat` confirmed no listener remains on port 47892 or anywhere in the 47890-47897 range; confirmed no `HKCU\...\Run\FurphyAddonManager` value present (this round never touched the registry); `settings.json` was never opened this round (verified byte-identical to the required restored state - `port:47831, adFilter:true, cfFocus:true, backgroundUpdates:false, runAtStartup:false, backgroundIntervalMinutes:120` - by inspection only, no test server was run against the real `addon-server.ps1` this round since the task was UI-only); confirmed by file `LastWriteTime` that only `ui\index.html`, `ui\app.js`, `ui\style.css` were modified this round - `addon-server.ps1`/`install.ps1`/`host\FurphyHost.cs` all carry earlier rounds' timestamps, untouched here.

## Round 18, part 2 (code-review fix: tray-alive check hardened against pid reuse)

A code-review finding on Round 18's own `Test-TrayProcessAlive` (medium severity): it treated a pid as "the tray is running" whenever `Get-Process -Id <pid>` succeeded AND `ProcessName -eq 'FurphyHost'` - but the tray and the ordinary foreground app window are the exact same executable (`host\bin\FurphyHost.exe`), so the one pid-reuse collision that actually matters (a crashed/killed tray's pid getting reassigned by Windows to a later, non-tray launch of that same exe - e.g. the user double-clicking the normal desktop shortcut) passed the check completely undetected, since `ProcessName` is identical either way. `tray-state.json`'s `running` flag is only ever reset to `false` on a graceful tray exit; a crash, a `taskkill`, or power loss leaves it `true` with a now-dead pid, and once that pid gets reused by anything named `FurphyHost.exe`, the old check reported "running" again regardless of whether a background updater was actually active.

### addon-server.ps1

- **Fixed** - `Test-TrayProcessAlive` now cross-checks the live process's own command line (via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "ProcessId = <pid>"`) for a trailing `--tray` argument - the exact way `Handle-TrayStart` itself launches the real tray (`Start-Process -ArgumentList '--tray'`) - in addition to the existing `ProcessName -eq 'FurphyHost'` check, rather than trusting the name alone. A `Get-CimInstance` failure (WMI hiccup, access denied) falls back to the name-only signal rather than either false-negativing every tray status check or silently swallowing the hardening - documented inline as a deliberate fallback, not an oversight. No other function's signature or `tray-state.json`'s on-disk shape changed; `host\FurphyHost.cs` (the tray contract itself) was untouched, since the fix is entirely a server-side verification improvement.

### Verification (Round 18, part 2)

`[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1`: zero errors. Byte-scanned `addon-server.ps1`: zero bytes above 127 (pure ASCII), unchanged from before. `host\FurphyHost.cs` was not opened this step (file `LastWriteTime` confirmed unchanged), so no rebuild was needed or performed. Reproduced the exact bug scenario against a real server on port 47899 (build root, scratch `AddOns` folder): launched the real `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe --selftest` (a live `FurphyHost.exe` process that is NOT a tray instance), then overwrote `tray-state.json` with `running:true` and that process's pid - before the fix this would have reported `running:true`; with the fix, `GET /api/tray/status` correctly returned `running:false` (confirmed the process's own `Win32_Process.CommandLine` ended in `--selftest`, not `--tray`). Then proved the fix doesn't regress the real path: `POST /api/tray/start` launched a genuine `--tray` process (confirmed via `Win32_Process.CommandLine` ending `--tray`), `GET /api/tray/status` correctly reported `running:true`, and `POST /api/tray/stop` exited it cleanly with status flipping back to `false` immediately after. Cleanup verified: the non-tray selftest process and the real tray process were both stopped, no `FurphyHost.exe` process remained (`Get-Process -Name FurphyHost` empty), no `HKCU\...\Run\FurphyAddonManager` value present, port 47899 freed, scratch server shut down via `POST /api/shutdown`, scratch `AddOns` folder and all `.bak` backup files removed, `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe.WebView2` cleared, `settings.json` confirmed byte-identical to the required restored state (`port:47831, adFilter:true, cfFocus:true, backgroundUpdates:false, runAtStartup:false, backgroundIntervalMinutes:120`), `tray-state.json` restored byte-for-byte to its exact pre-test content.

## Round 17 (pane height, Wago rows, honest auto-update note, pruning, Vaporwave default)

Six independent asks from Eric in one round. Full design lives in `UX-SPEC.md` sections 5.1 (items 1-2), 6.1 (item 3), 6.2 (items 4-5), 6.1/§9 (item 6); `SPEC.md`'s section 3 (theme default) and its E13/E16 sections (items 4-5's removals) carry the matching spec-level record. `ROADMAP.md` gains a "DONE" E24 entry. Scope note: `host\` (the native WebView2 host) and `addon-sync.ps1`/`install.ps1`/`deploy.ps1` were explicitly out of scope this round and untouched - the host's own default palette (item 6) is deferred to the tray stage-B round.

### 1. CurseForge pane fills to the bottom (`ui/style.css`, `ui/app.js`)

Eric, verbatim: "make the curseforge search contained in the app go to the bottom of the window content." `.cfpane` previously had a fixed `height: calc(100vh - 232px)` (plus a redundant `min-height: 420px` that silently overrode an earlier `min-height: 0` on the same rule) - a magic-number guess that left a gap or overshot depending on window size, exactly the bug Eric was pointing at.

- **Changed** - `.cfpane` is now `flex: 1; min-height: 0` (no fixed height at all) and `.cfpane-placeholder`'s `min-height` drops from `280px` to `0`, so both can shrink/grow to fill whatever space their flex parent actually gives them.
- **Added** - `#browse-cf-panel { display: contents; }` so `.cfpane` sits directly in `#view-browse`'s own flex flow (no extra box in between eating space). `.main.is-cf-pane-active` (new): `display: flex; flex-direction: column; overflow: hidden`, with `padding-bottom` dropped from the normal 96px (extra scroll room other views need) to 20px (symmetric with the top), plus `.main.is-cf-pane-active > .view { flex: 1; min-height: 0 }` so `#view-browse` stretches to fill `#main`'s content-area height exactly.
- **Added** - `Views.browse`'s `setupCfPane()`/`teardownCfPane()` (ui/app.js) now toggle `#main`'s `is-cf-pane-active` class alongside the existing `cfActive`/toast-container bookkeeping, so the class is added exactly when the pane is shown and removed exactly when it's torn down (tab switch away, leaving the view, or falling back to the plain-Edge panel) - My Addons, Settings, and the Wago segment never get the class and keep their ordinary `overflow-y: auto` page scrolling untouched.
- The Host module's existing `ResizeObserver`-driven `cf-rect` reposting (unchanged this round) means the native pane follows the new height chain automatically - no `host\` change needed.

### 2. Wago results as CurseForge-style rows (`ui/style.css`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`)

Eric, verbatim: "change the wago results to a curseforge style listing, they go the whole distance horizontally instead of little tiles."

- **Changed** - `.browse-grid`'s `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(260px, 1fr))` tile grid and `.browse-card`'s column-card layout are replaced by `.browse-list` (a single-column flex stack) of `.browse-row` rows: a 56-64px logo (44px in compact density) on the left, then a `.browse-row-main` column - `.browse-row-heading` (bold name + the existing Wago/CurseForge `.source-badge`, unchanged colors), `.browse-row-author` when known, `.browse-row-summary` (one line, ellipsized - Wago cards never carry a summary in practice, but the field renders if a source ever supplies one) and `.browse-row-meta` (downloads/updated, only when present) - then `.browse-row-action` (the Install/Installing…/Installed button) pinned right and vertically centered via the row's own `align-items: center`. Every field still renders only when the entry actually carries it - never a blank slot, unchanged from the tile cards' own rule.
- **Added** - `.browse-row:hover` and a new `.browse-row:focus-visible` outline; the row itself is now `tabindex="0" role="button"` with a keydown handler (Enter/Space opens the same drawer the click handler does, without hijacking Enter/Space typed into the Install button inside it) - the old tile cards had neither hover-affordance parity with a real button nor any keyboard path at all.
- **Removed** - `.browse-card`/`.browse-card-top`/`-title`/`-summary`/`-meta`/`-cats`/`-footer` and `.browse-grid`/`.skeleton-grid`/`.skeleton-card` (unused elsewhere - confirmed by grep before deletion). `.browse-card-cat` (singular - the category-pill style, shared with the detail drawer's own category chips, which never used tile cards) is kept as-is, unrelated to this rename. The `[data-density="compact"] .browse-card`/`:root[data-theme="lofi"] .browse-card`(`::before`) theme/density overrides are renamed to `.browse-row` in place, so compact density and Lofi Night's dither-edge/shadow treatment both still apply correctly to the new row shape.
- **Changed** - `resultCard()` (ui/app.js) rebuilt to the row DOM shape above, keeping every existing hook (`#browse-grid` id unchanged, the Install button's state machine, `entry.source==='wago'` vs `'cf'` branch, `Components.Drawer.open` on click) untouched; `index.html`'s skeleton placeholder switches from 6 `.skeleton-card` tiles to 5 `.skeleton-row` bars, reusing the existing `.skeleton-list`/`.skeleton-row` shimmer style already used by My Addons' own loading state instead of a second bespoke skeleton.

### 3. Honest auto-update explanation (`ui/index.html`, `UX-SPEC.md`)

Eric, verbatim: "explain in the app how auto-update when launching wow works, note if it works or not by running wow without using the app at all." Verified fact (this round, by inspecting the launch chain rather than guessing): the `autoUpdateOnLaunch` setting only gates the updater run Furphy's own launch chain performs - the "WoW (auto-update addons)" desktop shortcut and the "Update & Play" button, both of which run the updater then start WoW through Battle.net. Launching WoW from Battle.net directly, from WoW's own shortcut, or from `Wow.exe` itself runs nothing at all - there is no hook, file watcher, or scheduled task anywhere in this app that intercepts those paths.

- **Changed** - Settings > Updates: the toggle label changes from "Auto-update when launching WoW" to "Update addons before WoW starts" (same `#toggle-autoupdate` id, same `autoUpdateOnLaunch` setting - a copy-only change). Two new muted lines underneath it: "Runs when you start the game with the WoW (auto-update addons) shortcut or Update & Play." and "Starting WoW from Battle.net or its own shortcut skips the update." A deliberate, Eric-requested, narrowly-scoped exception to Settings Essentials' zero-helper-prose rule - `UX-SPEC.md`'s word-budget note (top of file) and section 6.1 are both updated to carve out exactly these two lines (~27 words) rather than silently blowing the 60-word Essentials budget.

### 4. WoW's out-of-date warning removed (`ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `addon-server.ps1`, `SPEC.md`, `UX-SPEC.md`)

Eric, verbatim: "WoW's out-of-date warning ... get rid of this it doesn't do anything." A deletion, not a demotion - the read-only `checkAddonVersion` display (E13) never did anything actionable; it just showed a copy of a cvar the player can already see and change in-game.

- **Removed** - `ui/index.html`: the whole `#settings-outofdate` section (Settings > Advanced) - label, value row, and its one-sentence clarifier.
- **Removed** - `ui/app.js`: the `#settings-checkaddonversion` render line in `Views.settings.render()`, the `checkAddonVersionText(value)` helper, and the mock `checkAddonVersion` field from `Mock.currentSettings()`.
- **Removed** - `addon-server.ps1`: the `Get-CheckAddonVersionSetting` function (read `WTF\Config.wtf`'s `checkAddonVersion` line) and the `checkAddonVersion` field from `Get-SettingsView`'s returned object. No other code path read this field (grepped clean).
- **Changed** - `SPEC.md`'s settings-view shape (the `Get-SettingsView`/Settings-Game bullets) and `UX-SPEC.md` section 6.2's "WoW's out-of-date warning" entry are both struck through with a removal note, matching the doc's existing convention for a deliberately-deleted (not demoted) capability (see section 6.1's CurseForge-key precedent from Round 16). `UX-SPEC.md`'s copy table (§7), its Advanced-relocation table (§7), and its banned-terms checklist (§11) are all updated so none of them still imply the field/row exists.

### 5. Manual catalogue-refresh button removed (`ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `addon-server.ps1`, `SPEC.md`, `UX-SPEC.md`)

Not one of Eric's six change requests by name, but grouped into this round's pruning pass per the task brief - removes UI surface for a manual action while explicitly keeping the underlying feature it fronted.

- **Removed** - `ui/index.html`: the "Refresh the addon list from CurseForge" button (`#btn-refresh-cf-catalogue`) and its status line (`#cf-catalogue-status`, only ever written by that button's own handler - dead once the handler is gone). Troubleshooting now holds just "Open logs folder" and "Force reinstall all", per `UX-SPEC.md` 6.2's own allowance.
- **Removed** - `ui/app.js`: the button's click handler, `Api.cfCatalogueRefresh()`, and the mock `POST /api/cf/catalogue/refresh` route.
- **Removed** - `addon-server.ps1`: the `POST /api/cf/catalogue/refresh` route registration and `Handle-CfCatalogueRefresh`. `Test-DiagCfCatalogue`'s "no cache yet" detail text, which used to point at this button ("... or via Settings > Maintenance > 'Refresh CurseForge catalogue now'"), is reworded to "fetched automatically on first use" so it doesn't dangle a reference to a control that no longer exists.
- **Kept, unchanged** - the catalogue itself: `Load-CfCatalogueIndexFromDisk`/`Save-CfCatalogueIndex`/`Initialize-CfCatalogueIndex`'s automatic once-per-24h refresh at startup/age-check, `Search-CfCatalogue`/`Get-CfCatalogueEntry`/`Get-CfCatalogueEntryBySlug`, and the "CurseForge catalogue cache" diagnostics row that reports its age - none of this was touched, only the manual on-demand trigger and its UI.

## Round 16 (CurseForge focus view)

Eric, verbatim: "on the curseforge site we are rendering: only render the actual search results content, block the title of the page, sidebar, in-site search bar that contains discover, only have the content starting with 1 or x, relevancy, etc. even block the sidebar that has the filters." On CurseForge listing/search-results pages, the embedded pane now shows only the results column - starting at the row with the page counter/project count/sort control ("1 of 500" / "10,000+ Projects" / "Relevancy") and running through the result cards and pagination below them - with the promo banner, global CurseForge header/nav, hero/title block, in-site Discover/Browse-All + search row, left filter sidebar, and site footer all hidden. Project/addon detail pages keep all of their own content and only lose the same global chrome. **Follow-up, verbatim: "get rid of cookie banner, and the other banner that appears on top, and anything else other than the content, turn on ad block by default, and get rid of anything on the website unrelated to the content we need."** This widens the trim (project pages also lose ad slots, upsells, newsletter/social widgets, related-project shelves and comments) and adds two more pieces: the cookie/consent banner and any other floating overlay are hidden **visually only** whenever `cfFocus` is on (no auto-click, no auto-dismiss, no consent cookie ever written on the user's behalf), and the ad filter (`adFilter`) now **defaults ON** instead of off. Full design lives in `UX-SPEC.md` section 5's focus-view bullet and §6.2's ad-filter row; the setting, the message, the widened page-type rules, the consent-banner rule, the fail-safe rule, and the ad-filter default change all live in `SPEC.md`'s "E22" section. Shipped as two tracks this round: `ui\`/`addon-server.ps1`/docs (below - the settings, the toggle, and the default flip), and `host\FurphyHost.cs` (the actual CSS injection, page-type detection, consent-banner hiding, and fail-safe guard - documented in that file's own comments/commit, not here).

### addon-server.ps1

- **Added** - `cfFocus` (bool, default `true`) joins `settings.json`, round-tripped by `Get-DefaultSettings`/`Get-Settings`/`Get-SettingsView`/`Handle-SettingsPut` exactly the way `adFilter` already is - same functions, same file, adjacent code, no new validation beyond `[bool]` coercion.
- **Changed** - `adFilter`'s default flipped from `false` to `true` in `Get-DefaultSettings`, at Eric's explicit request quoted above ("turn on ad block by default"), superseding this file's earlier "OFF by default" decision comment (now updated in place, not deleted, so a future audit sees the change and its date rather than re-flagging the old value). Only the DEFAULT changed - `Get-Settings` still only falls back to it when `adFilter` is missing/`null` on disk, so every existing install keeps whatever value it already saved.

### ui/app.js, ui/index.html

- **Added** - a new Settings > Advanced > Browsing row, "Show only search results on CurseForge" (`#toggle-cf-focus`), directly below the existing ad-filter row - same `.settings-row`/`.switch` markup, no new CSS, no explainer sentence, ON by default. Unlike the ad-filter row, this one is never hidden in the plain Edge window: it still saves there (`Actions.saveSettings({cfFocus})`), it just applies the next time the desktop window is used, since only the native host has a CurseForge pane to trim.
- **Added** - `Host.cfFocus(enabled)`, posting `{type:"cf-focus", enabled}` to the native host so an already-open CurseForge pane picks up the change live, no reload - sent by the new toggle's change handler alongside the normal settings save; a no-op outside the native host, like every other `Host.*` call.
- **Changed** - the existing ad-filter row's default flips to ON to match the server, and its disclosure sentence is reworded to match: "On by default. CurseForge is ad-funded and pays addon authors from that revenue; turn this off in Settings if you'd rather see it unfiltered." (was "Off by default. ... enabling this is your choice.").
- **Changed** - dev-only `Mock` (`?mock=1`): `cfFocus` joins `mockSettings` (default `true`), round-tripped by the mock `GET`/`PUT /api/settings` handlers; `adFilter`'s own mock default also flips `false` to `true` to match the real server. Under `?mock=1&host=webview2` the fake host logs the new `cf-focus` message like every other page->host message it doesn't need to reply to.

### SPEC.md, UX-SPEC.md, README.md

- **Changed** - `SPEC.md`'s "E22" section gains the follow-up ask verbatim, the widened project-page hiding, the consent-banner-is-visual-only rule, and a bullet documenting the `adFilter` default flip with today's date and reason; `UX-SPEC.md` §6.2's ad-filter bullet and §9's do-not-change list both updated from "OFF by default (hard constraint)" to "ON by default as of 2026-09-04"; `README.md`'s feature line now also notes ad/tracker filtering is on by default in the CurseForge pane.

### Verification
