SPA+server half only (this round's own scope) - verified in the Browser pane against a local static file server serving `ui\` directly (no real server, no real host). `?mock=1`: the new row appeared in Settings > Advanced > Browsing right below the ad-filter row (now also defaulting ON, with its reworded "On by default..." text), `cfFocus` defaulting ON; toggling either off produced a "Settings saved." toast, and reloading confirmed both persisted. `?mock=1&host=webview2`: both rows visible, both ON by default; toggling cf-focus off produced the save AND a logged `[mock-host] page->host {type: cf-focus, enabled: false}`, zero console errors. `node --check ui/app.js` and a `PSParser` tokenize of `addon-server.ps1` both pass clean. Not verified this round (native host's own scope, not this task's): the actual CSS trim, page-type detection, consent-banner hiding, or fail-safe guard against a real CurseForge page - see `host\FurphyHost.cs`'s own commit for that half's verification.

## Round 16, part 2 (CurseForge API key removed entirely)

Eric, verbatim: "and i want the curse api key cleared, i want that removed as a feature." A deliberate deletion, not a demotion (UX-SPEC.md section 1's "demote, don't delete" rule explicitly does not apply here) - coherent because nothing in this app has needed a key for a while: installs/updates never did, search moved to Wago + the embedded CurseForge website (Round 15), and drawer metadata already came from Round 8's keyless enrichment chain (Wago match / addon-radar / catalogue-only). Full contract, the settings.json migration, and the acceptance bar live in `SPEC.md`'s new "E23" section.

### addon-server.ps1

- **Removed** - `cfApiKey` from `Get-DefaultSettings`/`Get-Settings`/`Get-SettingsView`/`Handle-SettingsPut` entirely - no field, no `hasApiKey`/`apiKeyHint` masking, nothing to PUT.
- **Removed** - `Invoke-CfApi` (the key-gated CurseForge Core API proxy and its `$Script:CfCache`), `Send-CfApiResult`, `Test-CfApiKey`, `Handle-SettingsTestKey` (`POST /api/settings/test-key`), and every key-gated `/api/cf/*` handler and route (`search`, `categories`, `mods` GET/POST, `description`, `files`, `changelog`, `resolve`). `Handle-CfEnrich` (Round 8/E16) loses its official-API-first branch and now always uses the keyless fallback chain it already had. `Handle-Diagnostics` loses its "CurseForge API key" check row. The only `/api/cf/*` routes left are the keyless `enrich`, `browse`, and `catalogue/refresh`.
- **Added** - a one-time migration in `Get-Settings`: an existing `settings.json` still carrying a `cfApiKey` property (any value, including empty) has it dropped and the file rewritten - same atomic write every other settings save uses - with one log line recording that it happened. The key's value itself is never logged.

### addon-sync.ps1, install.ps1, deploy.ps1

- **Removed** - `cfApiKey` from `addon-sync.ps1`'s settings defaults/parsing (4 references, never used for anything else in the CLI). `install.ps1` and `deploy.ps1`'s default `settings.json` literals no longer include `"cfApiKey": ""`.

### ui/app.js, ui/index.html, ui/style.css

- **Removed** - the whole Settings > "CurseForge key (optional)" section from `index.html` (the collapsed row, password field, Show/Hide, Test, Save, Clear, status/hint/test-result lines) and every handler for it in `app.js`, plus its two now-unused icon sprites (`#icon-eye`/`#icon-eye-off`). `Api.testKey`/`cfSearch`/`cfCategories`/`cfMod`/`cfMods`/`cfDescription`/`cfFiles`/`cfChangelog`/`cfResolve` and `Actions.testKey` are gone; `Api.cfEnrich`/`cfBrowse`/`cfCatalogueRefresh` (keyless) are the only CurseForge calls left.
- **Removed** - the drawer's keyed `'curseforge'` source and its `d.mod`/`loadMod()` path, and the `LogoCache` module it fed (plus `Store.cacheMods`/`getCachedMod`) - all dead now that `hasApiKey` can never be true. The drawer's CurseForge source is now always `'cf-keyless'` (Round 8's enrichment path, unchanged); `renderHeader`/`renderOverview`/`renderChangelog`/`renderScreenshots` each collapse to that one branch.
- **Changed** - every "Add a free API key..." nudge (Overview/Changelog/Screenshots) becomes plain text - "No description available." / "No changelog available." - plus the existing "Open on CurseForge" header link; never a nudge to add a key. The Add-addon dialog drops its "paste a curseforge.com page URL" option (its only backing endpoint, `/api/cf/resolve`, is gone with no keyless replacement) - a numeric CurseForge ID or a wago.io link still work, and Install-from-the-embedded-pane (Round 15) is the better path for a CurseForge URL anyway.
- **Changed** - dev-only `Mock` (`?mock=1`): no `cfApiKey`/`hasApiKey` state, no `test-key` route, no keyed `/api/cf/*` fixtures (`browsePool`/`fakeMod`/the mock category list, all now-unused, removed too) - `mockCfEnrich`/`cfCatalogueMock`/`/api/cf/browse`/`/api/cf/catalogue/refresh` are the only CurseForge fixtures left.

### SPEC.md, UX-SPEC.md, README.md, ROADMAP.md

- **Changed** - `SPEC.md` loses `cfApiKey` from its settings-shape example and API list, and gains a new "E23" section recording the removal as a dated decision plus the full contract of what was deleted and how the migration works. `UX-SPEC.md` section 6.1 loses the "CurseForge key (optional)" Essentials group (struck through, kept only as a historical note so nothing reinvents it by accident); section 1's "demote, don't delete" principle and section 9's do-not-change list both get a line noting this is the deliberate exception. `README.md`'s "No API key needed"/"The optional CurseForge API key" sections collapse into one short "No accounts, no API keys" statement; its privacy section drops `api.curseforge.com` from the list of hosts this app ever talks to. `ROADMAP.md` gains a "DONE" E23 entry and marks the old deferred E9 idea (official-API fingerprint adoption) obsolete, since the API it depended on no longer exists in this app.

### Verification

Verified via `grep -rni "cfApiKey|hasApiKey|apiKeyHint|test-key|x-api-key|api\.curseforge\.com|Invoke-CfApi"` across every touched file - the only survivors are the migration code itself, this changelog/SPEC's own prose describing the removal, and one smoke-test print line in `deploy.ps1` (now reporting `adFilter`/`cfFocus` instead of the retired `hasApiKey`); zero hits in `ui\app.js`/`ui\index.html`/`ui\style.css`/`addon-sync.ps1`/`install.ps1`. `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean. `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1`/`addon-sync.ps1`/`install.ps1`/`deploy.ps1` all return zero errors, and `host\build-host.ps1` still compiles clean (host\ files untouched by this half). Verified in the Browser pane under `?mock=1` and `?mock=1&host=webview2`: Settings has no CurseForge key row anywhere; a catalogue-only/no-match drawer shows plain "No description available."/"No changelog available." text plus a working CurseForge link, never a key nudge; a wago-match/addon-radar drawer renders exactly as it did before this change (Round 8's enrichment paths untouched); the Add-addon dialog's free-text field rejects a curseforge.com URL with a plain "type the ID number" message; zero console errors throughout.

## Round 15 (Get new addons: Wago search | CurseForge site, host tab strip removed)

Eric, verbatim: "i dont like the weird tab thing on the left with furphy and curseforge - get rid of that. rename browse to get new addons. at the top of get new addons screen, let user switch between wago in app search, and curseforge, which browses the site contained in the area that the wago search was in, one unified experience." Full design lives in `UX-SPEC.md` section 5 (rewritten this round); the additive page<->host contract lives in `SPEC.md`'s new "E21" section (the task that commissioned this round called it "E20," but that number was already taken by round 14's own entry - see E21's own numbering note). Shipped as two tracks, both complete this round: `ui\`/docs (below), and `host\FurphyHost.cs`/`host\selftest.html`'s tab-strip removal plus the native side of the new messages (also below) - the embedded pane works end to end.

### ui/app.js, ui/index.html, ui/style.css

- **Changed** - "Browse" renamed "Get new addons" in every visible string (nav item, empty-state button, the first-run welcome dialog's "See Get new addons" link) - internal identifiers (`Views.browse`, `Store.state.view`'s `"browse"` value) are unchanged, and `?view=browse` keeps working as an alias of the new `?view=get-new-addons` (`?tab=wago|curseforge` alongside either one picks the initial segment).
- **Added** - a segmented `[Wago | CurseForge]` switch at the top of the screen (`localStorage`-backed, default Wago), replacing round 14's single merged CurseForge+Wago search box. The Wago segment is the pre-existing in-app search, unchanged in mechanics but Wago-only now - the CurseForge half of the old merged grid is gone (its `/api/cf/*` endpoints are untouched server-side and keep backing add-by-link resolution and the drawer's keyless enrichment, just aren't queried for in-app search here any more). The old keyless-CurseForge nudge line is deleted outright (Wago search never needed a key). The Wago panel's fallback line is now "Not on Wago? Try CurseForge" (switches segments in the native host; opens the existing chromeless side window directly everywhere else).
- **Added** - the CurseForge segment: inside the native host with the new `cf-pane` capability, an HTML toolbar (Back/Forward/Reload/Home/Search, muted page title) drawn above a placeholder the host positions its `WebView2` overlay over; everywhere else (a plain Edge-window install, or an older host without `cf-pane`), a compact fallback panel ("CurseForge browsing happens inside the Furphy desktop window." + "Open CurseForge in a window" + the paste-a-link fallback).
- **Added** - `Host.init()`/`hasCfPane()`/`getCfState()`/`onCfState`/`onCfJob`/`onHostReady`/`cfShow`/`cfRect`/`cfHide`/`cfNav` - the page's half of the new page<->host contract (SPEC.md E21): `cf-show`/`cf-rect`/`cf-hide`/`cf-nav` sent to the host; `host-ready`/`cf-state`/`cf-job` received back. The placeholder's rect is measured via `getBoundingClientRect()` and posted rAF-throttled off a `ResizeObserver` on it plus window resize/scroll listeners. A new `OverlayTracker` (any dialog/drawer/kebab-popover/lightbox opening/closing) posts `cf-hide`/re-`cf-show` so the pane - which always paints above ordinary HTML - never covers a popover; toasts move over the sidebar (bottom-left) while the pane is showing, for the same reason. The job progress panel renders **inline** between the toolbar and the site while this segment is active (the same `#job-panel` DOM node, relocated - see `#job-panel-anchor`), instead of floating.
- **Changed** - `Actions.openOnCurseForge`/`searchCurseForgeWebsite` now switch to Get new addons' CurseForge segment and navigate the pane directly when `Host.hasCfPane()`; unchanged (existing `'curseforge'`/`'cf-window'` `/api/open` targets) everywhere else. `Actions.searchDependency` (drawer missing-dependency search) now lands on the Wago segment specifically; its button label changed from "Search CurseForge" to "Search for it," since it no longer searches CurseForge directly.
- **Removed** - the old `#browse-keyrejected` gate that used to block the whole screen on a rejected CurseForge key - no longer relevant now that neither segment's default path needs a key (Wago never did; CurseForge is now the real website).
- **Changed** - Dev-only `Mock` (`?mock=1&host=webview2`): stands up a fake `window.chrome.webview` so the whole host contract is exercisable with zero real host process - every page->host message logged to the console, a fake host replying `host-ready {capabilities:["cf-pane"]}` and plausible `cf-state` updates (title derived from the URL, a tiny in-memory back/forward history) to `cf-show`/`cf-nav`. `#cf-placeholder` always shows a dashed-outline "CurseForge site renders here in the desktop window" label (inert in production - the real host's overlay physically covers it).

### host/FurphyHost.cs, host/selftest.html

- **Removed** - the left `_navPanel` Furphy/CurseForge WinForms tab strip (`HostTab` enum, `ActivateTab`, nav-button building/painting/click handlers) and the WinForms CurseForge toolbar (back/forward/home/search buttons and handlers) entirely - the native window is now the SPA only, edge to edge. `RecolorChrome` now only repaints the form background, the single content panel, and both `WebView2` `DefaultBackgroundColor`s.
- **Added** - the CurseForge `WebView2` is now a plain, initially-hidden child of the same content panel the Furphy `WebView2` docks into, positioned entirely by the page: it handles `cf-show`/`cf-rect`/`cf-hide`/`cf-nav` from the page (rect/dpr -> `Bounds = Rectangle(round(x*dpr), round(y*dpr), round(w*dpr), round(h*dpr))` relative to the content panel, `BringToFront` on show, allow-listed to `https://www.curseforge.com/` for any navigation) and sends `host-ready {version, capabilities:["cf-pane"]}` (once on init, again on the page's `hello`) plus `cf-state`/`cf-job` back via `PostWebMessageAsJson` on the Furphy webview. `{type:"open-curseforge", url}` is kept accepted for compatibility (navigate + `cf-state` only, no show/hide). New `--view`/`--tab` CLI args are appended to the SPA URL outside `--selftest` mode. Ad filter, `curseforge://`/`/install/<fileId>` interception, `NewWindowRequested`, `DefaultBackgroundColor`, DWM title-bar theming, and `hostWindow`/`hostTheme` persistence are all unchanged.
- **Changed** - `host\selftest.html` now loads as the Furphy webview's own page during `--selftest` (contract E messages are only ever honored from that webview) and drives hello -> theme -> `cf-show` (rect `{300,120,800,600}`, dpr 1, curseforge.com/wow/addons) -> after 2s, `cf-nav back` (no-op) + `cf-hide` -> `cf-show` again. The `--selftest` marker drops `tabBarPixel`/`toolbarPixel`/`cfTabTitle`/`cfTabActiveAfterMessage` and gains `hostReadySent`, `cfShowCount`, `cfHideCount`, `cfPaneVisible`, `cfPaneBounds` (device px), `cfStateMessages`, `cfLastUrl`, and `capturePath` - a screenshot of the host's own on-screen window (`Graphics.CopyFromScreen`), saved as a PNG next to the marker about 1s before it's written. Every other marker field (`init`/`version`/`dpi`/`dpiAware`/`themeMessages`/`themeBg0`/`captionHresult`/`darkModeHresult`/`openCurseforgeUrl`/`intercepted`/`jobPostStatus`/`blockedRequests`/`allowedRequests`) is unchanged.
- **Fixed** - the selftest screenshot capture (`CaptureSelftestScreenshot`) now forces the host window to the foreground (`TopMost`/`Activate`/`BringToFront`, reverting `TopMost` after) immediately before `Graphics.CopyFromScreen`; without this, the capture grabbed whatever unrelated window happened to be on top of that screen rectangle instead of the host itself, which a review pass caught by running `--selftest` live and inspecting the resulting PNG.

### Verification

Live-verified in the Browser pane against a local static file server (no real server, no real host) at `?mock=1` and `?mock=1&host=webview2`: `document.body.innerText.match(/Browse/g)` returned `null` on My Addons/Get new addons/Settings; the segmented switch defaulted to Wago (persisted across a reload) and showed 4 Wago-only results with no CurseForge cards; switching to CurseForge under `?host=webview2` produced the exact expected `cf-show`/`cf-state` sequence, a working toolbar (search -> `cf-nav go` -> title/back-state update; Back -> `cf-nav back` -> state reverted), and the dashed placeholder; opening a dialog while the pane was active produced `cf-hide` + the toast container's `is-cf-pane` class, closing it re-posted `cf-show` with no `url`; a mock job started while on the CurseForge segment rendered inline between the toolbar and the placeholder (screenshotted) with `cf-rect` updates following its height changes, then returned to floating on completion; `?mock=1` alone (no `host=webview2`) showed the correct Edge-window fallback panel; the drawer's "Search for it" button landed on the Wago segment with the dependency name pre-filled; Lofi Night/Light/Vaporwave all spot-checked for contrast on the new segmented switch, toolbar, and fallback panel. `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean throughout.

The native `host\FurphyHost.cs` half was also verified live this round: `host\build-host.ps1` compiled clean, and a live `--selftest` run (after the foreground-capture fix above) returned `hostReadySent:true`, `cfShowCount:2`, `cfHideCount:1`, `cfPaneVisible:true`, `cfPaneBounds:{x:300,y:120,w:800,h:600}`, `cfStateMessages:9`, the expected `cfLastUrl`, and a `capturePath` PNG that actually shows the host window with the CF pane visible - confirming `host-ready`/`cf-show`/`cf-rect`/`cf-hide`/`cf-nav`/`cf-state`/`cf-job` all behave per the SPEC.md E21 contract end to end. Not run this round: a live `addon-server.ps1` instance on port 47899 (add-by-link/enrichment `/api/cf/*` calls were exercised only through Mock).

## Round 14 (UX pass: clean experience)

Five judged redesign proposals were scored against the app as it stood after Round 13; every judge flagged the same problems - freshness stated three different ways in three places, Status and Compat as two separate pills per row, a raw scrolling log as the only view of an update in progress, a detail-drawer header that could show a raw "Project N" placeholder over an addon whose real name was already known locally, Browse's pick-a-source-then-search flow, and a flat 10-card Settings page with no priority order. `UX-SPEC.md` (repo root) is the authoritative synthesis of the winning proposal plus the best ideas grafted in from the others, and is the source of truth for every visible string, layout and removal decision below - this entry is a summary; see it for the full spec. Note: `UX-SPEC.md`'s own CS1-CS5 change-set numbering does not map 1:1 to this changelog's Round numbers - all five change sets shipped together as this one round.

### CS1 - progress mechanism (addon-sync.ps1, addon-server.ps1)

- **Added** - `-ProgressPath`/`Write-ProgressStep` in `addon-sync.ps1`: five call sites through the main sync loop and both per-addon sync functions write a small `progress.json` (`{total, index, addon, phase, [failPhase]}`) as each addon moves through `checking`/`downloading`/`installing`/its final mapped phase; a `failed` write carries `failPhase` (checking/downloading/installing). No-op when the param is absent - fully backward compatible. The CLI's final `-Json` stdout contract is completely untouched (verified: same top-level key set, same bare failure-row shape).
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1` threads `-ProgressPath` through `Start-Job` for the `sync`/`check`/`add`/`install` job kinds, does a best-effort read of it in `Update-JobStatus` into `Job.progress`, and exposes it via `Get-JobStatusView`. New `Get-ComputedFreshness` helper and three new `/api/state` fields: `freshness` (`not_checked`/`checking`/`up_to_date`/`updates_available`/`check_failed`), `lastCheckFailed`, `lastCheckError` (both in-memory only, reset on restart). `Apply-JobCompletionSideEffects` gains a failure branch that sets the latter two instead of silently overwriting them with a stale success timestamp.

### CS2 - Home / Freshness / Row / Progress UI (ui/app.js, ui/index.html, ui/style.css)

- **Added** - `Components.Freshness`: the one place freshness is computed-for-display, mounted twice (compact in the sidebar, full atop My Addons) and nowhere else. Replaces the old sidebar "Server OK - checked X ago" text, the My Addons multi-clause summary sentence, and the separate "Last run ... Details" line (and its now-deleted `Actions.showLastRunDetails`) outright.
- **Changed** - My Addons table: 7 columns to 5 (Installed+Latest merge into one Version cell, Compat folds into Status). One `Components.Chip.forStatus(addon)` replaces the old separate Status/Compat chip renderers with the full 8-priority pill vocabulary (Couldn't update - Retry > Needs: X > Update > Old patch > Won't work this patch > Pinned > Ignoring updates > Up to date); the two actionable pills are real buttons that start that one addon's job directly. The old hidden Compat hover tooltip (raw Toc Interface numbers) is deleted outright, not just hidden.
- **Changed** - Filter chips: only All/Updates always shown; every other status appears only once its count is > 0, grouped under one "More" popover.
- **Changed** - `Components.JobPanel`: a determinate `<progress>` bar + "Updating i of N addons..." + a current-item phase line, driven by `job.progress`; the raw log moves behind a closed-by-default "Details" disclosure; a completed run with a failure shows one clean results list with a plain-language reason (mapped from `failPhase`) and an inline Retry scoped to just that addon. Poll interval dropped 800ms to 500ms.
- **Fixed** - the detail drawer's worst functional defect (flagged independently by every judge): the header now reads a tracked addon's name/author from `Store.state.addons` (known instantly) before ever falling back to the mod/enrich response, which could itself resolve to a literal "Project N" placeholder that used to silently overwrite the already-known real name once it loaded.

### CS3 - Search / Browse (ui/app.js, ui/index.html)

- **Removed** - the CurseForge/Wago source-tab switch, the category/sort selects, pagination, and the permanent keyless banner ("Showing a keyless index via community mirrors...").
- **Changed** - one search box now fires the CurseForge and Wago searches concurrently; results merge into one grid, interleaved by relevance tier then downloads, each card carrying a plain source badge (the "Indexed"/"Live match" provenance pill is deleted outright, not relocated). A thin-results-only line ("Search results are limited without a CurseForge key.") replaces the old permanent banner. "Install by Project ID" is demoted into a fallback line under the grid alongside "Search CurseForge.com directly", both reusing existing dialogs/plumbing - no new server code.
- **Removed** - the `curseforge://` protocol toggle from Browse; its one remaining home is Settings > Advanced.

### CS4 - Settings (ui/app.js, ui/index.html, ui/style.css, addon-server.ps1)

- **Changed** - Settings restructured into an always-expanded Essentials (Updates, Appearance, CurseForge key - under a 60-word running-prose budget) and one closed-by-default Advanced disclosure holding everything else in `UX-SPEC.md`'s specified order. The release-channel 3-way radio becomes two independent toggles ("Include beta versions" in Essentials, "Also include alpha/experimental versions" in Advanced) driving the same `releaseType` value.
- **Changed** - the four "Open sync log/last run report/server log/backups folder" buttons collapse into one "Open logs folder" button; **added** a matching `/api/open {what:'logs'}` server target that opens the app's own folder (containing all of them) in Explorer.
- **Changed** - Diagnostics on-screen rows reworded to plain pass/fail language ("Reached CurseForge OK" instead of `Reachable (HTTP 200)`, "6 addons tracked" instead of `addons.json: 6 records`); PowerShell version, raw ISO timestamps and internal source names are dropped from the visible list entirely, staying verbatim in "Copy report". "Port" is dropped from the visible About list (kept in Copy report only).
- **Changed** - "Adopt"/"Adopting" renamed to "Take over"/"Taking over" and "Untracked folders" renamed to "Folders Furphy doesn't manage yet" in the Settings section this change set owned.

### CS5 - copy sweep, CSS pruning, deep-link param, doc updates (ui/app.js, ui/index.html, ui/style.css, SPEC.md, ROADMAP.md, README.md)

- **Fixed** - applied every remaining row of `UX-SPEC.md`'s copy table that CS2-4 hadn't already covered: the toolbar's "Check for updates" button (still unrenamed to "Check now"), the first-run "take over" dialog (dynamic "Found N addons..." title, rewritten body, "Take over all (N)" button, and its job-panel label - "Adopting" to "Taking over"), the Add-addon dialog and its three validation-error toasts (dropped "Project ID" wording), the manual-ID input in the untracked-folders row (placeholder + toast), the empty My Addons state ("No addons yet" / "Add one by name, link, or ID." plus a second "Browse addons" button, matching the spec's own wireframe), the drawer's keyless description/changelog/screenshot messages (shortened to the spec's exact wording), the drawer's old "via catalogue"/"via addon-radar"/"via Wago Addons" provenance pill (deleted outright, replaced with the same plain CurseForge/Wago source badge used everywhere else), the drawer's Compatibility section (dropped its raw "Toc Interface: ..." detail line - the same jargon the table's own hover tooltip was already deleted for, now also gone from here - and renamed "Older patch (12.0.7)"/"Not for Midnight" to the table pill's own "Old patch"/"Won't work this patch" wording so the two never say it two different ways), and the catalogue-refresh status/toast text (dropped a leaking raw internal source name and the word "indexed").
- **Fixed** - a filter chip ("Old patch / won't work") that CS2 had added on its own, beyond what `UX-SPEC.md` section 3.4's own four-item extras list (Pinned/Ignored/Couldn't update/Needs another addon) specifies - the copy table is explicit that a separate patch-staleness filter chip is deleted, folded only into the Status pill. Removed the chip, its `matchesFilter` branch, and the now-dead `isStale()` helper.
- **Removed** - dead CSS left behind by CS2-4's own element deletions: the whole `.cf-web-search*` block (the old Browse search-box-plus-protocol-row card), `.browse-source-row`, `.loadmore-wrap`, `.source-pill` (superseded by the plain source badge above), `.compat-detail`, and `.banner.is-info` (the old permanent keyless-banner style).
- **Added** - a `?view=my-addons|browse|settings` query param (`App.applyInitialViewFromQuery()`, called once at the top of `App.init()`) that selects the initial view on load; default behavior (no/unrecognized param) is unchanged. Built for this round's own verification and any future deep link.
- **Verified** - a whole-tree sweep for `UX-SPEC.md` section 11's banned-term list turned up zero remaining hits in visible strings (labels, buttons, chips, toasts, dialog copy, placeholders, aria-labels, title attributes) after the fixes above; every hit found before fixing is listed in this round's own session notes. `node --check ui/app.js` and `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `.ps1` files pass clean throughout. Live-verified in the Browser pane at `?mock=1` and `?mock=1&host=webview2`: My Addons (table/pills/freshness/filters), the detail drawer (tracked-name-first header, compat pill, keyless messages), Browse (merged grid, fallback links), Settings (Essentials word count, Advanced collapsed-by-default, Diagnostics on-screen vs. Copy report), the first-run dialog, the empty state, the Add-addon dialog, and the job panel's running/done/failed states - all four themes spot-checked for the new source badge and pill colors, Lofi Night confirmed still default with its art untouched.

## Round 13 (native host half of host theme sync + CurseForge-tab routing)

Completes E19b (SPEC.md's "E19b" section, ROADMAP.md). Round 12 shipped the server+UI half only; this round shipped the `host\FurphyHost.cs` half that round 12's own changelog entry and SPEC.md flagged as not yet done - `host\FurphyHost.cs` previously had no `WebMessageReceived` handler and no DWM calls at all.

### host component (host\FurphyHost.cs, host\selftest.html)

- **Added** - `HostWebView_WebMessageReceived`, wired to both `_furphyWebView.CoreWebView2.WebMessageReceived` (per the contract) and `_cfWebView.CoreWebView2.WebMessageReceived` (a deliberate addition beyond the contract's literal wording - see below). Parses `e.WebMessageAsJson` via the existing `MiniJson` helper into `{type:"open-curseforge", url}` or `{type:"theme", name, colors:{...}}`; any missing/wrong-typed key is tolerated (colors keeps its previous value per-key; a missing `type` is a no-op).
- **Added** - `HandleOpenCurseforgeMessage(string url)`: if `url` starts with `https://www.curseforge.com/` (ordinal, case-insensitive), activates the CurseForge tab and navigates `_cfWebView` there; otherwise logs and ignores.
- **Added** - `ApplyTheme(string name, Dictionary<string,object> colors, bool persistIfChanged)` and `RecolorChrome()`: repaint the form `BackColor`, `_navPanel`/tab bar (`bg0`), active tab button (`bg2`), hover (`bg3`), inactive tab (`bg0`), `_cfToolbar` (`bg1`), search box (`bg2`), button/muted text, accent, and both `WebView2` `DefaultBackgroundColor`s (`bg0`) to the reported (or persisted/default) palette.
- **Added** - `ApplyTitleBarColors()`: calls `DwmSetWindowAttribute` on the form handle for `DWMWA_USE_IMMERSIVE_DARK_MODE` (20, `1` when `bg0`'s relative luminance < 0.5 else `0`), `DWMWA_CAPTION_COLOR` (35, `bg0`), `DWMWA_TEXT_COLOR` (36, `text`), `DWMWA_BORDER_COLOR` (34, `border`) - `COLORREF` built as `0x00BBGGRR`. Called from `OnHandleCreated` (handle-created trigger) and again at the end of every `ApplyTheme` call (theme-message trigger); each HRESULT is recorded (`captionHresult`/`darkModeHresult` etc. for the selftest marker) and a non-zero result (expected on pre-22000 Windows builds for the caption/text/border attributes) is tolerated, not thrown.
- **Added** - `InitializeDefaultTheme()`/`LoadPersistedTheme()` (constructor-time only, no HTTP): the initial palette is the Lofi Night values (replacing the old Vaporwave `ChromeBg`/`ChromeBgAlt`/`ChromeBgActive`/`ChromeText`/`ChromeMuted`/`ChromeAccent`/`ChromeHover` constants near line 620) unless `settings.json` has a persisted `hostTheme`, read via `HostFiles.LoadJsonObject(_settingsPath)["hostTheme"]` - the same file `HostFiles` already resolves via `FindUpward` for `port`/`adFilter`/`hostWindow`.
- **Added** - `PersistThemeIfChanged()`: writes `hostTheme` to `settings.json` via `HostFiles.UpdateJsonObject` (the same direct atomic-write mechanism `SaveWindowBounds` already uses for `hostWindow`) whenever an incoming theme message's colors differ from the last-saved ones. **Deviation from the literal contract text, deliberately**: the task brief said to save it "the same way it already saves hostWindow - via the server's settings endpoint" and, conditionally, "do not write settings.json directly if hostWindow goes through the server." Reading the actual code (`SaveWindowBounds`, and `addon-server.ps1`'s `Handle-SettingsPut` comment) showed `hostWindow` does NOT go through `PUT /api/settings` as its primary path - it's a direct `HostFiles.UpdateJsonObject` write. Per the brief's own conditional, `PersistThemeIfChanged` mirrors that actual mechanism instead: no HTTP call to the server is made from the host for this. Server-side `PUT /api/settings` validation/round-trip for `hostTheme` (SPEC.md's E19b section) still exists and still works - it's just not the path the host itself uses to persist.
- **Added** - `host\selftest.html` extended to post, ~1 s after load, a `{type:"theme", ...}` message with a distinctive test palette (`bg0 "#102030"`, `bg1 "#203040"`, `bg2 "#304050"`, `bg3 "#405060"`, `border "#506070"`, `text "#f0f0f0"`, `muted "#c0c0c0"`, `accent "#ff8800"`), then a `{type:"open-curseforge", url:"https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons/bigwigs"}` message. The `--selftest` JSON marker gains `themeMessages` (count received), `themeBg0` (last-applied `bg0`, `"#102030"` expected), `tabBarPixel`/`toolbarPixel` (now expected `"#102030"`/`"#203040"` after the message), `captionHresult`/`darkModeHresult` (ints, `0` = S_OK), `openCurseforgeUrl` (last handled url), `cfTabActiveAfterMessage` (bool). Every pre-existing marker field (`init`, `version`, `dpi`, `dpiAware`, `intercepted`, `jobPostStatus`, blocked/allowed lists) is unchanged.
- **Deliberate deviation, flagged** - contract F says `selftest.html` "is loaded in `_furphyWebView` during `--selftest`." Tracing `MainForm_Load`/`SelftestActive` showed this is not what the code actually does and was told not to change: `_furphyWebView.Source` always points at the real app (`http://localhost:<port>/?host=webview2`); only `_cfWebView.Source` points at the selftest page during `--selftest` (confirmed against `e19.workflow.js` and this file's own Round 10 entries describing the same architecture). Only `_cfWebView` has the `curseforge://` interception `selftest.html`'s deep-link relies on. So `HostWebView_WebMessageReceived` is attached to BOTH `_furphyWebView.CoreWebView2.InitializationCompleted` (per the letter of the spec, and what production actually uses - the SPA never runs in `_cfWebView`) AND `_cfWebView`'s (so the selftest page, which really does load there, can reach the same handler and populate the new marker fields at all).
- **Not verified end-to-end** - `--selftest` itself was not run this round (per instructions, a later verify stage does). Verified only via: a clean `host\build-host.ps1` compile (zero errors), full code-reading/tracing against the contract, and byte-scanning both files as pure ASCII. Specifically unverified: the real `DWMWA_CAPTION_COLOR`/`DWMWA_BORDER_COLOR`/`DWMWA_TEXT_COLOR` HRESULTs this machine's Windows build actually returns; whether `e.WebMessageAsJson` serializes a plain-object `postMessage` exactly the way `MiniJson` expects; and the real pixel/marker values (`tabBarPixel`/`toolbarPixel`/`themeBg0`/`openCurseforgeUrl`/`cfTabActiveAfterMessage`) a live `--selftest` run would produce. `addon-server.ps1`'s side of the `hostTheme` server contract and `ui\app.js`'s side of the postMessage contract were not touched or re-verified this round (out of scope - see Round 12's own entries for that half).

## Round 12 (host theme + cf-window)

Server + UI half only, per SPEC.md's new E19b section (the `host\FurphyHost.cs` half - receiving these messages and actually recoloring the WinForms chrome/title bar - is a separate item, not touched this round). Two problems: (1) Browse's "Search on CurseForge.com" box has posted the `'cf-window'` `/api/open` target since Round 9, but the server never gained a case for it, so every click toasted "Couldn't open that: unknown what: cf-window"; (2) the native host's chrome/title bar is hard-coded to the old Vaporwave palette regardless of which theme (now Lofi Night by default, since Round 11) the app is actually showing.

### server component (addon-server.ps1)

- **Added** - `'cf-window'` `/api/open` target: `POST /api/open {what:'cf-window', url}` -> 400 if `url` is missing or does not start with `https://www.curseforge.com/` (narrower than E3's `'url'` target, which also allows `addons.wago.io`); else `Open-CfSideWindow` opens a chromeless side window (`msedge.exe --app=<url> --window-size=1100,900`), same `msedge.exe` path + `Test-Path`/`Start-Process` fallback `Open-InBrowser` and the `$OpenBrowser` startup block already use elsewhere in this file - there is no registry/App Paths lookup anywhere in this codebase to reuse instead (grepped `addon-server.ps1`, `Addon Manager.vbs`, `curseforge-handler.vbs`: all three hardcode the same default-install path). `{ok:true}`.
- **Added** - `settings.json` gains an optional `hostTheme` object (`{name, colors:{bg0,bg1,...}}`), round-tripped through `Get-DefaultSettings`/`Get-Settings`/`Get-SettingsView` the same way E19's existing `hostWindow` already is - EXCEPT that unlike `hostWindow` (an opaque host-owned blob this server never inspects), `PUT /api/settings`'s `hostTheme` is actually validated by a new `Test-HostTheme`: `name` a 1-32 char string matching `^[a-z0-9-]+$`, `colors` an object of at most 12 keys whose values all match `^#[0-9a-fA-F]{6}$`; anything else -> 400 `{error}`, nothing from that PUT saved (all-or-nothing, same shape `releaseType`'s existing out-of-range check uses). `GET /api/settings`/`/api/state` return it unmasked (not a secret). The native host itself is expected to keep writing it the same way it already writes `hostWindow` - a direct `settings.json` read-modify-write (`HostFiles.UpdateJsonObject`), bypassing this endpoint entirely - so this validation only actually gates a client PUTting the shape straight to the API, not the host's own writes.
- **Fixed (caught during this round's own verification, before ship)** - `Test-HostTheme`'s first draft checked the name field with `-notmatch '^[a-z0-9-]+$'`. PowerShell's `-match`/`-notmatch` are case-INSENSITIVE by default (unlike almost every other language's regex-match operator) - confirmed live against the real running test server: `PUT /api/settings {hostTheme:{name:"LofiNight", colors:{bg0:"#0f1226"}}}` returned 200 and the uppercase name round-tripped straight into `settings.json`, silently violating the documented lowercase-only contract. Switched to `-cnotmatch` (case-sensitive); re-verified the same request now correctly returns 400, and a lowercase `"lofi"` with a full 8-key color set still returns 200 and round-trips exactly as saved.
- **Verified** - against a real throwaway server instance (`addon-server.ps1 -Port 47899`, this build root, never the production install): `POST /api/open {what:'cf-window'}` (no url) -> 400 `{"error":"url required"}`; `{what:'cf-window', url:'https://addons.wago.io/x'}` -> 400 `{"error":"url must start with https://www.curseforge.com/"}`; the actual curseforge.com-url-opens-a-window case was deliberately NOT run (would have opened a real window on the desktop, per the standing instruction for this pass). `PUT /api/settings` with a valid 8-key `hostTheme` -> 200, and a subsequent `GET /api/settings` returned it back unchanged; an uppercase name, a non-hex color value, a `hostTheme` missing `colors` entirely, and a 13-key `colors` object all correctly returned 400 with nothing saved (confirmed via a follow-up GET still showing the last-accepted value, not the rejected one). Test writes cleaned from `settings.json` afterward (`hostTheme` key removed) so this scratch build root's settings file was not left with test residue; the server was stopped via `POST /api/shutdown` and `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 47899` confirmed the port free afterward. `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` returns zero tokenizer errors; the file confirmed pure ASCII (byte-scanned, zero bytes > 127) after every edit in this section.

### ui component (index.html, app.js)

- **Added** - a new `Host` module (`ui\app.js`, placed immediately before `App`, whose `getServerHost()` it reads): `isNative()` (`window.chrome && window.chrome.webview && App.getServerHost() === "webview2"`, wrapped in try/catch so a call before `App`/`Host` itself exists yet degrades to `false` instead of throwing - see below), `post(obj)` (`window.chrome.webview.postMessage`, no-op outside the native host), `openCurseForge(url)` (posts `{type:"open-curseforge", url}` and returns `true` only for a curseforge.com URL in the native host, so callers can fall back to their existing `/api/open` call otherwise), and `reportTheme()` (posts `{type:"theme", name, colors}` - `name` from `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` directly, `colors` from `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)` on `--bg-0/--bg-1/--bg-2/--bg-3/--border/--text/--text-muted/--accent`, trimmed and `#rrggbb`-validated per key, non-matching keys omitted rather than sent malformed).
- **Added** - `Prefs.applyTheme` now calls `Host.reportTheme()` after stamping `data-theme`, wrapped in try/catch: this function's very first call is `Prefs`'s own module-load-time `applyTheme(readTheme())`, which runs before `Host` (and, transitively, `App`, which `Host.isNative()` depends on) exist yet this far down the file - a `ReferenceError` there is caught and the call is a harmless no-op. `App.init()` calls `Host.reportTheme()` again, unguarded, right after `await fetchPingInfo()` resolves - the earliest point `App.getServerHost()` actually has an answer - which is what delivers the real initial report; every later call (a user switching themes in Settings > Appearance, long after the whole script has loaded) goes through cleanly on the first try.
- **Added** - `Actions.openOnCurseForge` now builds the project-page URL client-side (`https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons/<slug>` when slug known, else `.../projects/<projectId>` - the same shape the server's own `'curseforge'` target already built) and tries `Host.openCurseForge` first, falling back to the existing `'curseforge'` `/api/open` call unchanged when not in the native host. `Actions.searchCurseForgeWebsite` (Browse's search box) and `Actions.searchDependency`'s no-key fallback (drawer Overview's "Search CurseForge" on a missing dependency) get the same `Host.openCurseForge`-first treatment, falling back to `'cf-window'`/`'url'` respectively. `Actions.openOnWago` is untouched - Wago URLs always use `'url'`, never routed through the host's CurseForge tab.
- **Added** - `ui\index.html`: `#cf-web-search`'s explainer `<p>` gains `id="cf-web-search-hint"`. `Views.browse.render()` now sets its text to "Opens in the CurseForge tab; installs still happen here by Project ID for now." in the native host, or the original "Opens CurseForge in a side window; ..." wording otherwise - re-evaluated on every render, guarded the same try/catch-around-`Host` way `Prefs.applyTheme` is (this view can in principle render before `Host` exists, though in practice `App.init()`'s render pass never runs that early).
- **Verified** - `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean after every edit in this section. `?mock=1`'s `/api/open` mock (`if (p === "/api/open" && method === "POST") return { ok: true };`) already accepts any `what` unconditionally, `cf-window` included - confirmed by reading the mock's dispatcher directly; no mock change was needed. Grepped `ui\index.html` for duplicate `id` attributes after the `cf-web-search-hint` addition - none found.

### Contract notes for the native-host builder (host\FurphyHost.cs, not touched this round)

- Messages to parse from `CoreWebView2.WebMessageReceived` (`e.WebMessageAsJson`, a JSON object, not a string): `{type:"open-curseforge", url}` and `{type:"theme", name, colors:{bg0,bg1,bg2,bg3,border,text,muted,accent}}` (any subset of the 8 color keys may be present - a key is omitted client-side, not sent as an invalid value, when the corresponding CSS custom property didn't resolve to `#rrggbb`).
- `PUT /api/settings` accepts and validates `hostTheme` (see above) - the host can `PUT` its own writes through this endpoint instead of a direct `settings.json` read-modify-write if that's simpler now that server-side validation exists, though `hostWindow`'s own precedent (direct write, this server only round-trips it) is also still fully supported.
- `GET /api/settings`/`/api/state` return the last-saved `hostTheme` unmasked, for painting the right chrome before the page has loaded on the next launch.

## Round 1

No code changes applied. All three findings against addon-server.ps1 were verified and rejected:

- **StreamReader leak in Update-JobStatus (high)** - rejected. The reader is created from the already-open `$fs` FileStream and never gets its own OS handle; the enclosing `finally { $fs.Close() }` (line ~646) closes the real underlying file handle on every poll regardless of whether `$reader` was explicitly closed. There is no handle growth across repeated 800ms polls; the "~100 polls / 82s -> handle exhaustion" scenario does not occur.
- **Addon disable/enable (medium)** - rejected, out of scope for this round. Not present anywhere in SPEC.md (the authoritative spec): no `disable`/`enable` job kind, no `disabledAt`/`disabledReason` fields in the addons.json record contract, no `-Disable`/`-Enable` CLI params. The "E11 bulk actions" reference traces to ROADMAP.md ("E11 - Bulk actions"), a future-work doc, not the current spec. Implementing it now would require unauthorized changes to addon-sync.ps1 (a different component) and to the addons.json record shape.
- **Download progress percentage (medium)** - rejected, out of scope for this round. SPEC.md's JobStatus shape (`id, kind, params, state, startedAt, finishedAt, exitCode, log[], results[], error`) and result-row shape (`status, name, version, projectId, fileId`) have no progress field, and the job panel spec only calls for live log lines plus a final results list - which addon-server.ps1 already provides via the sync.log tail in `Update-JobStatus`. The fix also requires CLI-side changes (parsing CurseForge response headers in addon-sync.ps1), a different component than the one being fixed here.

### ui component (app.js / style.css)

- **Fixed (high)** - `pagehide` -> `sendBeacon('/api/shutdown')` fired on any reload/navigation, not just an actual window close, silently killing the local backend on a plain F5 and leaving the user on a dead, unstyled page. Removed the listener entirely in `App.wireGlobal()` (app.js); the server's existing `-IdleMinutes` auto-exit already reclaims the process.
- **Fixed (high)** - the bottom job-progress panel had no auto-dismiss and, once left open, could visually cover newly-rendered content in a different view (e.g. a completed Settings > Scan result row), making a successful action look like it did nothing. Added `Components.JobPanel.collapseIfOpen()` and call it from `App.switchView()` (app.js) so the panel collapses to its slim title bar on every view change instead of staying full-height over the page; it stays reachable via the collapse chevron.
- **Fixed (medium)** - the Versions tab table omitted the game-version column even though `/api/addons/{id}/files` already returns `gameVersions[]` per file. Added a "Game Versions" header/cell to `Components.Drawer`'s versions table (app.js) and a `.version-gameversions` style (style.css).
- **Fixed (medium)** - at the app's documented 1000px minimum width, `.addon-table`'s 900px min-width overflows its ~720px content column, and the resulting horizontal scroll hid the per-row kebab (More actions) menu - the only way to Update/Pin/Ignore/Uninstall a single addon from that view. Made the last table cell (`.addon-row td:last-child`) `position: sticky; right: 0` with an explicit background in style.css so the kebab stays reachable regardless of scroll position.
- **Rejected** - "async API-key-test handler has no try/catch" (app.js ~1909-1918). `Actions.testKey()` already wraps `Api.testKey()` in its own try/catch and always resolves to `{ok, message}`; the DOM elements queried (`#input-apikey`, `#apikey-test-result`) are static and always present in index.html. No path to an unhandled rejection exists.
- **Rejected** - "install-by-ID promise chain missing .catch()" (app.js ~1793-1796). `Actions.addByProjectId()` → `Actions.startJob()` already wraps its only awaited call (`Api.postJob`) in try/catch and always resolves to `true`/`false`; the chained `.then()` has nothing to fail on.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "per-addon release channel override UI" needs a new server endpoint (`POST /api/addons/{id}/release-type`) and a new CLI param (`-SetReleaseType`), neither of which exists in SPEC.md's addon-server.ps1/addon-sync.ps1 contracts - both are different components than the ui-only pass this round covers.
- **Rejected** - "missing `pagehide` -> `sendBeacon('/api/shutdown')`" (app.js ~2400-2407, expansions review pass). This is the exact listener already removed by the "Fixed (high)" entry above in this same round: `pagehide` fires on a plain F5/reload and any in-tab navigation, not only on an actual window close, and script has no reliable way to tell those apart. Re-wiring it as the finding suggests would reintroduce killing the local backend mid-reload, stranding the user on a dead unstyled page with no recovery short of the desktop shortcut. The documented comment block in `App.wireGlobal()` stands as-is; `-IdleMinutes` auto-exit remains the intended cleanup path.

### E2 - Automatic update checks (addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1` now persists the last check results (`updatesCheckedAt` + the `updateAvailable` map) to `ROOT\state.json` after every `check`/`sync`/`add`/`remove` job (`Save-CheckState`) and reloads them once at startup (`Load-CheckState`), so the sidebar's "n updates" badge and the "checked X ago" status line survive a server restart instead of resetting to unknown.
- **Added** - `ui\app.js` now starts a background `check` job on its own: once on load (if the last check is missing or more than 10 minutes old) and again every 30 minutes while the window stays open, skipping whenever another job is already running (`Actions.autoCheckForUpdates`, `App.scheduleAutoCheck`). The automatic check's progress panel stays collapsed to its slim title bar rather than popping open full-height like a manually-triggered check does.

### E7 - UI polish batch (addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - status filter chips (All / Updates / Pinned / Ignored / Failed / Missing deps) above the My Addons table, each with a live count, combining with the existing search box to filter the visible rows (`Views.myAddons` in app.js, `.filter-chips` in style.css).
- **Added** - the My Addons column headers (Addon, Installed, Latest, Status, Updated) are now clickable to sort, with a ▲/▼ indicator on the active column and `aria-sort` for assistive tech; replaces the old plain `<select>` sort control. The choice persists across reloads via `localStorage`.
- **Added** - keyboard shortcuts: `/` focuses the current view's search box, `r` re-runs Check for updates (only when idle), and `Escape` now also closes an open row menu in addition to the drawer/dialogs/lightbox it already closed. All three are suppressed while typing in a field or with a modifier key held.
- **Added** - Settings > Appearance: a Density toggle (Comfortable/Compact) and a Theme toggle (Dark/Light), both persisted in `localStorage` and applied via `data-density`/`data-theme` on `<html>` before first paint (`Prefs` in app.js). The light theme is a complete token override in style.css, including two colors (`.banner` and `.form-msg.is-error` text) that were hardcoded and would otherwise have gone unreadable on light backgrounds.
- **Added** - Settings > Maintenance > "Open backups folder", backed by a new `'backups'` case in `addon-server.ps1`'s `Handle-Open` (creates `ROOT\backups` first if it doesn't exist yet, rather than treating an empty/missing folder as an error).
- **Added** - `title` tooltips on every icon-only button that was missing one (drawer close, job panel collapse/close, lightbox close, API-key show/hide, per-row kebab, toast dismiss), and focus-visible outlines on inputs, selects, links, dropdown items, and drawer tabs that previously had none.

### cli component (addon-sync.ps1)

- **Fixed (high)** - a `-DryRun -Json` "Would-update" row paired the NEW target `version` string with the OLD (currently-installed) `fileId`. In the main sync loop, every results row was built as `FileId = $record.fileId` unconditionally; for a real Installed/Updated row this is correct because `Sync-SingleAddon` mutates `$Record.fileId` in place before returning, but its DryRun branch returns early without ever touching `$Record`, so `$record.fileId` there still held the old/currently-installed id while `Version` (from the newly selected file's `displayName`) already reported the new/target version - reproduced directly: project 911525 recorded at fileId 8672833/v9.1 with 8787143/v9.2 actually newest, `-DryRun -Json` returned `{"status":"Would-update","version":"v9.2","fileId":8672833}`, an impossible pairing (8672833 is v9.1). This corrupted `addon-server.ps1`'s persisted `updateAvailable` map (and `/api/state`/`state.json`) the same way, so any explicit-fileId install action built on it would silently re-pin the addon to the file it is already on instead of the real update. Fixed by having `Sync-SingleAddon`'s DryRun branch return an explicit `FileId = $selectedFileId` (the newly selected/target file, matching `Version`) and having the outer loop prefer that field when present, falling back to `$record.fileId` for every other status (which has no such field, since only DryRun leaves `$record` unmutated).
- **Fixed (medium)** - `-Files <id> -Json` added an undocumented `error` field to its JSON output on a network failure (`{action,projectId,files,error}`), but SPEC.md line 55 documents the shape as exactly `{"action":"files","projectId":N,"files":[...]}`, and no other `-Json` action in the script adds an ad-hoc field like this. Removed the `Add-Member ... 'error'` block; the failure is still logged to sync.log (unchanged) and the non-JSON table path still prints `ERROR: $filesError` to the console.
- **Fixed (high)** - every array-typed CLI parameter (`-Add`, `-Remove`, `-Only`, `-Unpin`, `-Ignore`, `-Unignore`) silently mis-bound under the script's real invocation (`powershell.exe -File addon-sync.ps1 ...`, used by both the launcher and `addon-server.ps1`'s job dispatch): a comma-joined token like `-Remove 1521253,911525` (quoted or not) arrived as one literal-comma string instead of being split, so `-Remove` silently no-opped (reported `Skipped`, removed nothing) and `-Add` (declared `[int[]]`) made PowerShell's own parameter binding throw before the script body ever ran - exit code 1, a native `Cannot convert value ... to type System.Int32[]` error on stderr, and zero stdout even with `-Json`. Declared all six parameters `[string[]]` and added `ConvertTo-ExpandedStringArray`/`ConvertTo-ExpandedIdArray`, called immediately before the existing "compute targeting flags" block, which re-split every raw element on commas and (for the five id-typed params) parse each piece to `int64`; a non-numeric piece now fails through the script's normal `Write-Log`/exit-2 path instead of throwing past it. Verified end-to-end via `-File`: `-Remove 1521253,911525 -Json` now correctly empties `addons[]` in one call (previously a `Skipped` no-op); `-Add 1521253,911525 -FileId <id>` now correctly parses two ids and hits the existing "-FileId requires exactly one project id" validation (previously crashed at parameter binding, before reaching that check); comma-joined `-Ignore`/`-Unignore`/`-Unpin` verified likewise; single-value (no-comma) usage of all six parameters regression-tested unchanged.

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - unquoted Handle-Open paths

All five findings verified against the code and fixed. `Start-Process -ArgumentList` joins array/string elements with spaces and never quotes them (the same pitfall already documented and handled for `New-CliProcessArgs`), so every path handed to `notepad.exe`/`explorer.exe` in `Handle-Open` needed the same `'"' + $path + '"'` treatment - most visibly for the production deploy target `C:\Program Files (x86)\World of Warcraft\_retail_\AddonSync\`, whose ROOT and derived paths (sync.log, addons.json, last-run.txt, backups\) all contain spaces and parentheses, and for the AddOns folder itself (`...\World of Warcraft\_retail_\Interface\AddOns`).

- **Fixed (high)** - `'log'` case (line ~1552): quoted `$Script:SyncLogPath` before passing to `notepad.exe`.
- **Fixed (high)** - `'folder'` case (line ~1557): quoted `$addonsPath` before passing to `explorer.exe`.
- **Fixed (high)** - `'addons'` case (line ~1565): quoted `$Script:AddonsJsonPath` before passing to `notepad.exe`.
- **Fixed (high)** - `'lastrun'` case (line ~1587): quoted `$lastRunPath` before passing to `notepad.exe`.
- **Fixed (high)** - `'backups'` case (line ~1608): quoted `$backupsPath` before passing to `explorer.exe`.

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - multi-id sync job silently dropped ids

- **Fixed (high)** - `Build-CliArgs`'s `sync` job kind built a multi-id sync as `-Only id1 id2 id3 ...`, with each id its own separate `-ArgumentList` element passed into `Start-Process`. addon-sync.ps1 is always launched as a brand-new `powershell.exe -File ...` child process (never in-process), and Windows PowerShell 5.1's own `-File` argument binder does not collect several space-separated bare tokens into one array-typed parameter the way an in-process call (`& $script -Only 1 2 3`) does - reproduced directly: only the first id bound to `-Only`, and every id after it fell through as a positional value for whichever parameter came next, with no error surfaced anywhere (not in the job's results, not in sync.log, not in server.log). A multi-id sync job would therefore silently sync/reinstall only the first addon in the requested batch. This pairs with the cli component's fix this same round: addon-sync.ps1's id-array parameters are `[string[]]` and re-split any comma-joined token internally (`ConvertTo-ExpandedStringArray`/`ConvertTo-ExpandedIdArray`), and a single comma-joined token like `"1521253,911525"` (unlike separate space-separated tokens) does survive `-File` binding intact as one literal string - verified directly against that real normalization code. Changed the `sync` case to comma-join `$Params.ids` into one `-Only` token instead of adding each id as its own array element; `install`/`add`/`remove` were already single-id and needed no change. Not reachable through any UI control built so far (My Addons only ever sends single-id sync jobs; the multi-id "Update selected" bulk action is still on the E11 backlog), so it had produced no visible failures yet, but would have silently corrupted multi-id sync results the moment that UI ships.

## Round 2

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - remaining Get-Content sites re-audited

- **Rejected (verified, no behavior change needed)** - "audit every remaining Get-Content whose output can reach a JSON response" (high). Grepped every `Get-Content` call left in addon-server.ps1: `Get-Settings` (settings.json), `Get-AddonRecords` (addons.json), `Load-CheckState` (state.json) - three sites total, matching the two already-converted call sites (`Update-JobStatus`, `Invoke-Cli`) that read raw CLI stdout/stderr text directly into an error/response field. In all three remaining sites the decorated `$raw` string returned by `Get-Content -Raw` is consumed only as input to `ConvertFrom-Json` and is never itself assigned into a hashtable/PSCustomObject that later reaches `Send-Json`/`ConvertTo-Json`; every value that does reach a response (`$obj.releaseType`, `$obj.cfApiKey`, addon record properties, `$obj.updatesCheckedAt`, etc.) is a fresh object produced by JSON parsing, not the original PSPath/PSDrive/PSProvider-decorated string, so `ConvertTo-Json` never walks the provider/runspace graph for these. This matches the finding's own carve-out ("Keep ConvertFrom-Json inputs as they are (parsed objects are clean)"), so no functional change was made. Added a one-line comment above each of the three `Get-Content` calls (Get-Settings ~L235, Get-AddonRecords ~L287, Load-CheckState ~L625) documenting why the read is safe there, so a future edit that starts threading `$raw` itself into a response won't reintroduce the hazard unnoticed.

### cli component (addon-sync.ps1)

- **Fixed (high)** - author backfill for the pre-existing "Unknown author" records. `Sync-SingleAddon`'s `Up-to-date` early return (the path hit for every already-current addon) never touched `$Record` at all, so the ~37 records saved before the `author` field existed stayed `null` forever - a plain sync never has a reason to revisit a file it isn't installing. Since `$needsInstall` is only false when `$selected.id` already equals `$Record.fileId`, `$selected` at that point *is* the file record matching what's installed, with `$selected.user.username` sitting right there unused. Added a metadata-only backfill immediately before that return: when `-not $DryRun` and `-not $Record.author` and the selected file carries `user.username`, copy it onto `$Record.author`. Gated on `-not $DryRun` specifically because this early return is reached identically in both DryRun and real runs (the `$DryRun` check that gates the install path sits further down, after this point) - without that guard a plain `-DryRun` "check" would have silently started mutating records, which SPEC.md's "no disk writes" DryRun guarantee forbids. No change needed to force a save: `Save-Config` already runs unconditionally on every non-DryRun invocation that reaches the main sync loop (it is not conditioned on `$needsInstall`/status), so the backfilled `author` is persisted on the very next `addons.json` write regardless of whether an install happened this run.
- **Rejected** - "include `$filesError` in the `-Files -Json` output" (high). This is the exact `error` field Round 1 already added and then deliberately removed from this same JSON block, on the grounds that SPEC.md line 55 documents the `-Files -Json` shape exactly as `{"action":"files","projectId":N,"files":[...]}` with no error field, and no other `-Json` action in the script adds an ad-hoc field beyond what SPEC.md specifies. Re-verified the code still matches that decision (no `error` property present) and left it as-is; the failure is still logged to `sync.log` and still surfaces via `Write-Host "ERROR: $filesError"` on the non-JSON table path, unchanged from Round 1.
- **Rejected** - "spurious backtick before colon" on `"$key`: $($counts[$key])"` (medium). Verified empirically in PowerShell 5.1: the backtick is a required escape, not a stray character. `"$key: $(...)"` (no backtick) fails to *parse* - "Variable reference is not valid. ':' was not followed by a valid variable name character" - because an unescaped `:` immediately after a variable name is tokenized as a scope-qualifier attempt (like `$env:`), not literal text; removing it would break the whole script at parse time, not just cosmetically. Confirmed the backtick-escaped form produces a clean `"Up-to-date: 5"` with no literal backtick byte (0x60) anywhere in the output. No change made.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Invoke-RollbackForRecord`'s success path swapped `fileId`/`version`/`previousFileId`/`previousVersion` and set `pinnedFileId`, but never touched `Record.fileName`, leaving it naming the file that was just replaced rather than the one actually restored (reproduced live: rolling project 911525 back from fileId 8787143/v9.2 to fileId 8672833/v9.1 left `addons.json` with `fileId 8672833` / `version "v9.1"` but `fileName` still `"Max_Camera_Distance-v9.2.zip"`, confirmed via both `/api/state` and `-Status -Json`). There is no `previousFileName` on the record to swap from - E1's record contract is closed to `previousFileId`/`previousVersion` only - so `Save-BackupZip` now also writes a small sidecar text file `ROOT\backups\<projectId>\<fileId>.filename.txt` next to every archived zip, holding that file's original CurseForge `fileName` (passed in from `Sync-SingleAddon`, which already has `Record.fileName` set to it by that point); the existing prune-to-2 step now prunes matching sidecars alongside their zips so the two never drift apart. `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` reads the restored fileId's sidecar and assigns it to `Record.fileName` in the same block as the other swapped fields; a missing/unreadable sidecar (e.g. a backup archived before this fix) degrades to leaving `fileName` exactly as it was rather than guessing or nulling it. Verified offline against a hand-built backups folder (no CurseForge access): a rollback with its sidecar present now restores the correct `fileName` alongside `fileId`/`version`; a rollback whose backup predates the sidecar leaves `fileName` unchanged and still reports `Rolled-back` with no crash; `Save-BackupZip` unit-verified to write the sidecar, prune an unrelated stale zip+sidecar pair, and keep exactly the two zip+sidecar pairs that matter (current + previous) after a second archive.

### ui component (app.js / style.css)

- **Fixed (high)** - the left sidebar wasn't pinned: `#app` used `min-height: 100vh` and `.main-col` had no `min-height: 0`, so the flex column stretched to fit its content instead of being capped, leaving `body` (not `.main`) as the real scroll container and scrolling the entire nav rail (brand, nav, Update & Play/Launch WoW, status line) off-screen along with the page. Changed `#app` to a hard `height: 100vh` and added `min-height: 0` to `.main-col` (style.css) so `.main`'s existing `overflow-y: auto` becomes the sole scroll container and the sidebar stays fixed in the flex row.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Components.Chip.forAddon`'s Pinned chip built its label as `"Pinned · v" + addon.version`, but `addon.version` already carries its own leading "v" (as shown elsewhere, e.g. the Installed column), doubling it into "Pinned · vv1.1.2". Dropped the literal "v" from the template (app.js).
- **Fixed (medium)** - `#btn-save-apikey`'s click handler chained `Actions.saveSettings({cfApiKey: raw}).then(...)` with no `.catch()`, and unlike the sibling install-by-ID/add-by-project-ID call sites, `saveSettings` deliberately re-throws after toasting its own error - so a failed key save (e.g. server unreachable) produced a genuine unhandled promise rejection in the console. Added a `.catch()` no-op (the error is already surfaced via `saveSettings`'s own toast) (app.js).
- **Fixed (medium)** - toasts were anchored `top: 16px; right: 16px`, landing directly on top of the topbar's rightmost buttons (e.g. Add addon) on every view since the topbar sits right at the top of `.main`'s padding. Moved `.toast-container` down to `top: 70px`, clearing the topbar's ~54px-tall button row (style.css).
- **Fixed (medium)** - the detail drawer's Versions tab table (7 columns incl. the per-row action button) was ~2-19px wider than the drawer's 640px content width with no `overflow-x` anywhere in the drawer to reach the overflow, silently clipping the rightmost "Pin this version"/"Install" button off the visible edge with no way to scroll to it. Dropped the standalone Size column and folded it into the Version cell as a small muted suffix (`.version-size`) instead, freeing enough width for all 6 remaining columns, action button included, to fit (app.js, style.css).
- **Fixed (medium)** - at the app's own documented 1000px minimum window width, the content area available to `.addon-table` (1000 - 232px sidebar - 48px `.main` padding = 720px) is narrower than the table's 900px min-width, so Latest/Updated stayed hidden behind a horizontal scroll by default even though the kebab column is pinned. Added a `@media (max-width: 1100px)` rule hiding the Updated column and shrinking `.addon-table`'s min-width from 900px to 680px to match, so the remaining columns fit without scrolling at the documented floor width (style.css).
- **Fixed (medium)** - the idle-state poller (`App.startIdlePolling`) called `/api/state` every 5s unconditionally, including while `markOnline` had already flagged the server unreachable and shown the offline banner - no backoff, so a genuinely-down server (e.g. exited on `-IdleMinutes`) got hit at the same 5s cadence as normal operation. Rewrote it from a fixed `setInterval` to a self-rescheduling `setTimeout` loop (`scheduleIdlePoll`) that reads `Store.state.online` each tick: 10s while offline, dropping straight back to 5s the moment a `reloadState` call succeeds and `markOnline(null)` flips it back (app.js). The banner-show/hide and auto-reconnect toast in `markOnline` were already correct and needed no change.
- **Rejected** - "unhandled promise rejection on `Actions.addByProjectId(...).then()` without `.catch()`" (app.js ~1972-1975, install-by-Project-ID form). This is the same call chain Round 1 already verified for the identical pattern elsewhere: `Actions.addByProjectId` → `Actions.startJob`, which wraps its only `await` (`Api.postJob`) in try/catch and always resolves to `true`/`false` - it never throws, so there is nothing for a missing `.catch()` to leave unhandled. Re-verified `startJob`'s current source directly; no change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "SPEC requirement not implemented: `pagehide` → `sendBeacon('/api/shutdown')`" (app.js ~2400-2407). Round 1 already fixed this exact bug and left the documented rationale in place: `pagehide` fires identically on a plain F5/reload/in-tab navigation as on an actual window close, with no reliable script-side way to tell them apart, so wiring the beacon back in (with or without a "no job running" guard - the guard doesn't distinguish reload from close either) would reintroduce killing the local backend mid-reload. The finding's own second option - "document as a design deviation in SPEC" - would mean editing SPEC.md, which is outside `ui/*` and outside this component-scoped round; the in-code comment block already carries that documentation. No change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "Settings > Game: add a game-version selector (Retail/Classic/etc.)" (medium). SPEC.md hardcodes `gameVersionTypeId=517` (Retail) into every CurseForge proxy endpoint (`/api/cf/search`, `/api/cf/mods/{id}/files`) and the `settings.json` schema has no field for it; a working selector needs a new persisted setting, changes to `addon-server.ps1`'s proxy handlers, and changes to `addon-sync.ps1`'s file-selection logic - three components beyond the `ui/*` files this round covers. No change made.

### E1 - Rollback / version history (addon-sync.ps1 / addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - `addons.json` records gain `previousFileId`/`previousVersion` (both null until an addon has been updated at least once), backfilled on load like `author`/`ignoreUpdates`/`pinnedFileId`/`releaseType` before them so an old record never trips a missing-property dot-assignment.
- **Added** - every successful install/update in `Sync-SingleAddon` now records what it replaced (`previousFileId`/`previousVersion`, set right before the record's `fileId`/`version` are overwritten - only on an actual update, not a fresh install) and archives the just-downloaded zip into `ROOT\backups\<projectId>\<fileId>.zip` via new function `Save-BackupZip`, which moves the file out of staging (not a copy - staging is wiped at end of run) and immediately prunes that project's backup folder down to at most 2 zips (the one just archived plus, when known, `previousFileId`'s), by exact fileId match rather than file age. Archiving is best-effort and never fails an otherwise-successful install.
- **Added** - new CLI param `-Rollback <id[]>` and worker function `Invoke-RollbackForRecord`: reinstalls a project from its locally archived previous-version zip (same `Install-AddonPackage` path as a normal install, zero network access), swaps fileId/version with previousFileId/previousVersion (so a second rollback undoes the first), and pins the restored fileId so a following plain sync does not immediately update it away again. Reports `Rolled-back` on success, `Failed` (with the reason logged to sync.log) when there is no recorded previous version or its backup zip is missing, `Skipped` for an unrecognized project id. `-DryRun -Rollback` makes zero disk writes and reports `Would-update` under action `"check"`, matching every other DryRun path in the script. A rollback-only run does not also trigger a full sync of every other addon.
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1` job kind `rollback` `{projectId}` -> `-Rollback id -Json`, wired into `Build-CliArgs`/`Handle-JobsPost` alongside the existing kinds; `Apply-JobCompletionSideEffects` clears the `updateAvailable` entry for a project on a successful `Rolled-back` row, same as it already does for `Updated`/`Installed`/`Pinned`. `previousFileId`/`previousVersion` reach `/api/state` for free through `Handle-State`'s existing generic per-record property clone - no server-side field mapping needed.
- **Added** - UI: a "Roll back to `<version>`" entry in the My Addons kebab menu (only rendered when `addon.previousFileId` is set - pushed conditionally rather than ternary'd, since a `null` array entry renders as a visible menu separator, not nothing), a muted "Previous" tag on the matching row in the drawer's Versions tab, and a tooltip ("Rolled back — unpin to resume updates.") on the Pinned chip when the pin resulted from a rollback (`pinnedFileId === previousFileId`). New `#icon-history` sprite entry; `Rolled-back` recognised everywhere `Would-update`/`Pinned`/etc. already were (status chip color, job panel summary/title, `Store.jobActingOn` busy-state matching). The dev-only `?mock=1` fake backend grew matching `previousFileId`/`previousVersion` bookkeeping and a fake `rollback` job kind so the feature can be exercised without the real server.
- **Verified offline** (no CurseForge access, per this round's constraints) against a hand-built throwaway folder: real zip archives extracted via `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile`, a addons.json with a rollback-eligible record, a no-previous record, and a record predating this expansion (missing the two new fields entirely, to exercise the backfill path). `-Rollback` on all four cases (eligible / no-previous / pre-expansion / unknown id) produced the expected `Rolled-back`/`Failed`/`Failed`/`Skipped` rows in a single `-Json` document, with the reinstalled folder's `.toc` "## Version" line matching the restored version and `addons.json` correctly swapped (fileId/version/previousFileId/previousVersion) and pinned. `-Rollback -DryRun` left the on-disk `.toc` and `addons.json` byte-for-byte unchanged. `Save-BackupZip` extracted verbatim and exercised in isolation across 3 sequential archives confirmed the prune-to-2 and move-not-copy behavior exactly.

### E3 - Dependencies (addon-sync.ps1 / addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - `addons.json` records gain `requiredDeps`/`optionalDeps` (both empty arrays until an addon has been installed/updated at least once under this build), backfilled like every other optional field before them so an old record never trips a missing-property dot-assignment.
- **Added** - `Sync-SingleAddon` now parses every installed folder's primary `.toc` for `## Dependencies:`/`## RequiredDeps:` (treated as synonyms) and `## OptionalDeps:` right after a successful install/update (new `Get-PackageDependencies`, built on new `Get-TocDependencies`/`Split-TocDepList`), excluding names that match one of the package's own folders and deduplicating case-insensitively; `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` performs the same recomputation from the restored folders so a rollback never leaves stale deps behind from the version it replaced.
- **Added** - `-Status` (`-Json` and the console table) and `addon-server.ps1`'s `/api/state` both add two live-computed fields per addon - `missingDeps`/`missingOptionalDeps` - by comparing the stored `requiredDeps`/`optionalDeps` against the current AddOns directory listing; neither is ever written back to `addons.json`. `-Status` degrades to "every requiredDep counts as missing" rather than requiring a resolvable AddOns path, preserving its long-standing no-path-required contract (unlike a real sync, which still exits 2 without one).
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1`'s `/api/open` gains a `'url'` target (`{what:'url', url}`) restricted to the `https://www.curseforge.com/` and `https://addons.wago.io/` prefixes, used by the drawer's no-key "Search CurseForge" fallback.
- **Added** - UI: My Addons rows show a "Missing: n" warning chip alongside the existing status chip when `missingDeps` is non-empty (the E7 filter chip of the same name now has real data to filter on instead of a permanent zero); the drawer's Overview tab lists Required/Optional dependencies with an installed/missing status dot and a "Search CurseForge" button on missing entries (switches to Browse pre-filled with a key configured, opens CurseForge's search page via the new `/api/open` target without one).
- **Fixed (high, self-caught during this expansion's own offline verification, before it ever reached the acceptance pass)** - the new `Get-AddonsFolderSet` (CLI) and `Get-PresentAddonFolders` (server) helpers both initially returned their `HashSet[string]` via a bare `return $set`. A `HashSet` is `IEnumerable`, so PowerShell's pipeline enumerates it on return like any other collection; for a set with one-or-more entries this happened to still produce something the caller could (mostly, coincidentally) use, but for an EMPTY set - exactly the case an unresolvable/missing AddOns path produces - it enumerates to zero pipeline objects, and the caller's assignment (e.g. `$statusPresentFolders = Get-AddonsFolderSet ...`) silently receives `$null` instead of the set. Reproduced directly: `-Status -Json` with no `-AddonsPath` at all (unresolvable) crashed `Get-MissingDeps`'s `Mandatory` `PresentFolders` parameter with "Cannot bind argument ... because it is null." This is the same hazard class SPEC.md's `List[object]`/`@()` quirk documents, generalized to any enumerable return value rather than just that one collection type. Fixed both helpers to return via `Write-Output -NoEnumerate`, matching every other collection-returning function already in this codebase; re-verified both the unresolvable-path case (every requiredDep now correctly reported missing, no crash) and the resolvable-path case (unaffected - `missingDeps: ["Foo"]` as expected) via `-Status -Json` and a real (test-port 47899) `addon-server.ps1` instance's `/api/state`.
- **Verified offline** (no CurseForge access, per this round's constraints): the toc-parsing pipeline (`Split-TocDepList`/`Get-TocDependencies`/`Get-PackageDependencies`) unit-tested in isolation against hand-built folders - comma- and space-separated values, the `RequiredDeps` synonym, case-insensitive tag matching, a folder with no matching tag, a nonexistent folder, self-package-folder exclusion, and case-insensitive cross-and-within-folder dedup all produced the expected lists. `-Status -Json`/`/api/state` verified against a hand-crafted record (`requiredDeps: ["LibStub","Foo"]`) and a scratch AddOns folder containing `LibStub` but not `Foo`, producing `missingDeps: ["Foo"]` exactly as the roadmap item's acceptance test describes, plus a pre-E3 record with neither field at all backfilling to `[]` without crashing. `-Rollback` against a hand-built backup zip confirmed `requiredDeps` is recomputed from the restored folder's `.toc` rather than left over from the replaced version. `/api/open {what:'url'}` verified 400 on a disallowed host, 400 on a missing `url`, 200 on both allowed prefixes (which, as a side effect of exercising the real `Open-InBrowser` code path, opened two Edge tabs to curseforge.com/wago.io search pages on this machine).

### E5 - Update digest and changelog access (ui)

- **Added** - the job results panel now shows a "What changed" link-button next to any `Updated`/`Installed` row (`Components.JobPanel.update`'s new `whatChangedButton`, wrapped in a `.job-result-name` flex group alongside the existing status chip) - `Actions.whatChanged(projectId, fileId)` opens the detail drawer on the Changelog tab pinned to that exact file when an API key is configured, or the Versions tab (already key-free, and already tags the installed file) when one isn't. `Components.Drawer.open` gained an `opts.changelogFileId` to carry that pin - the Changelog tab's existing `d.changelogFileId || d.files[0].id` fallback already honored it once files load, so no change was needed there.
