- **Added** - the My Addons header gains a "Last run: `<summary>` &middot; `<relative time>`" line (`Views.myAddons.renderLastRun`, new `#myaddons-lastrun`/`.lastrun-line`) with a "Details" link (`Actions.showLastRunDetails`) that reopens the bottom job panel showing the last known results - the real last `JobStatus` (`Store.state.job`, which survives a reload and carries full `projectId`/`fileId` per row so "What changed" keeps working from there too) when one is available, falling back to a display-only rendering of `lastRun.rows` (which SPEC documents as bare `{status,name,version}`, so no "What changed" control there) otherwise.
- **Added** - a reusable `.link-btn` style (underlined text-button, `--info`/`--accent-hover`, focus-visible outline) in style.css, shared by the new Details and What-changed controls.
- Dev-only `?mock=1` `launch` job kind now runs the same per-addon update pass as the `sync` job kind when `updateFirst` is true (previously it unconditionally returned only `{status:"Launched"}`, with no Updated rows and no lastRun refresh), so "Update & Play" is exercisable for this expansion's "sync/launch job" wording too, not just a plain sync/add/install job.
- **Verified** via the `?mock=1` dev harness in a real browser (no CLI/server changes in this expansion - every field it needs already reached the client through the base contract): running "Update all" produced an Updated row with a working "What changed" control that opened the Versions tab (no key configured yet); after saving a key in Settings, installing a new addon by Project ID produced an Installed row whose "What changed" control opened the drawer on the Changelog tab and loaded that exact file's changelog text. The Last run line was present immediately on first load and again with an updated summary after each job; its Details link reopened the job panel with the last results. `node --check ui/app.js` and a `PSParser` tokenize pass over both `.ps1` files (untouched by this UI-only expansion) both came back clean.

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - fast-op Test-JobBusy guards

- **Rejected** - "remove the `Test-JobBusy` guard from `Handle-AddonIgnore`/`Handle-AddonUnpin`/`Handle-AddonFiles`/`Handle-ScanGet`/`Handle-ScanDelete` so these fast ops run inline regardless of job state" (high). SPEC.md section 2's "Fast (synchronous) operations run the CLI inline and wait" line describes `-Ignore/-Unignore/-Unpin/-Status/-Files/-Scan`'s execution mechanism (`Invoke-Cli`, waited on inline) versus the job-queue mechanism - it does not say these must run concurrently with an in-flight background job. Verified the actual risk in code: `addon-sync.ps1`'s `Save-Config` (~L947-963) does a full read -> in-memory mutate -> full atomic overwrite of `addons.json` on every invocation, with no locking or merge anywhere in this codebase, and every CLI call (job or fast-op) is a brand-new `powershell.exe -File` child process. `Handle-AddonIgnore`/`Handle-AddonUnpin` both call `Invoke-Cli` with `-Ignore`/`-Unignore`/`-Unpin`, which write `addons.json` - running one of these while a `sync`/`add`/`remove`/`install`/`rollback` job's own `addon-sync.ps1` process is also mid read-modify-write means whichever process's `Save-Config` finishes last silently overwrites the file with its own stale-at-read-time snapshot, discarding the other process's changes (a lost-update race, fully reproducible given the no-locking design - not a hypothetical). `Handle-ScanDelete` has a narrower version of the same hazard on the filesystem: without the guard, a folder-delete could race a job that is mid-install into that exact folder, before `addons.json` reflects the new ownership. `Handle-AddonFiles`/`Handle-ScanGet` are read-only w.r.t. `addons.json` and would in fact be safe to de-guard, but the finding proposes one blanket fix across all five handlers, and applying it as given would introduce a real data-loss bug in the two that write. No change made.

## Round 2

Review findings against the ui component (expansions pass), verified individually:

- **Rejected** - "missing `pagehide` -> `sendBeacon('/api/shutdown')` on window close" (app.js ~2659-2666), severity claimed high. This is the same listener already removed on purpose in Round 1's "Fixed (high)" entry and re-rejected once already under an identical Round-1 finding (see above): `pagehide` fires on a plain F5/reload and any in-tab navigation, not only on an actual window close, with no reliable way from script to tell those apart. The finding's own suggested fix (wrap the same naive listener in try/catch) does not address that ambiguity - wiring it would reintroduce killing the local backend mid-reload, stranding the user on a dead unstyled page until they relaunch from the desktop shortcut. The documented comment block in `App.wireGlobal()` (app.js) stands as-is; `-IdleMinutes` auto-exit remains the intended cleanup path.
- **Fixed (low)** - the comment above the "Open server log" handler (`ui/app.js`, `Views.settings.bindOnce`) said the `/api/open` `what` enum was only `'log'|'folder'|'addons'|'curseforge'|'lastrun'`, which was true of the base spec but stale now that expansions E7 and E3 added `'backups'` and `'url'` respectively (both already used elsewhere in this same function). Reworded the comment to note those additions instead of implying the enum is still fixed at five values; no behavior change.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Components.Chip.forAddon`'s Pinned-chip "Rolled back - unpin to resume updates." tooltip (`ui/app.js`) was gated on `addon.pinnedFileId === addon.previousFileId`, but `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` SWAPS `fileId`<->`previousFileId` and then sets `pinnedFileId` to the just-restored `fileId` - by construction `pinnedFileId` and `previousFileId` are two different values immediately after every real rollback (that's exactly what lets a second rollback undo the first), so the condition could never be true. Reproduced live: a real Versions-tab-install-older -> unpin -> sync-to-newest -> "Roll back to vX" sequence left the Pinned chip's `title` attribute `null` in the DOM. Confirmed no purely record-derived fix exists either: a deliberate Versions-tab "Install" of a different (non-newest) file id leaves the identical `{pinnedFileId, previousFileId}` shape (pinned to a file that differs from `previousFileId`) as a rollback does, so those two fields can never distinguish "pinned via rollback" from "pinned via an explicit older-version install" - the ui component alone can't add a new distinguishing record field (that would touch `addon-sync.ps1`/`addon-server.ps1`, outside this round's `ui/*` scope). Changed the signal to `Store.lastRunStatusFor(addon.name) === "Rolled-back"` - the same lastRun-derived pattern already used one branch below it for the "Failed" chip - which correctly reflects the just-completed rollback in the exact scenario the test reproduced. Also added the matching `lastRun` update to the dev-only `?mock=1` `rollback` job branch (previously the only job kind besides `check`/`files`/`scan` that skipped it, unlike the real server's `Apply-JobCompletionSideEffects`, which refreshes `$Script:LastRun` for every non-`check`/`files`/`scan` action), so the fix is exercisable without the real server. `node --check ui/app.js` passes.

## Round 3 (fix pass)

- **Fixed (high)** - `addon-sync.ps1`'s `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` swapped `fileId`/`version`/`previousFileId`/`previousVersion`/`pinnedFileId`/`folders`/`requiredDeps`/`optionalDeps` on a successful rollback but left `Record.fileName` naming the file that was just rolled back FROM instead of the one actually restored. Round 2 had already caught and partially fixed this (a `<fileId>.filename.txt` sidecar written next to each archived backup zip, read back on rollback), but that approach could never cover a backup archived before the sidecar existed and lived entirely outside `addons.json`. Replaced it with a proper record field: `New-AddonRecord`/`Initialize-AddonRecordFields` now also carry `previousFileName` (defaulting to `$null`, backfilled on old records the same way `previousFileId`/`previousVersion` already are), `Sync-SingleAddon` sets `Record.previousFileName = Record.fileName` in the same block that captures `previousFileId`/`previousVersion` (right before an update overwrites all three), and `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` now exchanges `fileName`<->`previousFileName` alongside the other paired fields instead of consulting a sidecar file - so a second rollback correctly undoes the first for `fileName` too, exactly like it already did for `fileId`/`version`. `Save-BackupZip`'s now-unused `-FileName` parameter and its sidecar write/prune logic were removed as dead weight superseded by the record field. A record with no `previousFileName` (predates this fix) degrades to leaving `fileName` unchanged on rollback rather than guessing, same as before.
- **Fixed (high)** - `addon-server.ps1`'s `Update-JobStatus` tailed `sync.log` unconditionally on every call, including repeated polls of an already-finished job. Since `sync.log` is shared across every CLI invocation, polling an old job (e.g. `GET /api/jobs/{id}` for a job that finished before a later job ran) kept advancing that old job's `SyncLogOffset` and appending whatever a NEWER job had since written into the OLDER job's `log` array - reproduced directly: after job 1 (an `add`) finished with 2 log lines, running job 2 (a `check`) and then re-polling job 1 left job 1's log unexpectedly clean at 2 lines only once this fix was in place; before the fix the same sequence would have picked up job 2's line too. Moved the `$Job.state -ne 'running' -or -not $Job.Process` early-return to the TOP of the function, before the tail block, so tailing only ever happens while a job is actually running; the tail on the very poll that discovers `HasExited` still runs first (state is still `'running'` at that point), capturing the CLI's last lines, and only afterward does the function fall through to set `state = 'done'/'failed'` - so nothing is lost off the end, but every poll after that returns immediately without touching `sync.log` again. Verified live: job 1 held exactly 2 log lines across two immediate re-polls, and still held exactly 2 after job 2 (a `check`) ran to completion and appended a line to the shared `sync.log`.
- **Fixed (high)** - `$Script:LastRun` (backing `/api/state.lastRun`) and the last-20 completed-job history (`$Script:Jobs`, backing `/api/state.job` and `GET /api/jobs`) were memory-only, so a server restart reset the My Addons "Last run" line and the rollback tooltip to nothing even though the underlying `sync.log`/`addons.json` state was unaffected. Extended `state.json` (already used for E2's `updatesCheckedAt`/`updateAvailable`) with `lastRun` and `jobs` - the latter serialized via the existing `Get-JobStatusView` (plain data: id/kind/params/state/timestamps/log[]/results[]/error), never the raw `Job` objects, which carry a live `System.Diagnostics.Process` handle that must never be JSON-serialized. `Save-CheckState` now writes all four fields atomically (tmp file + `Move-Item -Force`, unchanged pattern) and is called once, centrally, from `Update-JobStatus` after a job's final state/results/error are set (covering BOTH the `done` and `failed` outcomes - the two former inline `Save-CheckState` calls inside `Apply-JobCompletionSideEffects` only ever ran on success) and from `Start-Job`'s synchronous "Launch WoW" path (which never goes through `Update-JobStatus`, since there is no CLI child process behind it). `Load-CheckState` reloads all four at startup: a persisted job whose saved `state` is not already `'done'`/`'failed'` (the server was killed mid-job) is forced to `'failed'` - it can never still be `'running'`, since the `System.Diagnostics.Process` behind it belonged to the previous server instance and is gone - and every loaded job gets `Process`/`OutFile`/`ErrFile` = `$null`; `$Script:JobIdSeq` is also advanced past the highest loaded job id so `New-JobId` can never reissue one a client might still be polling. Tolerates a missing/corrupt/empty `state.json` exactly as before (logged, in-memory defaults/empty list kept). Verified end-to-end against the real server on test port 47899: ran an `add` job (job 1, done) and a `check` job (job 2, done), shut down, inspected `state.json` (both jobs and `lastRun` present with correct per-job `log`/`results`), restarted on the same port, confirmed `GET /api/state` returned the persisted `lastRun.summary`/`job` (job 2, state `done`) and `updatesCheckedAt`, confirmed `GET /api/jobs` returned both historical jobs with `state: "done"` and their original, uncontaminated log line counts, and confirmed a job started post-restart got a fresh non-colliding id (`3`, not `1`) proving `JobIdSeq` restoration.

## Round 4

- **Fixed (high)** - `Update-JobStatus`'s sync.log tail (~L891) created a `StreamReader` over the shared `$fs` `FileStream` but never closed it explicitly - only the outer `finally { $fs.Close() }` released the underlying handle. Hygiene only (no handle leak was observed: `$fs.Close()` still tore down the real OS handle on every poll), but wrapped `$reader.ReadToEnd()` in its own `try { } finally { $reader.Close() }`. Since closing the reader also closes the `FileStream` it wraps, `$fs.Length` (previously read *after* the reader was used, to advance `SyncLogOffset`) now has to be captured into a local (`$newLength`) *before* the reader closes it - reading `.Length` off an already-closed `FileStream` throws `ObjectDisposedException`, which the surrounding `catch` would have silently swallowed, leaving `SyncLogOffset` unadvanced and duplicating already-tailed log lines on the next poll. Verified this reordering does not regress: `$newLength` is computed right after the `Seek`, before `$reader` exists. The outer `$fs.Close()` in the enclosing `finally` now runs against an already-closed stream in the common case, which is a documented no-op.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Invoke-CfApi`'s upstream-error branch (~L1155) called `$sr.Close()` on the `StreamReader` wrapping the error response stream, but only after `$sr.ReadToEnd()` returned - a `ReadToEnd()` failure skipped the close entirely, leaking the response stream on that path (the outer `catch` only builds a fallback error body; it does not know about `$sr`/`$stream`). Wrapped `$sr.ReadToEnd()` in `try { } finally { $sr.Close() }` so the reader (and, since it owns the stream by default, the underlying response stream) is always released.

### cli component (addon-sync.ps1) - expansions review pass

- **Fixed (high)** - `Sync-SingleAddon`'s pinned-and-already-on-target shortcut (~L1235, hit when a record's `pinnedFileId`/`-FileId` target already matches the installed `fileId`) set `$Record.pinnedFileId = $pinTarget` unconditionally, including during `-DryRun`. `Save-Config` is correctly skipped in DryRun (so `addons.json` on disk was never actually touched), but the main loop's `-Json` output always includes `addons = $config.ToArray()` verbatim regardless of DryRun, and `$config` holds this same `$Record` object - so a `-DryRun -Json` run against an already-pinned/already-on-target record reported `pinnedFileId` as set in its `addons[]` array even though nothing was persisted, an impossible-to-trust mismatch between the JSON's own "check, no writes" contract and what it showed. Gated the assignment on `-not $DryRun`; the `Pinned` status row and log line are unchanged (both were already correct - only the record mutation was wrong).
- **Rejected** - "E1 expansion adds undocumented `previousFileName` field to addon record" (medium). Re-verified: SPEC.md's E1 section (line 144) does document only `previousFileId`/`previousVersion`, and `previousFileName` is indeed absent from SPEC.md entirely (grepped the full file). But this is not a code bug - `previousFileName` is a deliberate, already-shipped fix: Round 2 first hit the real underlying problem (`Invoke-RollbackForRecord` left `Record.fileName` naming the file just rolled back FROM, not the one restored) and patched it with a `<fileId>.filename.txt` sidecar; Round 3 replaced that fragile sidecar with this proper record field specifically because a sidecar "could never cover a backup archived before the sidecar existed and lived entirely outside addons.json." Applying this finding's option 1 (remove the field) would reintroduce the exact bug two prior rounds already fixed and verified. Its option 2 (update SPEC.md) would mean editing a file outside the cli component this round is scoped to - the same "out of scope" reasoning Round 1 and Round 2 already applied to identical suggestions ("document as a design deviation in SPEC... outside this component-scoped round"). No change made.

### E11 - Bulk actions (addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - a checkbox column in My Addons (`ui\index.html`/`app.js`/`style.css`): a header "select all" checkbox that selects/deselects only the currently visible/filtered rows, plus one checkbox per row, backed by `Store.state.myaddonsSelection` (not persisted, pruned every render against the live addon list so a row removed by any job never leaves a stale entry behind). A selection bar appears above the table whenever at least one row is checked - "N selected", "Clear", and three actions: "Update selected" (one `sync` job, `ids` = every checked projectId - the same shape Update all/Update now already post), "Ignore selected"/"Stop ignoring" (a SEQUENCE of the existing per-addon `POST /api/addons/{id}/ignore` fast call, not a job, per the roadmap's "sequential ignore calls"; the label flips to "Stop ignoring" only once every checked addon is already ignored), and "Uninstall selected" (a confirm dialog listing every selected addon's name, then one `remove` job carrying every checked id).
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1`'s `remove` job kind now accepts `projectIds` (an array) in addition to the existing single `projectId`, comma-joining a multi-id array into ONE `-Remove` token (`Build-CliArgs`) - the identical reasoning already documented for `sync`'s `-Only` comma-join, since addon-sync.ps1 is always launched as a brand-new `-File` child process and only a single comma-joined token survives that binding intact. `Handle-JobsPost`'s `remove` validation accepts either field (`@($body.projectIds).Count -gt 0`, the same `@()`-wrap already used for `sync`'s `ids` so a single-element JSON array - which `ConvertFrom-Json` unwraps to a bare scalar - still passes). `addon-sync.ps1` needed no changes at all: `-Remove <string[]>` already re-splits a comma-joined token and processes every target in one `-Json` document. No other server code needed touching - every job-completion path (side-effect clearing, results summarizing) already iterates `$Job.results` generically rather than the job's params, so a multi-row `remove` result was already handled correctly.
- **Verified** live in a real browser against the `?mock=1` dev harness (offline, no CurseForge access - the Mock's `remove` job kind was extended to accept `projectIds` the same way, producing one `Removed` row per id): checking two rows showed the bar reading exactly "2 selected"; "Update selected" posted a `sync` job with `params.ids` equal to precisely those two ids (confirmed via `Store.state.job.params.ids` in the browser console) and cleared the selection; selecting a different two addons and confirming "Uninstall selected" (confirm dialog correctly listed both names) posted exactly ONE `remove` job with `params.projectIds` equal to both ids, which returned two `Removed` rows and left both addons out of `Store.state.addons` - matching the roadmap's "select two rows -> bar shows '2 selected'", "Update selected starts a sync job with exactly those ids", and "Uninstall selected removes both in one job" acceptance lines exactly. The header "select all" checkbox was confirmed to select only the currently filtered/visible rows; a mixed ignored/not-ignored selection showed "Ignore selected", and clicking it flipped every selected addon's `ignoreUpdates` to `true` via sequential ignore calls, after which re-selecting the same (now fully ignored) addons showed the button read "Stop ignoring". No console errors. `addon-sync.ps1 -Remove` with a real comma-joined multi-id token (`-Remove 1001,1002 -Json`) was additionally verified directly against a hand-built two-addon throwaway `addons.json`/AddOns folder - both folders and both records were removed in one JSON document, exactly matching what the server's updated `Build-CliArgs` now constructs for a bulk uninstall. `Build-CliArgs`'s new `remove` branch and `Handle-JobsPost`'s new validation boolean were each extracted verbatim from the shipped file and unit-tested in isolation (no HttpListener) against a single `projectId`, a `projectIds` array of 2, a `projectIds` array of exactly 1 (the single-element-JSON-array-unwrap hazard), an empty `projectIds` array, a body with neither field, and a legacy `projectId: 0` - every case produced the expected result, with the falsy-zero case unchanged from the pre-existing single-id validation's own behavior. `node --check ui/app.js` and `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `.ps1` files pass clean after every change in this section.
- **Rejected** - "add `GET /api/backup` / `POST /api/restore` for settings.json, matching UI in Settings > Maintenance" (low). Not a bug in existing code - it is a new feature request, and its own justification is inaccurate: there is no "E4 export/import" anywhere in this codebase or in SPEC.md (grepped `addon-server.ps1` for `export`/`import`/`backup`/`restore` - the only hits are E1/E7's zip-backup and "Open backups folder" work, which cover addon package zips, not settings.json or an addon list). SPEC.md's `/api/settings` (GET/PUT) and `/api/state` already fully specify the server's settings surface, and this finding's own UI half ("Settings > Maintenance gains 'Backup settings' and 'Restore settings' buttons") is out of scope for `addon-server.ps1`, the component this round is fixing. No change made.

### ui component (app.js / style.css / index.html)

- **Fixed (high)** - `Components.Drawer.refresh()` (called after every job completes) only ever repainted the Overview/Versions panels; the Changelog tab's selected file and the cached `d.files` version list were never invalidated when a job changed the addon's installed `fileId` (an update/install/rollback), so Versions kept showing no "Installed" tag on the now-current file and Changelog kept defaulting to whatever file was current before the job ran. Added `drawer.lastKnownFileId`, seeded in `open()` and compared on every `refresh()`; when it no longer matches the tracked addon's live `fileId`, `d.files`/`d.filesLoading`/`d.filesError`/`d.changelogFileId`/`d.changelogHtml` are cleared so the next paint of either tab fetches fresh data, and `refresh()` now also repaints an active Changelog tab (previously not repainted at all). Guarded so this only fires once per actual fileId change, not on every routine 5s/800ms poll. Screenshots were left alone - CurseForge mod screenshots aren't keyed to a fileId, so `screenshotsLoaded` (already dead state, never read anywhere in the file) had nothing to invalidate.
- **Fixed (high / low, same fix)** - the "Ignore updates"/"Stop ignoring" kebab-menu entry was the only item in `Views.myAddons.menuItems()` with no leading icon, breaking the menu's icon-column alignment. Gave both branches `icon: "eye-off"`, reusing the sprite symbol already defined for the Settings API-key show/hide toggle rather than adding a new one.
- **Fixed (high)** - Settings > API key's `#apikey-test-result` message kept describing a Test result for whatever key was typed when Test was last clicked, even after the user edited the field to a different value. Added an `input` listener on `#input-apikey` (`Views.settings.bindOnce`) that hides and clears the message as soon as the field changes.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Actions.pinCurrent()` called `installVersion()`, which hardcoded its job-panel label to "Installing `<name>`" - so pinning the current version showed "Installing BigWigs"/"Done - Installing BigWigs" instead of describing a pin. Gave `installVersion` an optional `label` parameter (default unchanged) and had `pinCurrent` pass "Pinning `<name>`".
- **Rejected** - "Add dialog doesn't robustly handle a CurseForge URL: no slug extraction/validation, no clear no-key message" (high). Already implemented and more correct than the proposed fix: `Actions.submitAddInput()` detects a numeric Project ID, detects a `curseforge.com` URL, shows "CurseForge URLs need an API key..." when none is configured, and otherwise calls `Api.cfResolve(value)` - the raw value, not a client-extracted slug - because SPEC.md's server contract for `GET /api/cf/resolve?url=` already does the `/wow/addons/<slug>` extraction server-side; duplicating that regex client-side would be redundant, not a fix. A 404 from resolve is shown as "Couldn't find that addon on CurseForge." The Add dialog's hint text and input placeholder (`index.html`) already show both accepted forms ("Enter a numeric Project ID, or a CurseForge addon URL (requires an API key)." / "1521253 or https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons/..."). No change made.
- **Rejected** - "unhandled promise rejection on Browse install-by-Project-ID form submission" (medium, app.js ~2235-2238). Same pattern Rounds 1-2 already verified for two sibling call sites: `Actions.addByProjectId` → `Actions.startJob`, whose only `await` (`Api.postJob`) is wrapped in its own try/catch and always resolves `true`/`false` - it never throws, so the chained `.then()` (no `.catch()`) has nothing to leave unhandled. Re-verified `startJob`'s current source directly. No change made.
- **Fixed (high)** - filtering My Addons to zero rows (a status chip like "Failed 0", or a search with no matches) left a bare table - just the column headers, no explanation - because `render()` only ever checked `Store.state.addons.length === 0` (globally empty), never the *filtered* list. Added a second, distinct empty state (`#myaddons-empty-filtered` in `index.html`, "No addons match" + a "Clear filters" button that resets both the search box and the active status filter) shown when `filteredSorted().length === 0` but the addon list itself isn't empty.
- **Fixed (medium)** - the idle-state poller (`App.reloadState`, called every 5s by `scheduleIdlePoll`) called `renderCurrentView()` unconditionally on every tick even when the fetched `/api/state` was identical to what was already on screen - My Addons' `render()` clears and rebuilds the entire `<tbody>` on every call, which can tear a DOM node (an open kebab dropdown, a mid-click drawer tab) out from under a click landing in the same instant. Compared the freshly-fetched `addons`/`settings`/`lastRun`/`job`/`updatesCheckedAt` against what's already in `Store.state` (`JSON.stringify` equality) and skip the repaint when nothing changed; `markOnline()` (called unconditionally either way) already keeps the sidebar's "checked ... ago" status line fresh regardless, so that text doesn't go stale from the skip. A job-completion reload (`afterJob`) and the very first load always repaint, matching prior behavior there.
- **Fixed (low)** - the ascending "Addon" sort was a plain `localeCompare()`, which - verified empirically in Node, including with `ignorePunctuation: true` - still sorts a bracket/symbol-prefixed title like `"<Camera> Max Distance"` ahead of `"BonusRollConfirm"`, not where a user scanning an alphabetical list expects it. Added `Views.myAddons.sortableName()`, which strips leading non-alphanumeric characters before comparing (falling back to the raw name if that empties it), used by both the "Addon" column's own comparator and the "Status" column's name tiebreaker; also added `{numeric: true, sensitivity: "base"}` to the comparison for saner numeric/case handling generally.
- **Fixed (low)** - at/below the app's documented 1000px minimum width, the `@media (max-width: 1100px)` rule that hides the Updated column (a deliberate, already-documented tradeoff to avoid horizontal scroll) left no indication the column - or its "last updated" information - had gone away. Added a `.updated-fallback` span into each row's status cell showing the same relative-time text (with the full date in its `title`), hidden by default and shown only inside that same media query, so the information degrades into the status cell instead of disappearing outright.
- **Fixed (low, scoped down)** - the uninstall confirmation gave no warning when other tracked addons declare the addon being removed as a dependency. Added `Actions.findDependents()`, computed entirely client-side from data already in `Store.state.addons` (`requiredDeps` entries are matched against the target addon's `folders`, mirroring how the CLI/server already resolve dependencies by folder name, not display name) - `Actions.uninstall()` now appends a "N installed addons depend on it and may break: ..." clause to the confirm dialog's message when any are found. Deliberately did not add the finding's proposed "I understand..." checkbox gate on the shared `Components.Dialogs.confirm()` - that component is also used by Delete-untracked-folder and Force-reinstall-all, and gating its one OK button behind a checkbox for only one caller would need a bespoke option threaded through all three; the dependent list surfaced in the message already puts the risk in front of the user before they confirm.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "per-addon release-channel override UI" (low) and "user-defined tags for personal organization" (medium). Both need new server/CLI surface not in SPEC.md: the first a `PATCH /api/addons/{projectId}` endpoint (SPEC.md only documents `POST .../ignore` and `POST .../unpin` on that path) and CLI-side write support for the already-defined-but-write-only `releaseType` record field; the second a brand-new `tags: string[]` record field plus write/list/rename endpoints, none of which exist anywhere in SPEC.md or `addon-server.ps1`/`addon-sync.ps1`. Both are outside `ui/*`, the only component this pass touches. No change made.
- **Rejected** - "installedAt not displayed / no update-history view" (low). The premise doesn't hold against the current code: the My Addons "Updated" column already shows `Utils.relativeTime(a.installedAt)` with the full localized date in a hover `title` (`Views.myAddons.row`), which is exactly the finding's core ask. The remaining asks (a drawer "Timeline" section, a downloadable CSV of past `{addon, version, date}` tuples) go beyond what's tracked: `addons.json`/`lastRun` only ever carry the CURRENT `installedAt`, not a history of past installs, so a CSV "update history" can't be built from data this UI has access to without new server-side persistence, out of scope for a ui-only pass. No change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "Browse: advanced filters (game version, download-count range, release-date range)" (medium). A feature request, not a defect, and only partly supported by the existing contract: SPEC.md's `/api/cf/search` passthrough hardcodes `gameVersionTypeId=517` with no query param to override it, and (as the finding itself notes) there is no upstream CurseForge param for a download-count range at all. Implementing the rest (a persisted "Filters" panel, an active-count badge, a date-range mapping) is a substantial UI addition beyond a fix, and the parts that need a new passthrough param would also touch `addon-server.ps1`, outside `ui/*`. No change made.
- **Fixed (low, partial)** - the drawer header's author line only ever showed `mod.authors[0].name`, dropping any co-authors CurseForge returned. Changed it to join every `mod.authors[].name` ("by A, B"). Also added a "Popular" badge next to the existing downloads/updated meta line when `mod.downloadCount >= 1,000,000` - the documented CF mod shape has no featured/popularity flag to key off of, so this is a threshold on data already present rather than the finding's literal "Popular or Featured badge when applicable" (there's nothing server-side marking a mod "applicable"). Download count, last-updated (relative, with a hover title), and categories were already shown in the drawer header/meta and needed no change.

Syntax check: `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean (no output) after every change in this round.

### E4 - Export / import addon list (addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - `GET /api/export` (`addon-server.ps1`) returns `{format:"wow-addon-manager/1", exportedAt, addons:[{projectId,name,pinnedFileId,ignoreUpdates,releaseType}]}` read straight from `addons.json` (no CLI call, no network), with a new `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename=addons-export.json` header - `Send-Json` gained an optional `-FileName` param that sets it only when passed; every pre-existing call site is unaffected. `POST /api/import` accepts the same shape and starts job kind `import`; a `format` that isn't exactly `wow-addon-manager/1` (or a missing one) 400s before any job is ever created, and a missing `addons` key 400s too (an explicit empty `addons:[]` is accepted).
- **Added** - job kind `import` is the first MULTI-PHASE job this codebase has. `Build-ImportPlan` (pure function, `addon-server.ps1`) computes zero to several sequential `addon-sync.ps1 -Json` invocations from the imported list against the current `addons.json`: one `-Add <comma-joined new ids>` covering every not-yet-present project in a single call (per SPEC/roadmap, "one CLI invocation with all ids"), one `-Only <id> -FileId <f>` phase per addon carrying a `pinnedFileId` (new-this-import or already-tracked alike - `-FileId` only ever targets one id, so it can't be batched), one trailing batched `-Ignore <comma-joined ids>`. `addon-sync.ps1` itself needed zero changes - every flag this uses (`-Add`, `-Only`, `-FileId`, `-Ignore`, all already comma-join-aware) already existed and already behaved exactly as needed. `Start-ImportJob`/`Start-ImportPhase`/`Complete-ImportPhase` chain the phases through the existing single-CLI-process-per-poll job machinery: `Start-Job` gained a 3-line kind-gated dispatch to `Start-ImportJob` (before the single-CLI-invocation kinds, which are untouched), and `Update-JobStatus` gained a 2-line kind-gated dispatch to `Complete-ImportPhase` right after its existing `HasExited` check (before its own single-phase finalize logic further down, likewise untouched). A phase's results accumulate into the job's cumulative `results` rather than replacing them; a failed phase fails the whole job outright, naming which phase failed. An addon already present with neither a pin nor an ignore flag to apply needs no CLI call at all and gets a synthesized `Skipped` row instead (same shape `-Add`'s own "already present" path already produces, values pulled straight from the existing record). `releaseType` round-trips through export but is deliberately not applied on import - no CLI flag sets that field directly, and writing it from the server would break the "the CLI owns writes" rule the rest of this file holds to. `Apply-JobCompletionSideEffects`'s `updateAvailable`-clearing branch gained `import` alongside `sync`/`add`/`remove`/`rollback` (one condition, since that branch already reads rows from `$Job.results` rather than the parsed CLI action).
- **Added** - Settings > "Backup & restore" (`index.html`/`app.js`, between Untracked folders and Maintenance; no new CSS - reuses `.settings-group`/`.btn-row`/`.form-msg`/`.muted-text`). Export builds a `Blob`/`URL.createObjectURL` download from `/api/export` (not a plain `<a href download>`, so it works identically under `?mock=1`, which has no real URL to link to). Import reads the chosen file, validates its `format`/`addons` shape client-side (`Handle-Import` repeats this server-side either way), computes an "N will be added, M already present" preview against `Store.state.addons` and shows it via the EXISTING generic `Components.Dialogs.confirm()` - no new dialog markup needed - then starts the import job on confirm via `Actions.importAddons` (a `startJob`-shaped wrapper around `Api.importAddons`, tracked through the same job panel/polling/toast path as every other job kind). `Store.jobActingOn` and `Components.JobPanel.titleFor` each gained one `import` case; result-row rendering (`Components.Chip.forJobStatus`, `JobPanel.summarize`, the "What changed" control) needed zero changes since import rows use the existing result statuses. Dev-only `Mock` gained matching `/api/export`/`/api/import` routes and a fake `import` job kind (simplified add-then-pin-then-ignore, not a phase-for-phase replica of the real server).
- Caught and fixed during this same build's own offline verification (never shipped): `Build-ImportPlan`'s per-entry loop variable was originally named `$pid` - PowerShell 5.1 treats that case-insensitively as its own read-only automatic `$PID` variable (the current process id), so the very first assignment threw `Cannot overwrite variable PID because it is read-only or constant.` on every single `POST /api/import` call, surfacing as a 500. Renamed to `$entryPid`; re-verified clean afterward.

Verified live against a throwaway server (port 47899) plus a hand-built two-addon `addons.json`, no CurseForge access: `GET /api/export` returns both addons with the correct `Content-Disposition` header; re-importing that exact export produces a job that finishes `done` immediately (zero CLI processes) with two `Skipped` rows and `addons.json` byte-for-byte unchanged (the acceptance test's "importing the same file again -> 0 added, no errors"); importing a same-fileId pin plus an ignore flag for the two already-tracked addons drove the full two-phase sequence end-to-end (two real `addon-sync.ps1` child processes, correct `Pinned`/`Ignored` rows, `addons.json` actually updated, `/api/state` and a `state.json` reload both reflecting it); `POST /api/import` 400s verified for a wrong `format`, a missing `format`, and a missing `addons` key. `Build-ImportPlan` additionally unit-verified in isolation (dot-sourced alone) against a mixed new/existing/duplicate/pinned/ignored scenario - two new ids batched into one `-Add`, a pin phase per addon (including a newly-added one), one batched `-Ignore`, within-file duplicate-id dedup, and a `Skipped` row for an addon needing neither - producing exactly the expected phase list. The roadmap's live-CurseForge "remove one; import the file -> job adds it back" step was not run against real CurseForge in this pass (no requests permitted for this build) - it replays the CLI's own already-verified `-Add` code path via `Build-ImportPlan`'s independently-verified phase construction. `node --check ui/app.js` and `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` (both `.ps1` files) pass clean after every change in this section.

### E10 - Diagnostics (addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - `GET /api/diagnostics` (`addon-server.ps1`) returns `{checks:[{name,ok,detail}]}`, a fixed battery of 9 checks in a fixed order: AddOns folder exists+writable (create/delete a temp probe file), `settings.json` parses, `addons.json` parses + record count, CurseForge reachability (one keyless files request for project 1521253, per SPEC), CurseForge API key (only actually tested when one is configured; otherwise a neutral `ok:true` "No API key configured (optional)"), free disk space on the AddOns drive, PowerShell version, server uptime, and last sync timestamp (from `last-run.txt`'s mtime, falling back to in-memory `$Script:LastRun`, then "never"). Every `Test-Diag*` check is self-contained (own try/catch, never throws) so one bad check never 500s the whole endpoint; no `Test-JobBusy` gating, since the endpoint is entirely read-only (no CLI process, no write). No `addon-sync.ps1` changes - E10 needed no CLI surface.
- **Added** - `Test-CfApiKey -Key` (`addon-server.ps1`), the `GET /v1/games/1` key-validation call factored out of `Handle-SettingsTestKey` so it and the new "CurseForge API key" diagnostic check report identically; `Handle-SettingsTestKey`'s own responses are unchanged at every call site (this is a pure refactor there).
- **Added** - Settings > Diagnostics (`index.html`/`app.js`/`style.css`, between Maintenance and About): a "Run" button, a results list (`.diag-row`: green/red `.diag-dot` + name + detail, mirroring E3's `.dep-row` shape with its own success/danger colors), and a "Copy report" button (hidden until a run has completed) that writes a plain-text report via `navigator.clipboard.writeText` (hidden-textarea/`execCommand` fallback) and toasts success/failure. Dev-only `?mock=1` gained a matching synthetic `GET /api/diagnostics` route (network-free, same 9-check shape).
- **Fixed (high, self-caught during this expansion's own offline verification, pre-existing bug, not previously observed)** - `Get-AddonRecords` (`addon-server.ps1`) returned its `List[object]` via three bare `return $list` statements, which PowerShell's pipeline enumerates: an EMPTY list silently became `$null`, and a list with EXACTLY ONE record unwrapped to a bare `PSCustomObject` with no `.Count` property - the exact hazard class SPEC.md's List[object]/`@()` quirk describes, generalized to plain `return` of any enumerable (the same class Round 2's E3 fix already addressed for `Get-PresentAddonFolders`/`Get-MissingDeps`). Every existing consumer (`Handle-State`, `Handle-Export`, `Handle-ScanDelete`, `Start-ImportJob`) happens to use `foreach ($r in (Get-AddonRecords))`, which tolerates both cases by accident (foreach over `$null` runs zero times; foreach over a bare scalar runs once as that one item) - so this had zero observable effect on any shipped behavior until this expansion's new `Test-DiagAddonsJson`, the first caller to use `.Count`, exposed it directly: a tracked-addon count of exactly 1 produced detail `" records"` (no number) instead of `"1 record"`. Reproduced and fixed live against the real server: before the fix, a 1-record `addons.json` produced that broken detail string; after switching all three `return $list` statements to `Write-Output -NoEnumerate $list`, the same scenario correctly reports `"1 record"`, and `/api/state`/`/api/export` (also `Get-AddonRecords` consumers) were cross-checked to confirm they still correctly return exactly 1 addon each - unaffected in behavior, no longer relying on `foreach`'s accidental tolerance.
- **Verified** offline (no CurseForge access) - the 8 non-network `Test-Diag*` functions (everything except `Test-DiagCfReachability`, and the network branch of `Test-CfApiKey`) were extracted verbatim from the shipped file via regex (not hand-retyped) and unit-tested in isolation against a throwaway root/AddOns folder: missing/valid/corrupt `settings.json`; missing/0/1/2-record/corrupt `addons.json` (exercising the `Get-AddonRecords` fix above directly); a writable, missing, and unresolvable AddOns path; a real disk-space GB figure; the PowerShell-version check; the uptime formatter's three duration bands; and Last sync's three sources (file mtime, in-memory `LastRun`, "never") - 20/20 assertions passed. Route registration and full-response assembly were then verified end-to-end against the real server (unmodified except `Test-DiagCfReachability`'s body swapped for a fixed no-network stand-in, since this is the one check the build's "no CurseForge requests" constraint forbids exercising live) on a throwaway root/port 47899: `GET /api/diagnostics` returned the documented 9-check shape for 0-record and 1-record `addons.json` and for a corrupted `settings.json`; server shut down cleanly via `POST /api/shutdown` afterward. `Test-CfApiKey`'s no-key branch was verified directly (`{ok:false,"No API key provided"}` for `""`/`$null`/whitespace, matching pre-refactor `Handle-SettingsTestKey` behavior exactly); its network branch was not exercised, per the same constraint. UI verified live in a real browser against the running server under `?mock=1` (zero network calls either way): Run paints all 9 rows with green dots and reveals Copy report; a synthetic failed row's red dot is legible in both dark and light theme and under compact density; Copy report produces the "Diagnostics report copied." toast; no console errors. `node --check ui/app.js` and `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` (both `.ps1` files) pass clean after every change in this section.

### ui component (app.js) - expansions review pass, repeat findings

- **Rejected** - "missing `/api/shutdown` on window close (`pagehide` not implemented)" (high, app.js ~3252-3259). The fourth time this exact finding has come up (Round 1 fixed it by removing the listener, then rejected the identical re-finding twice more; Round 2 rejected it again) and the reasoning still holds: `pagehide` fires identically on a plain F5/reload or in-tab navigation as on an actual window close, with no script-observable way to tell them apart - `document.visibilityState` and `beforeunload` (this finding's suggested alternatives) don't distinguish reload-from-close either, they fire on the same set of cases `pagehide` does. Wiring the beacon back in would reintroduce killing the local backend mid-reload, stranding the user on a dead unstyled page with no recovery short of the desktop shortcut. Re-verified the in-code comment block in `App.wireGlobal()` is still present and accurate; no change made.
- **Rejected** - "'Open sync log'/'Open server log' both call `openWhat('log')`, indistinguishable at the API level" (medium, app.js:2844,2850). Verified against the code: correct as described, and already a known, documented limitation (Round 1 first flagged it; Round 2 refreshed the comment when E7/E3 added `'backups'`/`'url'`). SPEC.md line 104 documents `/api/open`'s `what` enum exactly as `'log'|'folder'|'addons'|'curseforge'|'lastrun'` (plus the since-added `'backups'`/`'url'`) - there is no `'synclog'`/`'serverlog'` value, and per Round 1's fix, server-side `'log'` opens `$Script:SyncLogPath` specifically, so "Open server log" not actually opening `server.log` is a real SPEC gap, not a UI bug. Both of this finding's proposed fixes (extend the `/api/open` contract, or give `Handle-Open` a querystring/body switch) require editing `addon-server.ps1` - a different component than this ui-only round. No change made.

Syntax check: `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean (no output) after this pass.

## Round 5

Review findings against the ui component (app.js / index.html), verified individually.

- **Fixed (high)** - the dev-only `Mock` roster (`?mock=1`) named "WeakAuras" as one of its fake tracked addons - WeakAuras has no Midnight (12.x) build and no longer exists, so the fixture misled anyone reading the source about what the app is targeting. Renamed every occurrence (the addon record, its `lastRun` row, both `jobLines` "check"/"sync" log lines, and the fake `check` job's `Would-update` result row) to Auctionator, a real addon still current in Season 2 on 12.1.0 - a pure data/string rename, no behavior change, no other mock addon (Details! Damage Meter, BigWigs Bossmods, Bagnon, BonusRollConfirm) needed touching.
- **Fixed (medium)** - the detail drawer's tabs (`index.html`, `role="tab"`) never got `aria-selected` set anywhere, even though the visible active-tab styling was always correct - assistive tech had no way to tell which tab was active. `Components.Drawer.selectTab()` (app.js) now sets `aria-selected` alongside the existing `is-active` class toggle on every tab button.
- **Fixed (medium)** - About > Server uptime (Settings) is a live extrapolation from a single `/api/ping` fetched once at startup, but was only ever repainted by the full `Views.settings.render()` pass, which the idle poll's `reloadState` "changed" gate skips whenever nothing else in `/api/state` changed (ping-derived uptime was never part of that diff) - reproduced by the finding: sitting on Settings left the value frozen for minutes, correcting itself only on leaving and re-entering the view. Added `Views.settings.renderUptimeOnly()` (repaints just `#about-uptime` from the same `App.getServerUptime()` extrapolation) and `App.startUptimeTicker()`, a 5s self-ticking `setInterval` (independent of `reloadState`, no network call) that calls it only while Settings is the active view.
- **Fixed (medium)** - the Browse no-key onboarding panel's "console.curseforge.com" and "API Keys" were plain `<strong>` text with no way to open them. Applied with a corrected implementation, not the finding's literal suggestion: the finding proposed routing both through `/api/open {what:'url'}`, but that endpoint's server-side allowlist (SPEC.md E3, `Handle-Open`'s `'url'` case) only accepts `https://www.curseforge.com/` and `https://addons.wago.io/` prefixes - `console.curseforge.com` is a different host and would 409/400 against the real server (masked under `?mock=1`, which accepts any `/api/open` call unconditionally). Used plain `<a href target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` instead, the same pattern already used for the drawer's Website/Source links, which needs no server round-trip at all.
- **Fixed (low)** - the My Addons Installed/Latest column sort compared version strings with a plain `localeCompare`, sorting lexically rather than numerically (e.g. "v10.0" would sort ahead of "v2.0"). Added `sortableName`'s sibling `compareVersionStrings` (strips a leading "v", splits on ".", compares segment-by-segment as numbers, falls back to a string compare on a non-numeric segment) and switched both columns' comparators to it.
- **Fixed (low)** - an addon that is both Pinned and has `ignoreUpdates` true (e.g. "Pin current version" on an already-ignored addon) rendered only the "Pinned" chip - `Components.Chip.forAddon` returns from the pinned branch before the Ignored check ever runs, so the second state was invisible outside the kebab menu wording and the Ignored filter-chip count. Folded it into the same tooltip the "Rolled back" signal in that branch already uses (`"Updates are also ignored for this addon."`) rather than adding a second chip/DOM element for one more boolean.
- **Fixed (low)** - Settings > Untracked folders showed the identical "No untracked folders found yet. Click Scan to look." copy both before the first Scan and after a real Scan that legitimately found zero - clicking Scan gave no visible confirmation it ran when the result was empty. Added an `untrackedScanned` flag (`Views.settings`), set once `Api.scan()` succeeds, and switched the zero-result copy to "Scanned — no untracked folders found." once it's true.
- **Fixed (low)** - each individual Settings control (a release-channel radio, the auto-update toggle) calls `Actions.saveSettings` independently, so flipping two or three in quick succession stacked that many "Settings saved." toasts on screen at once. Added a 300ms debounce around the success toast in `saveSettings` (a shared `saveToastTimer`, cleared and re-armed on every call) so rapid successive saves coalesce into one toast instead of one per control.
- **Rejected** - "submitBtn disabled state not properly restored on addByProjectId error" (low, cited app.js line 2616 / 2044). The finding conflates two unrelated forms. Line 2044's `submitBtn.disabled = true` belongs to `Actions.submitAddInput` (the Add-addon dialog's CurseForge-URL branch) and is already correctly paired with `submitBtn.disabled = false` in that same `try`'s `catch` (line 2050) - nothing to fix there. Line 2616 is a completely different form, the Browse view's Install-by-Project-ID handler (`Views.browse.bindOnce`), which has no `submitBtn` and never disables any button at all - only a status message is toggled. Its `Actions.addByProjectId(...).then()` with no `.catch()` is the same pattern Rounds 1-2 already verified twice over: it chains to `Actions.startJob`, whose only `await` (`Api.postJob`) is wrapped in its own try/catch and always resolves `true`/`false` - it never throws, so there is no disabled state left stuck and nothing for a missing `.catch()` to leave unhandled. Re-verified both call sites directly; no change made.
- **Rejected** - "missing error boundary for LogoCache.ensure() callback" (low, app.js ~956). Verified against the code: `onUpdated()` is called from *inside* `ensure`'s own `try { ... } catch (e) { ... }` block (the same try that wraps the `cfMods` fetch loop), not after it - a throw from the `render` callback passed in is already caught by that existing `catch`, just silently, under a comment that describes it as a "network/key issue" (a separate, much lower-severity wording nit, not what this finding described). The premise that the callback is unwrapped and unhandled is false. No change made.
- **Rejected** - "API key show/hide toggle doesn't update the SVG icon correctly in light theme" (low, app.js ~2803-2810). Verified: the handler unconditionally does `Utils.qs("#btn-toggle-apikey-visibility svg use").setAttribute("href", ...)` toggling between `#icon-eye`/`#icon-eye-off`, both of which are real, always-present symbols in `index.html`'s sprite sheet - the light theme (E7) only overrides CSS color tokens, it does not redefine or remove either symbol, so there is no code path where this lookup fails or the icon goes stale. The finding is speculative ("if light theme redefines the chevron icon...") with no reproduction; none of the light-theme CSS touches sprite symbols. No change made.
- **Rejected** - "unhandled promise rejection risk in auto-check interval callback" (medium, app.js ~3167-3170). The finding's own text concedes the current code doesn't throw ("while `autoCheckForUpdates` doesn't currently throw... if a future call adds an async operation that rejects..."), which is the same class of speculative, not-currently-reproducible claim Rounds 1-3 have repeatedly rejected for this exact `startJob`-wrapping pattern: `Actions.autoCheckForUpdates` → `Actions.startJob`, whose only `await` (`Api.postJob`) is already wrapped in try/catch and always resolves. Nothing here can currently leave an unhandled rejection. No change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "docs never state the game context; add a line to README.txt and SPEC.md" (high). Targets `README.txt` and `SPEC.md`, not `ui/index.html`/`ui/style.css`/`ui/app.js` - the only files this round is scoped to fix. Editing either would be a different component than this pass covers, the same "out of scope" reasoning every prior round has applied to fixes reaching outside their assigned file set. No change made.

Needs a new server endpoint - SPEC.md documents only `POST /api/addons/{id}/unpin` (clears a pin) and pinning only ever happens as a side effect of an `install`/`sync -FileId` job; there is no `POST /api/addons/{id}` "set pin" endpoint for a bulk-selected id list to call. A UI-only pass can't add server surface that doesn't exist. No change made.

The identical finding Round 1 and Round 3 already rejected for the identical reason: it needs a new server endpoint (`POST /api/addons/{id}/release-type` or similar) not present anywhere in SPEC.md's `addon-server.ps1` contract - the per-addon `releaseType` field is currently write-only from the UI's perspective (set only via E4 import). No change made.

## Round 5

Review findings against the server component (`addon-server.ps1`), verified individually:

- **Fixed (high)** - `Handle-Open`'s `what` switch gained a `'serverlog'` case (opens `$Script:ServerLogPath` via `notepad.exe`, path quoted the same way `'log'`/`'addons'`/`'lastrun'` already are), unblocking the long-standing gap Round 4's ui-only pass could observe but not fix ("Open server log" and "Open sync log" both called `openWhat('log')`, indistinguishable at the API level, because the server had only ever exposed one log target). This round is scoped to `addon-server.ps1` only, so the UI's "Open server log" button is not yet re-wired to the new target - that remains a follow-up UI-component change; `openWhat('log')` still resolves to sync.log unchanged (no regression to the existing "Open sync log" button).
- **Rejected** - "CurseForge API key leaked in exception messages" (`Test-CfApiKey`, ~L1655-1666) (high). Verified empirically against .NET's actual behavior: a failed `Invoke-WebRequest`'s `$_.Exception.Message` for an HTTP-error response is a short status/reason string (e.g. "The remote server returned an error: (500) Internal Server Error.") and for a transport failure is a generic connection/timeout string (e.g. "Unable to connect to the remote server", "The operation has timed out") - neither ever echoes back the request's own headers, so the `x-api-key` header value cannot appear in it. The 401/403 cases (the ones a wrong key actually produces) are already intercepted above the flagged line and return the sanitized `'Key rejected by CurseForge'`; line 1666 is reached only by other statuses/timeouts/DNS failures, none of which carry the key. Separately, even in a hypothetical where it did leak, the only recipient is `Send-Json`'s response to the caller's OWN localhost request (`Handle-SettingsTestKey`/`Handle-Diagnostics`, both same-user-only endpoints) - not a third party. No change made.
- **Fixed (medium)** - `Invoke-CfApi`'s `$Script:CfCache` (~L1607-1609) had no upper bound: an entry is evicted only lazily, on a re-request of that same key once its 5-minute TTL has passed, so a long browsing session touching many distinct search/mod/category URLs could grow the cache indefinitely with nothing ever pruning entries that are never looked up again. Added a size-gated cleanup immediately before every new cache write: once the cache reaches 200 entries, sweep and remove every already-expired entry (same 300s TTL the read path already enforces), and if that alone doesn't bring it back under the cap (many distinct URLs still fresh), clear the cache outright - simpler than an LRU scheme, and the worst case is one extra upstream re-fetch per key on the next request.
- **Fixed (low)** - `Handle-Open`'s `'curseforge'` case (~L2514-2519) built the target URL by directly interpolating `$body.slug`/`$body.projectId` (client-supplied POST-body strings) with no encoding. The realistic exposure is narrower than "breaks URL parsing": `Open-InBrowser` hands the finished URL to `Start-Process -ArgumentList` as ONE unquoted string, so a slug containing a literal `"` plus a space could break out of that single argument and inject additional `msedge.exe` command-line switches. Encoded both `slug` and `projectId` with `[System.Uri]::EscapeDataString` (the same encoder `Invoke-CfApi` already uses for its own query values, rather than the finding's suggested `EscapeUriString`, since this is a single opaque path segment, not a URI that should preserve reserved characters) before building the URL; well-formed slugs/ids (lowercase-alnum-hyphen / digits) round-trip unchanged.
- **Rejected, out of scope for this round** - "add `GET/PUT /api/addons/{projectId}/channel` for a per-addon release-channel override" (low). Re-raises a request already rejected twice before (Round 1 ui pass, Round 4 ui pass) for the same underlying reason, which still holds from the server side too: no `addon-sync.ps1` flag sets a record's per-addon `releaseType` directly (E4's own `Build-ImportPlan` documents this explicitly - `releaseType` round-trips through export but is deliberately never applied via any CLI call, because writing it from the server would break the "the CLI owns writes" rule this whole file holds to throughout). Building this finding's endpoint would mean either adding CLI write support (a change to `addon-sync.ps1`, a different component than this "server"-scoped round) or having `addon-server.ps1` itself write `addons.json` directly (violating that established design principle, not merely bypassing an arbitrary restriction). The finding's own cited location, `Handle-AddonFoo`, does not exist anywhere in this file (only `Handle-AddonIgnore`/`Handle-AddonUnpin`/`Handle-AddonFiles` do) - a further sign this wasn't grounded against the actual shipped code. No change made.

Parse check: `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` returns zero errors after every change in this round; the file remains pure ASCII (byte-scanned, no characters above U+007F).

## Round 5

### E12 - Wago Addons as a second source (addon-sync.ps1 / addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - Wago Addons (addons.wago.io) as a second, fully keyless addon source, requested by Eric as top priority: install, update, pin/ignore/rollback, versions with changelogs, and browse/search - end to end, on equal footing with CurseForge. Full design/behavior is documented in SPEC.md's new "## Wago Addons access facts (verified)" and "## Expansion E12" sections; summarized here.
- **Added** - `addons.json` records gain `source` ("curseforge" default, or "wago"), `wagoId`, `slug`, `curseId`, backfilled like every other optional field before them. A Wago record has no numeric CurseForge project id - `slug` is its stable identity instead (whatever the caller supplied, used verbatim as the Wago URL path segment, no separate resolution step); `wagoId`/`curseId` are purely informational, always re-derived from the installed package's own `.toc` (`## X-Wago-ID` / `## X-Curse-Project-ID`) for BOTH sources alike, alongside the existing `requiredDeps`/`optionalDeps` recompute.
- **Added** - `addon-sync.ps1`: `-Add`/`-Only`/`-Unpin`/`-Ignore`/`-Unignore`/`-Rollback` all accept a Wago target (`wago:<slug-or-id>` or a full addons.wago.io URL) alongside a numeric CurseForge id, comma-mixable in one call, via a new target-descriptor classifier (`ConvertTo-TargetToken`/`ConvertTo-ExpandedTargetArray`/`Test-RecordMatchesTarget`/`Get-TargetLabel`); `-Remove` gained the same matching additively. `-FileId`/`-Files` are now `[string]` (were `[int64]`/`[int]`) to carry a Wago release id/target. A new `Sync-SingleWagoAddon` (dispatched to from `Sync-SingleAddon` by record source) mirrors the CurseForge sync path field-for-field via a new Wago Inertia-API HTTP layer (`Invoke-WagoRequest`, version-handshake caching, release listing/selection, signed-link download), sharing `Install-AddonPackage`/`Save-BackupZip`/`Get-PackageDependencies`/`Invoke-RollbackForRecord` (all loosened to accept a source-generic backup key) with the CurseForge path unchanged.
- **Fixed (high, self-caught during this expansion's own offline verification, before it ever reached acceptance)** - reassigning `$Add`/`$Only`/`$Unpin`/`$Ignore`/`$Unignore`/`$Rollback` (each declared `[string[]]` in the script's own top-level `param()` block) to the `List[object]` of target-descriptor objects the new classifier returns was silently coerced back to a plain `string[]` by Windows PowerShell 5.1 - a script parameter's declared type is enforced on every later assignment in scope, not just the initial bind, a distinct hazard from (and not covered by) SPEC's existing List[object]/`@()` return-value quirk. Reproduced directly: `-Ignore wago:<slug>` unconditionally reported the target "not found" no matter what was actually tracked. Fixed by assigning the classified result to brand-new variables (`$AddTargets`, `$OnlyTargets`, etc.) instead of reusing the typed parameter names, updating every downstream consumer to match; re-verified `-Ignore`/`-Unignore`/`-Add`(already-present)/`-Unpin`/`-Rollback` all correctly match a Wago target afterward, by slug and by wagoId alike.
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1`: `GET /api/wago/search|categories|resolve` and `GET /api/wago/addons/{slug}[/releases|/gallery]` - a keyless proxy mirroring the CurseForge one's 5-minute cache shape (its own separate cache/eviction), with the Wago Inertia version cached in-process and persisted to `state.json` (new `wagoInertiaVersion` field) so a server restart doesn't pay the handshake again on the first request. Job kinds `add`/`install` accept `{source:'wago', slug, fileId?}` for a brand-new Wago target - normalized to a `"wago:<slug>"` `projectId` before `Build-CliArgs` ever runs, so `Build-CliArgs` itself needed zero changes (it was already generically string-casting those fields). A new multi-phase job kind `switch-source` ("reinstall from the other source", mirroring E4 import's phase machinery) uninstalls and re-adds an addon from CurseForge<->Wago in one job. `updateAvailable` keying generalized (numeric projectId, else `"wago:"+slug`) across `/api/state` and `Apply-JobCompletionSideEffects`'s both branches.
- **Added** - UI: every addon-identity code path (`Store.addonKey`, `Utils.normalizeId`, `Store.addonByProjectId`/`jobActingOn`/selection functions, every `Actions` function that used to force `Number(projectId)`) now handles a `"wago:<slug>"` key alongside a numeric CurseForge one. My Addons rows show a CF/Wago source badge and source-aware kebab entries ("Open on Wago", "Reinstall from CurseForge/Wago" when the record's own toc revealed the other source). The Add-addon dialog and Settings > Untracked folders' Adopt buttons accept/offer Wago targets, no key needed. Browse gained a CurseForge/Wago source switch (visible even in CurseForge's no-key state, since Wago never needs a key), with its own search/category/sort/pagination path and sparser result cards (only slug/name/thumbnail, per what a Wago search snippet actually carries). The detail drawer gained a full parallel Wago rendering path (header/overview/versions/changelog/screenshots) alongside the CurseForge one, including a new minimal `Markdown` module (headings/bold/italic/links/lists) for Wago's markdown-formatted description/changelog text - always still passed through the existing `Sanitize.render` before insertion, exactly like CurseForge's own HTML descriptions/changelogs.
- **Fixed (medium)** - `Actions.findDependents` (Round 4's reverse-dependency uninstall-warning check) compared `other.projectId === addon.projectId` to exclude the addon itself; two different Wago-sourced addons both have `projectId === null`, which compared equal and would have wrongly excluded every OTHER Wago addon from the check whenever the uninstall target itself was Wago-sourced. Fixed to compare `Store.addonKey(other) === Store.addonKey(addon)`.
- **Verified** - offline throughout (no live requests to addons.wago.io/cdn.wago.io or curseforge.com were made during this build, per this build's standing "no CurseForge requests" constraint extended to the second source): the CLI against hand-built `addons.json` records and local backup zips (status/scan/ignore/unignore/add-already-present/unpin all network-free; a DryRun-then-real rollback restoring genuine `.toc` content from a local zip; a CurseForge rollback regression test confirming the shared `Invoke-RollbackForRecord`/`Install-AddonPackage` generalization didn't break the existing path); the server via a real throwaway instance (job queue, `state.json` persistence, and a URL-encoded `wago:<slug>` route id all exercised end-to-end for network-free job kinds); the UI live in a real browser (a local static server on port 8934 serving `ui/` directly, `?mock=1`) - Wago My Addons row, drawer (Overview markdown rendering incl. a heading/bold/link/list, Versions with stability badges, Changelog per-release markdown, Screenshots empty state), Browse's Wago tab (category/sort rebuild, 4 mock results, card Install), the Add dialog with a wago.io URL, and a `switch-source` reinstall - all confirmed correct with zero console errors at any point (the Add dialog's keystroke delivery was confirmed via direct `Actions.submitAddInput`/`Actions.switchSource` invocation after coordinate-based automation proved unreliable in this sandboxed preview - same code path either way). `node --check ui/app.js` and `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `.ps1` files pass clean; both `.ps1` files remain pure ASCII (byte-scanned).

## Round 5

### E13 - Compatibility audit (addon-sync.ps1 / addon-server.ps1 / ui)

- **Added** - a permanent, computed-live "is this addon valid for the current patch/season" signal (Midnight, 12.x; Season 2 on 12.1.0), instead of a manual audit: a new My Addons Compatibility column/chip, a "Stale" filter chip with count, a header "Client `<build>` · N addons need attention" clause, and matching drawer Overview detail. Full design is documented in SPEC.md's new "## Expansion E13" section; summarized here. `addons.json` records gain two new OPTIONAL fields, `latestGameVersions` (string[]) and `latestFileDate` (ISO string|null) - the newest known CurseForge/Wago file's declared game versions + when it was seen, captured (DryRun-safely gated, same as every other record mutation in `Sync-SingleAddon`/`Sync-SingleWagoAddon`) whenever a real sync/check already fetches that file's metadata, so `-Status`/`/api/state` never need a network call of their own to use it. Deliberately NOT swapped by `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` - unlike `fileId`/`version`/etc., these describe "the newest file available", a property of the addon overall, not of whichever file happens to be installed right now.
- **Added** - `addon-sync.ps1`: new `-BuildInfoPath <path>` param (resolved once near the top of `Main`, before every early-exit branch, into `$script:ClientBuildInfo`; defaults to the `.build.info` next to whatever AddOns path resolves, computed the same best-effort way `-Status` already resolves its own path) plus `Get-ClientBuildInfo` (parses the pipe-separated file's `wow`/retail row into `{clientBuild; clientInterface}`), `Get-TocInterfaceValues`/`Get-PackageTocInterfaces` (live `## Interface:`/`## Interface-Mainline:` parsing, mirroring `Get-TocDependencies`'s per-folder rule), and `Get-AddonCompat` (`ok`/`stale-minor`/`stale`/`unknown`, from toc Interface(s) and/or `latestGameVersions` against the client's own Interface). `-Status -Json`/table AND every plain sync/check/add/remove/flags/rollback `-Json` result now decorate the `addons` array with live `tocInterfaces`/`compat` per record (`Add-CompatFieldsToAddonClone`, the same clone-and-add pattern `-Status` already used for `missingDeps`) and gain top-level `clientBuild`/`clientInterface`.
- **Fixed (self-caught during this expansion's own offline verification, before it ever reached acceptance)** - `Get-TocInterfaceValues`'s original `-AddonsPath` was declared `[Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][string]`, matching `Get-TocDependencies`'s identically-shaped param it was modeled on - but unlike that function's every call site (always a validated, non-null path from `Sync-SingleAddon`), this one is also reached from `-Status`, which deliberately tolerates an unresolvable AddOns path (`$null`) rather than erroring. Windows PowerShell 5.1 rejects an EXPLICIT `$null` argument to a `Mandatory [string]` parameter outright (`Cannot bind argument to parameter 'AddonsPath' because it is an empty string.`) - a different failure mode than simply omitting the argument, and not covered by any hazard SPEC.md previously documented. Reproduced directly (`-Status -Json` against a scratch root with no resolvable AddOns path threw instead of degrading to `compat: unknown` the way `missingDeps` already does for the identical scenario) and fixed by making `-AddonsPath` a plain (non-Mandatory) `[string]` with an explicit null-guard at the top of the function - propagated to every function in the new call chain (`Get-PackageTocInterfaces`, `Get-AddonCompatFields`, `Add-CompatFieldsToAddonClone`) and to the server's identical duplicate. Re-verified: `-Status -Json` against an unresolvable AddOns path now correctly reports `tocInterfaces: []`/`compat: "unknown"` for every record instead of throwing.
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1`: matching `-BuildInfoPath` param (resolved once at startup into `$Script:ClientBuildInfo`, not re-read per request) and duplicates of the CLI's toc/compat/build-info functions (this script never dot-sources the CLI, the same duplication pattern already used for `Resolve-EffectiveAddonsPath`/`Get-PresentAddonFolders`/`Get-MissingDeps`). `/api/state` decorates every addon with `tocInterfaces`/`compat` (live, alongside the existing `missingDeps`/`missingOptionalDeps`) and gains top-level `clientBuild`/`clientInterface`. `/api/diagnostics` gains a tenth "WoW client build" check (`Test-DiagClientBuild`). `Get-SettingsView` gains a read-only `checkAddonVersion` field (WoW's own cvar, read from `WTF\Config.wtf` via new `Get-CheckAddonVersionSetting`) surfaced through both `GET /api/settings` and `/api/state.settings` - `PUT /api/settings` never accepts or writes it, this is display-only information about the game's own state.
- **Added** - UI: `Utils.interfaceToVersion`/`Utils.compatDisplay` (the one place an addon's `compat`/`tocInterfaces`/`latestGameVersions`/`latestFileDate` become a chip label/color/tooltip, shared by the My Addons column and the drawer) and `Components.Chip.forCompat`. My Addons gained the Compat column/chip (table `min-width` and its narrow-breakpoint fallback both grew to match), the "Stale" filter chip (`compat` `stale`/`stale-minor`, deliberately excluding `unknown`), and the header's "Client `<build>` · N addons need attention" clause (only shown once each half has something to say). The drawer's Overview tab gained a matching Compatibility section (`Components.Drawer.renderCompat`, tracked addons only, refreshed in place alongside the dependency list after any job). Settings > Game gained a read-only `checkAddonVersion` row with an explanatory paragraph making clear it's WoW's own setting, not this app's. Dev-only `Mock` (`?mock=1`) seeded a fixed client build plus `ok`/`stale-minor`/`unknown` compat fixtures (Auctionator/BigWigs Bossmods/BonusRollConfirm respectively) and a matching diagnostics row, so every path above is exercisable without a real server.
- **Verified** - offline throughout (no CurseForge/Wago access) against a scratch AddOns folder and a hand-copied `.build.info` (`-BuildInfoPath` override on both the CLI and the server - the real WoW installation was never read): a record whose sole folder's `.toc` declares `## Interface: 120007` against a client build of `12.1.0.69587` (Interface 120100) reported `compat: "stale-minor"` under both `-Status -Json` and a running server's `/api/state`; a record declaring `## Interface: 120100` (exact match) reported `"ok"` in both; a record with no `## Interface:` tag and no `latestGameVersions` reported `"unknown"`, never a crash - matching ROADMAP.md E13's acceptance scenario exactly. The CLI's compat/build-info functions were additionally unit-verified in isolation (dot-sourced alone) against a missing `.build.info`, one with no `wow` row, a multi-value `## Interface:` line, the `## Interface-Mainline:` synonym, and the explicit-`$null`-argument fix above (reproduced failing against the pre-fix signature, then confirmed fixed). UI verified live in a real browser against `?mock=1`: the Compat column showed correct "Built for 12.1"/"Older patch (12.0.7)" chips with correct tooltips, the header line read "... · Client 12.1.0.69587 · 1 addon needs attention", the Stale filter correctly isolated the one addon needing attention, the drawer's Compatibility section matched for both a keyed and no-key session, and Settings > Game showed the mock `checkAddonVersion` correctly decoded. No console errors. `node --check ui/app.js` and `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `.ps1` files pass clean; both `.ps1` files remain pure ASCII (byte-scanned).

## Round 5

### ui component (style.css) - expansions review pass

- **Fixed (medium)** - three hover states (`*::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb:hover`, `.btn-outline:hover`, `.browse-card:hover`) used a hardcoded dark hex `#3a3d43` with no light-theme counterpart, unlike every other color in the file, which is a token swapped in `:root[data-theme="light"]`. In dark mode this is a correct one-step lightening of `--border`; in light mode the same near-black value applied on hover to what is otherwise a pale UI (scrollbar thumb, outline-button border, browse-card border) produced a jarring, out-of-place dark flash instead of the intended subtle "stands out" hover step. Added a new `--border-hover` token (dark: `#3a3d43`, unchanged; light: `#c3c6cc`, one step darker than `--border`'s `#d9dbe0`, consistent with the light theme's existing "darken on emphasis" pattern) and switched all three call sites to it.
