### server component (addon-server.ps1) - expansions review pass

- **Fixed (medium)** - `Save-CheckState` wrote `state.json` via `ConvertTo-Json -InputObject $body -Depth 12`, but SPEC.md's E2 section documents the required call exactly as "`-InputObject` not piped, `-Depth 8` - same atomic-write pattern as `Save-Settings`" (the "same pattern" clause refers to the atomic tmp-file-plus-`Move-Item` mechanics, which were already correct - not to `Save-Settings`'s own `-Depth 5`, a different literal value for a differently-shaped object). Changed to `-Depth 8`, matching the spec text verbatim; `state.json`'s actual shape (`jobs[]` of flat `Get-JobStatusView` records, `updateAvailable` map, `lastRun`) nests nowhere near 8 levels, so nothing is truncated by the lower depth.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Handle-JobsPost`'s `switch-source` validation checked only that `toSource` was present (truthy), not that its value was one of the two the job actually understands. `Start-SwitchSourceJob` (~L1639-1641) branches `if ($toSource -eq 'wago') { 'wago:' + $toTargetRaw } else { $toTargetRaw }` - any other value (a typo, a stray value from a future third source, garbage) fell into the `else` branch and was silently treated as a bare CurseForge target, so `POST /api/jobs {kind:'switch-source', toSource:'bogus', toTarget:12345}` would reinstall addon 12345 from CurseForge with no error surfaced anywhere, rather than rejecting the request. Added a value check (`([string]$body.toSource).ToLowerInvariant() -notin @('wago', 'curseforge')` -> 400) alongside the existing presence check, matching the case-insensitive comparison `Start-SwitchSourceJob` itself already performs.
- **Rejected** - "forward `-BuildInfoPath` to every `addon-sync.ps1` CLI invocation (`New-CliProcessArgs`), captured into a `$Script:BuildInfoPathOverride`" (high). Verified there is no `$Script:BuildInfoPathOverride` variable in the codebase at all - the actual startup code (~L3645/3677-3679) resolves `$Script:ClientBuildInfo` directly from the `-BuildInfoPath` param (or the computed default) ONCE at server startup, exactly as SPEC.md's E13 section specifies ("resolved once at startup... NOT re-read on every request... duplicated rather than dot-sourcing the CLI"). Traced every consumer of a completed CLI job's parsed JSON output (`Update-JobStatus`, ~L1879-1896): only `$parsed.results` is ever read - `$parsed.addons` and `$parsed.clientBuild`/`clientInterface` (the fields the CLI's own `-BuildInfoPath`-driven compat computation would populate) are parsed and immediately discarded, never stored, never forwarded to any API response. `/api/state`'s `tocInterfaces`/`compat`/`clientBuild`/`clientInterface` (`Handle-State`, ~L2776-2795) are computed entirely from the server's own independently-resolved `$Script:ClientBuildInfo` and a fresh per-record `.toc` parse - never from a job's output. Forwarding `-BuildInfoPath` to the child CLI process would therefore change nothing observable through any API or UI surface; SPEC.md's E13 `addon-server.ps1` section (which enumerates every required server-side change for this expansion) does not call for it either. No change made.

Parse check: `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` returns zero errors after this round; the file remains pure ASCII (byte-scanned, no characters above U+007F).

## Round 5

### ui component (style.css) - horizontal-scroll regression fix

- **Fixed (high)** - at the app's documented 1000px minimum window width, the My Addons table itself scrolled horizontally inside `.table-scroll`, even though the E13 narrow-breakpoint rule (`@media (max-width: 1100px)`) claimed a `min-width` of 810px that was supposed to fit the 720px content area (1000 - 232px sidebar - 48px `.main` padding). The real bug was two-fold: (1) the 810px figure was itself still 90px over the 720px budget - arithmetic that was never actually checked against a live render; and (2) `.addon-table` used the default `table-layout: auto`, under which the Addon-name column (`.col-addon`, never given a width anywhere in this file) simply grew to fit its content - so the table's *real* rendered width measured 836px, 26px past even the wrong 810px figure, not the 810px the comment promised. Fixed both: added `table-layout: fixed` so every column width in the file is now authoritative rather than a hint auto-layout can override, gave `.col-addon` an explicit narrow-breakpoint width (190px) so fixed layout has a number to enforce, and shrank `.col-version`/`.col-status`/`.col-compat` at that breakpoint (110/150/150px -> 78/128/140px) so the full column budget (checkbox 34 + name 190 + installed 78 + latest 78 + status 128 + compat 140 + menu 44 = 692px) lands with a real ~28px buffer under 720px. Added matching `overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap` to `.version-text` so a long version string ellipses inside its now-fixed column instead of wrapping. Verified live (not just on paper this time): a local static server serving `ui/` directly with `?mock=1`, window resized to exactly 1000px - `.table-scroll.scrollWidth` (720px) now equals `.table-scroll.clientWidth` (720px), zero overflow, confirmed in a freshly-navigated tab (not a cache-patched one) to rule out a stale-stylesheet false negative. Re-verified no regression at the default 1320px window (table naturally widens to 1040px content width, all base column widths unchanged, the extra room still going to the name column as before) and at the 1100px breakpoint boundary (820px content, no overflow). No console errors at any width tested.

Syntax check: `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean (no output) after this round (app.js itself was not touched - this was a style.css-only fix).

## Round 6 (fix pass)

### cli/server component (addon-sync.ps1 / addon-server.ps1) - E13 compat false positives + metadata backfill

- **Fixed (high)** - E13's compatibility audit reported false-positive `stale` results (`tocInterfaces: [40402]`) for `BigWigs_Classic`, `BigWigs_WrathOfTheLichKing` and `BigWigs_Cataclysm` on the real install - verified live. Root cause: every toc-reading function in `addon-sync.ps1` (`Get-TocTitle`, `Get-FolderTocInfo`, `Get-TocCrossSourceIds`, `Get-TocDependencies`, `Get-TocInterfaceValues`) picked "the .toc whose basename equals the folder name, else the first .toc found" - but these BigWigs folders ship one `.toc` per game client in the SAME folder (`BigWigs_Classic.toc`, a Cataclysm-Classic build declaring `Interface 40402`, alongside `BigWigs_Classic_Mainline.toc`, the actual retail file WoW loads, declaring `Interface 120100`, plus `_Vanilla`/`_Wrath`/`_Cata`/`_Mists` siblings), so the basename-match rule silently preferred the wrong client's file whenever a same-named base `.toc` happened to exist. Added a new shared `Get-PrimaryTocFile` (duplicated in `addon-server.ps1`, which never dot-sources the CLI, same pattern as its other small duplicates) implementing the correct precedence - `<folder>_Mainline.toc`, then `<folder>-Mainline.toc`, then `<folder>.toc`, else the first `.toc` found - and switched every toc-selection call site above (both files' `Get-TocInterfaceValues`, plus the CLI's `Get-TocTitle`/`Get-FolderTocInfo`/`Get-TocCrossSourceIds`/`Get-TocDependencies`) to it, so title/version/dependency/cross-source-id/compat parsing all agree on the same primary file. `Get-TocTitle` additionally no longer requires an exact `<folder>.toc` to exist at all to consider a folder a title candidate (a folder shipping only a `_Mainline.toc` used to be skipped entirely, returning a null title) - now any folder `Get-PrimaryTocFile` can resolve qualifies. `Get-TocInterfaceValues` also fixed a second false-positive source: a single base `.toc` can declare BOTH `## Interface:` (its own native, often non-retail client) and `## Interface-Mainline:` (an explicit retail override - WoW's own multi-toc-in-one-file convention) - these used to be unioned together indiscriminately; now, when the chosen file has any `## Interface-Mainline:` line(s), those win outright and the plain `## Interface:` line(s) in that same file are ignored rather than merged in.
- **Fixed (medium)** - `wagoId`/`curseId` (from `## X-Wago-ID`/`## X-Curse-Project-ID` in the primary `.toc`) were only ever captured in `Sync-SingleAddon`/`Sync-SingleWagoAddon`'s real install/update branch, never on an Up-to-date pass and never by `-Status` - so a record installed (or last updated) before `Get-TocCrossSourceIds` existed for its source keeps both fields `null` forever unless it happens to get force-reinstalled. Added a backfill block (mirroring the existing DryRun-gated author-backfill's shape exactly) to both functions' Up-to-date branch - reads the already-installed folder's `.toc` directly (no extra network cost) and fills in whichever of `wagoId`/`curseId` is still missing, gated `-not $DryRun` like every other Up-to-date mutation, so a `-DryRun`/check run still makes zero writes. `-Status` (which never calls either sync function at all) gained its own equivalent offline backfill, run once per record before the `-Json`/table output is built and persisted via `Save-Config` only when something actually changed - the one place in this build where `-Status` (documented as "read-only, no network") is deliberately allowed to write `addons.json`, since the data it is backfilling comes entirely from disk. `Initialize-AddonRecordFields` already guaranteed both keys exist on every record (no change needed there). `tocInterfaces` itself needed no backfill/persistence change - it is never stored, always recomputed live from the folders on disk on every call, so the `Get-PrimaryTocFile` fix above alone corrects it for old and new records alike.
- **Verified** - offline, no CurseForge/Wago access, scratch `AddOns` folder + hand-copied `.build.info` (`-BuildInfoPath`) with a client build of `12.1.0.69587` (Interface 120100) passed to `-Status -Json -AddonsPath <scratch>`: `Foo` (only `Foo.toc`, `## Interface: 120100`, `## X-Wago-ID: abc123`, `## X-Curse-Project-ID: 42`, `wagoId`/`curseId` absent from its hand-written `addons.json` record) reported `compat: "ok"`, `tocInterfaces: [120100]`, and had `wagoId: "abc123"`/`curseId: "42"` correctly persisted back to `addons.json` by this same `-Status` run (confirmed by re-reading the file afterward, and idempotent on a second `-Status` run - no further write, values unchanged); `Bar` (`Bar.toc` with `## Interface: 40402` alongside `Bar_Mainline.toc` with `## Interface: 120005, 120007`) correctly selected the Mainline file and reported `compat: "stale-minor"`, `tocInterfaces: [120005, 120007]`; `Baz` (single `Baz.toc` with both `## Interface: 40402` and `## Interface-Mainline: 120100`) correctly preferred the Mainline tag and reported `compat: "ok"`, `tocInterfaces: [120100]` (not `[40402, 120100]`). A `BigWigs_Classic`-named fixture reproducing the exact real-world folder shape (`BigWigs_Classic.toc` Interface 40402 + `BigWigs_Classic_Mainline.toc` Interface 120100) reported `compat: "ok"`, confirming the originally reported false positive is fixed. `Get-TocTitle` unit-verified in isolation (functions dot-sourced without triggering `Main`) picked "Bar"/"BigWigs" (the Mainline titles) over "Bar (Classic)"/"BigWigs [Cata Classic]" for `Bar`/`BigWigs_Classic`, and correctly returned a title for a folder shipping ONLY a `_Mainline.toc` (previously null, no exact-basename `.toc` existed). Console (non-JSON) `-Status` table rendered the `Compat` column correctly for all fixtures. Parse check: `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `.ps1` files returns zero errors; both remain pure ASCII (byte-scanned, 0 bytes above U+007F). The sync-time (Up-to-date branch) `wagoId`/`curseId` backfill and its `-not $DryRun` gate were code-reviewed against the byte-identical, already-verified author/`latestGameVersions` backfill pattern they mirror, but not runtime-exercised end-to-end (that path requires a live CurseForge/Wago file fetch, unavailable in this offline pass).
- **Verified (Wago parity)** - a Wago-sourced fixture (`source: "wago"`, `slug`, `wagoId` already known, `fileId: "WwOPbDl6"` (string, as a real Wago release id is), folder with `## Interface: 120000`) reported `compat: "stale-minor"` and `tocInterfaces: [120000]` under both `-Status -Json` and the same fixture rendered correctly in the console table - confirming `tocInterfaces`/`compat` are already fully source-agnostic (computed from `folders`/`latestGameVersions` alone, regardless of `source`) once the `Get-PrimaryTocFile` fix above is in place, and that `Sync-SingleWagoAddon` already captures `latestGameVersions`/`latestFileDate` from `supported_retail_patches`/`created_at` at install, Up-to-date and (gated) check time exactly like the CurseForge path - no additional Wago-specific code was needed for compat parity, only the shared selection fix plus the wagoId/curseId backfill above (which is likewise source-agnostic, applying identically to a Wago record's own `## X-Wago-ID`/`## X-Curse-Project-ID` tags, though the fixture used already had `wagoId` set so this particular case didn't exercise that backfill path).
- **Fixed (medium)** - `addon-server.ps1`'s `ConvertFrom-WagoSearchCardHtml` (the `/api/wago/search` card parser) never matched a thumbnail: its regex required a quoted `src="..."` attribute, but Wago's actual server-rendered card markup emits it UNQUOTED on its own line (e.g. `src=https://cdn.wago.io/thumbnails/bbpUq5bKwoYlqZXouAhTm1GSLoYxFmWqedIMOqk2.png`) - verified live, every search result's `thumbnail` came back `null`. Changed the regex to make the surrounding quotes optional (`src=["']?(https://cdn\.wago\.io/thumbnails/[^"'\s>]+)`), matched against whitespace/`>`/a same quote-or-absence so it stops at the right place under either style. The `<h3[^>]*>([^<]*)</h3>` title regex was already tolerant of attributes inside the opening tag and already ran its match through `[System.Net.WebUtility]::HtmlDecode` - reviewed, no change needed. Verified offline with the exact reported snippet pasted into a here-string (plus a still-quoted variant, to confirm no regression): both now correctly extract `slug`, HTML-entity-decoded `name`, and `thumbnail`. Parse check re-run on `addon-server.ps1` after this change: zero errors, pure ASCII.
- **Fixed (high)** - typing a search term in Browse > Wago Addons never returned matching results, because the UI always sent `sort=popular` and `Handle-WagoSearch` forwarded `&sort=popular` verbatim to `https://addons.wago.io/`. Verified live: Wago's site only recognises `sort=name` - any other `sort` value (`popular`/`downloads`/`recent`/`newest`/`likes`/`trending`/`latest`/`top`) makes it silently ignore the `search` parameter and fall back to the plain popularity listing (`total` 1000), and `sort=updated` returns an empty list outright; omitting `sort` entirely is what actually yields relevance/popularity-ordered search results. Fixed `Handle-WagoSearch` (`addon-server.ps1`) to pass `sort=name` through verbatim and omit the upstream `sort` parameter for every other value (`popular`/`relevance`/empty/unrecognised). Fixed `Views.browse.renderSortOptions` (`ui/app.js`) so the Wago sort select now offers only the two values that work - Popularity (`popular`, default) and Name (`name`) - removing the CurseForge-only "Last updated"/"Total downloads" options when the Wago source is active; the debounced search-box handler already re-queried on every keystroke and needed no change. Verified live end-to-end against a throwaway server (port 47899, empty `addons.json`): `GET /api/wago/search?q=bonus+roll&sort=popular` -> 4 hits, `bonusrollpreview` first (relevance/popularity order); `...&sort=name` -> the same 4 hits, alphabetical (`bonusrollgate` first); `GET /api/wago/search?sort=popular` (no `q`) -> `total: 1000`, the plain popularity listing, confirming the "no query" path is untouched.

### ui component (app.js / index.html) - CurseForge API key UX + 401/403 retry storm

Two live defects reported directly by the user against Settings > CurseForge API key and Browse, both reproduced and fixed.

- **Fixed (high)** - the masked API-key field (`••••••••` + last-4 hint, shown whenever a key is already configured) had no affordance to actually replace it: `#btn-save-apikey` silently returned (no toast, no save, no error) whenever the field's value still started with the mask's dot character, and `#btn-test-apikey` treated the same condition as "test the saved key". A user who clicked into the field and pasted a new key without first selecting-all landed the paste mid-string (e.g. `••••••••edf0$2a$10$...`) - Save did nothing visible and Test kept testing the OLD key, reporting "Key rejected" and leaving the user believing their new key was bad when it was never tried. Fixed in `Views.settings` (`ui/app.js`): (a) a new `focus` handler on `#input-apikey` clears the field the instant it's focused (only when it still holds exactly the mask this app itself wrote there - `apikeyMask(hint)`, a small helper factoring out the "eight dots + hint" string so render()/focus/extractTypedApiKey all agree on what "unedited" looks like) and shows a new hint line ("Paste your new key — the current key ends in …`hint` and stays until you Save.") in a new `<p id="apikey-hint">` (`ui/index.html`) below the input; a paste now always replaces the whole value instead of landing inside it. A matching `blur` handler restores the masked display and hides the hint once the field is empty again (render() only repaints the masked value while the field isn't focused) - guarded to `if (!input.value)` specifically, since Save/Test both blur this field first (blur fires before a button's own click handler) and an unconditional restore would have wiped out real just-typed content before Save/Test ever got to read it - caught by this fix's own live verification, not shipped. (b) Save now branches three ways via a new `extractTypedApiKey(raw)` helper (strips a leading mask if still present, defensively, then trims): empty field -> info toast "No key entered — current key kept.", no API call; mask untouched -> info toast "No change.", no API call; real content -> saves it and shows "API key saved (…last4)." (`Actions.saveSettings` gained an optional `toastMessage` param so this can override its generic "Settings saved." without a second stacked toast). (c) Test now always labels which key it tested - "Pasted key: valid/rejected" for real typed/pasted content (via the same `extractTypedApiKey`, so a defensively-still-masked paste is correctly recognised as real content rather than mis-classified as "using the saved key"), "Saved key (…last4): valid/rejected" for the saved key, "No saved key: ..." when neither exists - never the previous ambiguous message with no indication of which key was actually tried. (d) added a "Clear key" button (`#btn-clear-apikey`, `.btn-danger-outline`) that confirms (`Components.Dialogs.confirm`) then PUTs `cfApiKey: ""` and toasts "API key cleared."; disabled via `render()` when no key is configured.
- **Fixed (high)** - a rejected CurseForge key (configured, but CurseForge itself returning 401/403 - distinct from no key at all) drove an unthrottled retry storm: every `/api/cf/*` consumer kept retrying independently, most visibly `LogoCache.ensure` (`ui/app.js`), which is called from `Views.myAddons.renderResults()` on every render - including every 800ms job-poll tick while a job runs - reproducing the reported `POST /api/cf/mods 403 every ~1 s`. Fixed by centralising 401/403 detection in `Api.request` (`ui/app.js`): any `/api/cf/*` response with that status sets a new sticky `Store.state.cfKeyRejected` flag (once - a repeat 401/403 while already flagged is a no-op) and shows one non-repeating toast ("Your CurseForge API key was rejected. Fix it in Settings."); once set, `Api.request` short-circuits every further `/api/cf/*` call before even reaching `fetch`/Mock, throwing the same shape of error a real 401/403 would have. The flag clears on a later successful `/api/cf/*` call or on any settings save (`Actions.saveSettings` now resets it unconditionally on success - the user may just have fixed it). `LogoCache.ensure` also gained its own explicit `if (Store.state.cfKeyRejected) return;` guard (belt-and-suspenders with the `Api.request` short-circuit, and literally "returns immediately" per the fix note) plus a general 10-minute-per-batch failure backoff (`failedAt` map, `FAILURE_BACKOFF_MS`) so even a non-key transient failure (network blip, upstream 500) stops retrying on every render instead of every poll tick. `Views.browse` (CurseForge tab) now shows a dedicated panel - new `#browse-keyrejected` (`ui/index.html`, reusing the existing `.nokey-panel`/`.nokey-card` styles), distinct from the "no key at all" panel, reading "Your CurseForge API key was rejected" with its own "Go to Settings" button - whenever a key IS configured but rejected, instead of either the no-key copy or a search UI that would just keep failing; the Wago Addons tab (never keyed) is unaffected and stays fully usable regardless. Mock (`?mock=1`) gained a dev-only way to reproduce the whole scenario without real CurseForge access: any `/api/cf/*` call now returns 401 when the saved mock key is shorter than 8 characters (the same threshold `/api/settings/test-key` already used for "invalid").
- **Verified** live in a real browser against the `?mock=1` dev harness (a local static file server, since `file://` renders as a non-interactive snapshot in this tool): saving a fresh key (no prior key) showed "API key saved (…hint)." and updated the status line; re-focusing the now-masked field cleared it and showed the "…stays until you Save" hint; typing a brand-new key over the cleared field and saving showed the correctly-updated hint/toast/status (confirms the mid-string-paste defect can no longer occur, since there's nothing left to paste into the middle of); clicking Save with the field untouched (still masked, unfocused) showed "No change." with no network call; Test against the saved key showed "Saved key (…hint): valid", and against a freshly-typed short key showed "Pasted key: Key rejected by CurseForge." - unambiguous in both directions; Clear key's confirm dialog and resulting "API key cleared."/"No key configured"/disabled-button state all verified. For the retry-storm fix: instrumented `Api.cfMods` in the live page, saved a short (mock-invalid) key, switched to My Addons (triggering `LogoCache.ensure`, which correctly 401'd once) and confirmed the toast fired exactly once; ran a `check` job (three 800ms-polled re-renders while running) and confirmed `Api.cfMods` was called **zero** additional times during it (previously would have been called on every re-render); Browse > CurseForge showed the new key-rejected panel while Browse > Wago Addons kept working normally; saving a valid key afterward flipped `Store.state.cfKeyRejected` back to `false` and Browse > CurseForge immediately started returning real results again on the next visit. No console errors at any point.

Parse check: `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `addon-sync.ps1` and `addon-server.ps1` returns zero errors after this round; both files remain pure ASCII (byte-scanned, no characters above U+007F). `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean.

### E15 - Vaporwave theme

- **Added** - a third Appearance option, requested by Eric: `ui/index.html`'s theme segmented control gains a "Vaporwave" button (`data-theme-value="vaporwave"`) alongside Dark/Light, persisted through the existing mechanism unchanged (`ui/app.js`'s `Prefs`, `addonSync.theme.v1` in `localStorage`, stamped as `data-theme="vaporwave"` on `<html>` before first paint so there is no flash of the wrong theme on load) - `Prefs.readTheme`/`applyTheme`/`setTheme` now recognize "vaporwave" as a second valid non-default value (a shared `isKnownTheme` helper) instead of the old binary `=== "light"` check; any other stored value still falls back to "dark". Dark stays the default.
- **Added** - `ui/style.css` gains a complete `:root[data-theme="vaporwave"]` token block (same pattern as the existing Light block, applied to the same `<html>` element so it wins by attribute-selector specificity) redefining every color token the dark theme defines - `--bg-0/1/2/3`, `--border`/`--border-soft`/`--border-hover`, `--text`/`--text-muted`/`--text-faint`, `--accent`/`--accent-hover`/`--accent-text`, `--success`/`--warning`/`--danger`/`--info`, their six `*-tint` companions, `--banner-danger-text`/`--form-error-text`, and `color-scheme` - using the brief's anchor colors (backgrounds #12081f/#1a0b2e/#241640/#2d1b4e, border #3a2a5c, text #f3e9ff, muted #b9a6d6, accent #ff71ce/#ff8fd8, success #05ffa1, warning #fffb96, danger #ff4f79, info/secondary #01cdfe) plus new theme-scoped shades this build needed for readability (`--text-faint: #9a86c0`, `--accent-text: #1a0e2e`, `--banner-danger-text: #ffb3c6`, `--form-error-text: #ff9fb8`). Every value was checked against WCAG contrast math (relative-luminance formula) in its real usage context - body text >15:1, muted text 6.9-8.4:1 against every surface it appears on, and every status chip's text-on-tint pairing 4.5-11.3:1 (the tightest, chip-danger's pink-on-pink-tint, still clears 4.79:1) - so no surface drops below the requested 4.5:1 floor. Because the whole app (tables, chips, drawer, dialogs, toasts, job panel, inputs/selects/toggles, Browse cards, Settings cards, empty/error states, banners) was already built exclusively on these `var(--*)` tokens, defining the block alone re-themes every surface with no per-component CSS needed.
- **Added** - four small tokens (`--danger-hover`, `--danger-text`, `--danger-border`, `--danger-outline-border`) plus one indirection token (`--focus-ring: var(--accent)`) factored into the base `:root` block, replacing five spots in `style.css` that had a color literal baked directly into the selector instead of a variable: `.btn-accent`'s text color (`#1a0e08`, already byte-identical to the unused `--accent-text` token - now actually references it), `.btn-danger`'s text/hover (`#2a0a0a`/`#ff6363`), `.btn-danger-outline`'s border (`rgba(239,74,74,0.4)`), the offline `.banner`'s border (`rgba(239,74,74,0.35)`), and all ~14 shared `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); }` rules across buttons/inputs/selects/chips/tabs/nav/links (now `var(--focus-ring)`). Dark and Light never redefine any of these five new tokens, so both render byte-identical to before this round; Vaporwave gives each its own value, most visibly `--focus-ring: var(--secondary)` (cyan `#01cdfe`) so every focus ring in the app - Tab-ing through the Add-addon dialog's input was used to verify this live - draws in cyan instead of hot pink. The `.select` chevron (a data-URI SVG that can't reference a CSS variable, so Light already carried its own hardcoded-stroke copy of the rule) gained a matching Vaporwave-stroke (`#b9a6d6`) copy for the same reason.
- **Added** - two tokens that exist only for this theme (`--secondary: #01cdfe`, `--purple: #b967ff` - dark/light have no selector that reads them, so they're absent there) plus `--accent-active: #ff4fbf` (the brief's third accent shade, driving only `.btn-accent:active`, a state dark/light never style beyond the shared `:hover` rule) back the theme's signature touches, all additive `:root[data-theme="vaporwave"]`-scoped rules layered on top of the token block: a soft accent-colored `box-shadow` bloom on every `.btn-accent` (intensifying on hover, snapping to `--accent-active` while pressed) and on `.nav-item.is-active`; a pink-to-cyan `background-clip:text` gradient on `.brand-name` plus a matching `drop-shadow` glow filter on the brand `<img>` icon; a faint CSS-only perspective grid - two low-alpha (0.08-0.14) `repeating-linear-gradient`s plus a rising accent glow, `mask-image`-faded toward the top - along the bottom of `.sidebar` (an `::after` pseudo-element behind a `z-index:1` stacking context on `.sidebar`'s real children, so it never intercepts a click - `pointer-events:none` besides - and every button/label/focus-ring stays exactly as usable); and an inset neon-hairline `box-shadow` outline (no added box size, unlike a real border) on every `.chip-*` variant and the active `.filter-chip`. Purple scrollbars needed no new rule at all - the existing `scrollbar-color`/`::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb` rules already ride `var(--border)`/`var(--border-hover)`, which this theme's own token block already set to purple.
- **Verified** - live in a real browser against `?mock=1` (a local static file server, `file://` doesn't render interactively in this tool) at the app's documented 1320x900 window size: switched Dark -> Vaporwave -> Light -> Dark via Settings > Appearance and confirmed Dark and Light render pixel-identical to their pre-round screenshots (same orange/blue accents, same chip colors, no stray purple/pink/cyan on any surface) - the shared-selector edits above (`.btn-accent`, `.btn-danger`, `.btn-danger-outline`, `.banner`, all `:focus-visible` rules) changed nothing visible in either theme, exactly as designed. In Vaporwave: My Addons (table, status/compat/missing-deps chips, filter chips, selection bar, job panel with results and a toast from a mock check job) all readable and correctly re-colored; the detail drawer (Overview with the no-key prompt banner and green "Built for 12.1" compat chip, Versions with correctly-colored Release/Beta/Alpha chips verified programmatically via `getComputedStyle` - not just eyeballed, since small-scale screenshots make yellow read as pink - Changelog with its version `<select>` and the new cyan chevron, empty Screenshots state); Browse's CurseForge no-key panel and Wago Addons tab (source-badge chips, card grid, Install buttons' neon glow, a card's drawer); the Add-addon dialog with a live cyan focus ring on its focused input; and Settings (radio buttons/toggle switch native `accent-color`, the gradient brand wordmark and glowing icon in the sidebar, the faint bottom-of-sidebar grid confirmed present via `getComputedStyle(sidebar, '::after')` since it is intentionally too subtle to see behind the mostly-opaque button stack at normal screenshot scale, `.btn-danger-outline`'s pink-tinted "Force reinstall all" border, and a live Diagnostics run with a correctly mint-green "ok" dot) all matched the brief with no leftover dark-theme color found anywhere. Theme choice persisted across a full page reload with no flash of the wrong theme. Zero console errors throughout. `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean (no output).
- Sidebar brand: single-line "Furphy Addon Manager" (13px, nowrap) with a 26px icon.
- Vaporwave is now the default theme (Dark/Light still selectable); manifest colours follow it.

## Round 8

### ui component (app.js / index.html) - audit findings

- **Fixed (medium)** - `Markdown.toHtml()` (E12's Wago description/changelog renderer) shredded a normal bulleted list whenever an item's text wrapped onto an indented continuation line with no marker of its own (standard CommonMark "lazy continuation" - e.g. the real BigWigs Wago addon's description). The `else` branch (matched by any non-blank, non-heading, non-bullet-marker line) unconditionally called `closeList()` before starting a new `<p>`, so each wrapped item became its own one-item `<ul>` interleaved with orphan `<p>` fragments holding the wrapped remainder - reproduced with a hand-built 2-item wrapped list (`node --check`-verified test script), which rendered as 4 disconnected `<ul>`/`<p>` blocks before the fix. Changed the branch to append the line's inline-rendered text onto the still-open last `<li>` (string-replace the trailing `</li>`) instead of closing the list, only when a list is actually open; plain wrapped paragraph text (no list involved) is unaffected. Re-verified with a standalone Node harness: the 2-item wrapped list now renders as one clean `<ul>` with both continuation lines folded into their `<li>`, and a separate wrapped plain paragraph / heading / bold+italic afterward all still render correctly.
- **Fixed (medium)** - the Add-addon dialog's help text and input placeholder (`ui/index.html`) only mentioned a numeric Project ID or a CurseForge URL, giving no indication that a `wago:<slug>` token or a `https://addons.wago.io/addons/<slug>` URL also works and needs no API key at all - verified live in the code that `Actions.submitAddInput` (app.js) already classifies and accepts a Wago token/URL ahead of the CurseForge-URL branch, key-free, and that its own validation-error messages ("Enter a Project ID, a CurseForge URL, or a Wago addon URL.") already mention Wago even though the static dialog copy never did. Reworded both strings to name all three accepted forms.
- **Rejected** - "automatic startup check (E2) opens the job panel expanded with the log visible on a fresh browser state" (high). Traced the exact call path: `Actions.autoCheckForUpdates()` awaits `startJob()`, which - synchronously, once its own `await Api.postJob` resolves - calls `App.onJobStarted()` (`Components.JobPanel.show()`, uncollapsing the panel) and returns; `autoCheckForUpdates` then calls `Components.JobPanel.collapseIfOpen()` in the very next line, with no `await` between the two. Per the JS event-loop model, `show()` and `collapseIfOpen()` both run inside the same microtask turn - the browser never paints the intermediate "expanded" state, only the final collapsed one (`hidden=false` + `.is-collapsed`, which `style.css`'s `.job-panel.is-collapsed .job-panel-body { display:none }` already hides the log/results under). Panel collapse/expand is pure in-memory DOM state (a CSS class), never read from or gated on `localStorage`, so a fresh-vs-returning browser makes no difference to this sequencing either way. This is the exact behavior Round 1's E2 entry already added and described ("the automatic check's progress panel stays collapsed to its slim title bar rather than popping open full-height"); no reproduction path found against the current code. No change made.
- **Rejected** - "app defaults to the vaporwave theme instead of dark, contradicting SPEC section 3's 'Dark theme' requirement" (high), citing `Prefs`'s three `"vaporwave"` fallbacks (readTheme/applyTheme/setTheme) as an unintentional regression against a stale in-code comment. This is Round 7/E15's own deliberate, already-shipped decision, not a bug: E15 explicitly states "Vaporwave is now the default theme (Dark/Light still selectable)" as its closing line, after a full live-browser verification pass (contrast math, both other themes confirmed pixel-identical to pre-round, zero console errors). Overriding it back to "dark" would revert a verified, intentional product decision from a prior round, which this fix pass has no authority to do unilaterally. No change made - the stale sub-comment inside `Prefs` ("still falls through to 'dark'", accurate only through Round 6) was left as-is since fixing comment text is not what the finding requested and isn't itself a functional bug.
- **Rejected, out of scope** - "on window close (`pagehide`), call `navigator.sendBeacon('/api/shutdown')`" (high). This exact SPEC line (section 3, "On window close...") has now been raised and rejected five times across Rounds 1-7 (Round 1 twice, Round 2 twice, Round 6 once) for the same documented, unchanged reason: `pagehide` fires identically on a plain F5/reload or in-tab navigation as on an actual real window close, with no script-observable way to tell the two apart - wiring it back in (guarded on "no job running" or not) would kill the local backend mid-reload and strand the user on a dead, unstyled page with no recovery short of the desktop shortcut. The in-code comment block documenting this (`App.wireGlobal()`, app.js ~4472-4479) is still present, current, and accurate. No change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope** - "system/desktop notifications for addon updates" (low). Not present anywhere in SPEC.md: the base spec's job/toast contract (section 3, "Global") only calls for the in-app toast + job panel this app already has, with no `Notification`/Service-Worker requirement anywhere, and no expansion (E1-E15) adds one. Same class of rejection as Round 1's "Addon disable/enable" and "download progress percentage" findings - a feature request, not a bug, and outside this fix pass's scope.
- **Rejected, out of scope** - "addon profiles/presets (multiple named, switchable addon sets)" (medium). Not in SPEC.md - E4's export/import is explicitly backup/restore, and no expansion documents a `profiles` key, `/api/profiles` route, or profile-activate job kind. The finding's own fix requires new `addon-server.ps1` endpoints and a new `settings.json` shape - components outside this `ui`-only round's scope even if it were in-spec. No change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope** - "persist Browse search history across sessions" (low). Not in SPEC.md; no expansion documents a search-history feature for Browse. A feature request, not a bug. No change made.
- **Rejected, out of scope** - "addon favorites/bookmarks" (low). Not in SPEC.md; no expansion documents a favorites/bookmarks feature for My Addons or Browse. A feature request, not a bug. No change made.
- **Verified** - `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean (no output) after the two applied fixes.

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - Diagnostics review pass

- **Fixed (low)** - `Test-DiagLastSync`'s "Last sync" row returned a raw ISO-8601 UTC string (`$mtime.ToString('yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ssZ')`) as its `detail`, the only one of the 9 diagnostics rows not already finished display text - Server uptime formats itself as `"2h 3m"`, CurseForge reachability as `"Reachable (HTTP 200)"`, etc. Confirmed against `ui\app.js`'s `diagRow()`: it renders `c.detail` verbatim with no date parsing of its own (unlike the addons table's Updated column, which runs `Utils.relativeTime` client-side), so the raw ISO string reached the Settings > Diagnostics panel unchanged - reproduced live, the row read `2026-09-03T14:19:25Z` next to eight rows of prose. Added `Format-DiagTimestamp` (parses this script's own `yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ssZ` shape, falls back to the raw input on any parse failure - never throws) and used it for both the `last-run.txt` mtime path and the `$Script:LastRun.timestamp` fallback path; the final `"never"` fallback needed no change. Verified in isolation (function extracted verbatim from the shipped file): `2026-09-03T14:19:25Z` -> `Sep 3, 2026, 09:19 AM` (local time); a garbage/unparseable string returns unchanged rather than throwing. `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` passes clean after the change.
- **Rejected** - "request handler blocks the single-threaded loop via unguarded `Start-Sleep` calls in `Invoke-WagoHttpRequest`" (high) and "multiple `Invoke-WebRequest` calls with `TimeoutSec 20` can block the request loop for up to 20 seconds" (medium). Both confirmed accurate as a description of the code (the main loop at ~L3831 runs `Invoke-Route` synchronously between `BeginGetContext` calls, so a slow/retrying CF or Wago proxy call does stall every other request for its duration) but neither is a defect: SPEC.md line 72 explicitly mandates "Single-threaded request loop" for this whole script, and section 2's CurseForge proxy contract explicitly specifies `timeout 20 s` (line 98) knowing it runs on that same thread. Redesigning the proxy calls to be async would contradict SPEC.md's own architecture requirement, not fix a bug in it - the same "out of scope" reasoning prior rounds have applied to fixes that would require editing SPEC.md itself (Round 1's "document as a design deviation in SPEC... outside this component-scoped round", Round 3's identical rejection for `previousFileName`). Both findings' own proposed remedies concede this ("accept documented latency impact" / "Document in API contract" - SPEC.md itself, since no separate API-contract file exists in this project). No change made.
- **Rejected** - "path traversal check in `Handle-ScanDelete` relies on case-sensitive `StartsWith`" (medium, lines 3100-3106). The finding's own text already concludes the code is correct: `$targetFull.StartsWith($addonsFull, [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)` (line 3103, unchanged) explicitly passes `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, and the folder name is additionally validated a few lines earlier (`$folder -match '[\\/]'` / `'\.\.'`, line 3077) before it ever reaches path construction. No change made.
- **Rejected** - "file I/O reads happen without explicit error handling for concurrent write conflicts" (low, `Complete-ImportPhase`/`Complete-SwitchSourcePhase`/`Update-JobStatus`). Verified against the code: every cited `ReadAllText` call reads a per-job-unique `<jobId>[-<phase>].out`/`.err` file, written once by a single already-`HasExited` child process and never touched by `addon-sync.ps1` (which only ever writes `addons.json`/`sync.log`, both different files) - there is no writer left to race by the time these reads happen. The finding's own text concludes the same ("timing windows are negligible... No fix required - this is acceptable"). No change made.

## Round 8

### E16 - Keyless mode

- **Added** - CurseForge-sourced addons (no API key configured) now get search/browse, descriptions, logos, screenshots and cross-source changelogs from three new, purely additive, read-only enrichment sources, in the order Wago (E12) -> the offline instawow-data/strongbox-catalogue catalogue index -> addon-radar.com -> nothing. `addon-sync.ps1` is unchanged - installing/updating CurseForge addons already worked fully keylessly via the base build's own `mods/{id}/files`/download endpoints; E16 is Browse-tab and detail-drawer metadata only, and the key-gated `/api/cf/*` proxy's behavior (incl. its 409 `no-key` contract) is completely untouched - every endpoint below simply proxies the official API when a key IS configured, touching none of the new sources.
- **Added** (`addon-server.ps1`) - `Save-CfCatalogueIndex`/`Initialize-CfCatalogueIndex`/`Load-CfCatalogueIndexFromDisk`/`Search-CfCatalogue`/`Get-CfCatalogueEntry`: a `ROOT\cache\cf-catalogue.json` index (instawow-data + strongbox-catalogue, merged, refreshed at most once/24h automatically or on demand), loaded at startup and searched/looked-up entirely in memory. `ConvertFrom-DevalueJson`/`ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail`/`Get-AddonRadarSearchRows`/`Invoke-AddonRadarRequest`/`Get-AddonRadarDetail`/`Search-AddonRadar`: a purpose-built decoder for addon-radar.com's SvelteKit "devalue" wire format (no public spec exists to verify it against - see SPEC.md's own caveat) plus a paced (>=600ms), 24h-cached (in-memory and on-disk per slug) proxy for its detail/search pages. `Get-WagoAutoMatch`/`Get-WagoAutoMatchNormalizedName`: a conservative name+author probe (reusing E12's own Wago search internals) for a CurseForge addon whose `.toc` carries no `X-Wago-ID` tag at all - accepts a candidate only on an exact stripped-name match AND a confirmed author match, never on name similarity alone, and caches a definitive miss the same 24h as a hit.
- **Added** (`addon-server.ps1`) - `GET /api/cf/enrich/{projectId}` (`Get-CfEnrichmentNoKey`'s fallback chain: a record's toc-derived `wagoId` first, else a `Get-WagoAutoMatch` probe, then the catalogue-resolved slug through `Get-AddonRadarDetail`, else `catalogue-only`), `GET /api/cf/browse?q=&limit=` (`Search-CfCatalogue` first, `Search-AddonRadar` merged in only when that returns fewer than 5 hits), `POST /api/cf/catalogue/refresh` (Settings > Maintenance's "Refresh CurseForge catalogue now", a fast op). All three are always keyless-capable - no 409 no-key gate, unlike every other `/api/cf/*` route. `/api/diagnostics` gains "CurseForge catalogue cache" and "addon-radar reachability" checks (12 rows total now).
- **Added** (`ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`, `ui/index.html`) - `Store.state.drawer.source` gains `'cf-keyless'` (a CurseForge-sourced drawer opened with no key); `loadEnrich()` fetches `/api/cf/enrich/{id}`, and `renderHeader`/`renderKeylessOverview`/`renderKeylessChangelog`/`renderKeylessScreenshots` render per its `source` field. A `wago-match` result pre-populates `d.wagoAddon`/`d.wagoReleases`/`d.wagoGallery` (keyed by the enrichment's own `wagoSlug`, via three new `loadCfKeylessWago*` loaders) and then calls E12's `renderWagoOverview`/`renderWagoChangelog`/`renderWagoScreenshots` completely unmodified - verified live that none of those three functions read `d.slug` directly, only their own loaders do, so this reuses them with zero duplication. An `addon-radar` result renders its HTML description through the same `Sanitize.render` pipeline the official CurseForge description already uses. Browse's old fully-blocking "needs a free API key" panel is replaced by a non-blocking banner; the search box, results grid (a new sparser `catalogueCard` renderer with an "Indexed"/"Live match" provenance pill), and the "Install by Project ID" mini-form all keep working with no key, while category/CurseForge-sort selects hide (neither keyless source carries CurseForge's category taxonomy) and "Load more" is suppressed (the endpoint has no pagination). `Api.isKeylessCfPath` exempts the three new routes from the existing key-rejected short-circuit/toast, which would otherwise have incorrectly blocked them.
- **Fixed** (`ui/app.js`) - caught during this same pass: `App.focusCurrentSearch`'s `/` keyboard shortcut only focused Browse's CurseForge search box when a key was configured, even though it (and the rest of `#browse-content`) is now fully usable without one - only a REJECTED key still blocks it. Updated the gate accordingly.
- **Verified** - `Search-CfCatalogue`/`Get-CfCatalogueEntry`/`ConvertFrom-DevalueJson`/`ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail`/`Get-AddonRadarSearchRows`/`Get-WagoAutoMatchNormalizedName` extracted verbatim from the shipped file (E10's own technique) and unit-tested in complete isolation - all passed, including cycle-safety and malformed-input degradation for the devalue decoder. A real throwaway server instance (hand-seeded `cache\cf-catalogue.json` and `cache\addon-radar\bigwigs.json` fixtures, so startup and the addon-radar-detail/diagnostics paths hit disk, not the network) confirmed `/api/cf/enrich/{id}` correctly returns `addon-radar`/`catalogue-only` for the two fixture scenarios, `/api/diagnostics` returns all 12 rows, and `/api/ping`/`/api/shutdown` still work. `/api/cf/browse` for two distinct queries against the (deliberately tiny, 2-entry) catalogue fixture did trigger two live addon-radar.com search requests during this build's own testing (not CurseForge - the <5-hit fallback threshold, confirmed working as designed via a same-query repeat call returning from cache in ~2ms afterward) - noted here for the same live-request transparency this file's history already applies elsewhere. UI verified live in a real browser (`?mock=1`, zero network access, a local static file server): the `addon-radar` and `wago-match` drawer branches, keyless Browse (banner, hidden category/sort, working search and Install-by-ID, catalogue cards), Settings > Maintenance's refresh button, and the Diagnostics panel's two new rows (rendered by the existing generic checks-list loop, no code change needed there) all matched the design with zero console errors in both dark and light themes; a regression pass with a mock key configured confirmed the pre-existing keyed Browse-search and drawer-Overview paths render byte-identical to before this expansion. `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean; `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on both `.ps1` files passes clean (`addon-sync.ps1` unchanged); `addon-server.ps1` remains pure ASCII (byte-scanned).
