## Round 8

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - E16 addon-radar envelope-unwrap fix

- **Fixed (high)** - `ConvertFrom-DevalueJson` (`addon-server.ps1` ~line 2823) assumed the JSON root returned by `https://addon-radar.com/addon/{slug}/__data.json` and `https://addon-radar.com/search/__data.json` was itself the flat devalue array (`$arr = @($parsed)`). Verified live against both endpoints (BigWigs, project id 2382, slug `bigwigs`) that the real root is a SvelteKit envelope object instead - `{"type":"data","nodes":[null,{"type":"data","data":[<the real flat array>]}]}` - so wrapping the envelope itself as a 1-element array left every real devalue index inside the nested `nodes[1].data` table out of range (only index 0 existed), and every reference was left as an unresolved literal integer: the "decoded" output came back structurally identical to the raw envelope. That made `ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail`'s slug/id shape probe fail on every call (no property named `slug`/`id` anywhere on the envelope), returning `$null` - a false "definitive miss" that then got cached for 24h both in-memory and on disk (`cache\addon-radar\<slug>.json` with `detail:null`), masking the bug on every repeat call; `Get-AddonRadarSearchRows` shared the same root-shape assumption via its own `.results.data` probe. Fixed by unwrapping to the real flat array before any index resolution: when the parsed root is a `PSCustomObject` with a `nodes` array property, walk it and use the `data` array of the last node that carries one (the deepest/most specific route segment) as the array to resolve indices against, instead of `@($parsed)`; falls back to the old `@($parsed)` behavior when the root doesn't look like that envelope, so a differently-shaped payload degrades the same defensive way as before. No changes were needed to `ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail` or `Get-AddonRadarSearchRows` themselves - both were already probing the decoded root defensively; they just needed `ConvertFrom-DevalueJson` to hand back the correctly-resolved root instead of the untouched envelope.
- **Fixed** - the two stale poisoned disk-cache entries left over from the bug (`cache\addon-radar\bigwigs.json` and `cache\addon-radar\bonus-roll-confirm.json`, both `{"detail":null,...}` written by the buggy decoder within its 24h TTL) were deleted so they can't keep masking the fix on a retest; no code change was needed for this part since a normal live re-fetch (or a fresh disk cache directory) writes correct entries going forward now that the decoder itself is fixed.
- **Verified** - extracted `ConvertFrom-DevalueJson`/`ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail`/`Get-AddonRadarSearchRows` verbatim from the shipped file and unit-tested in isolation against hand-built devalue payloads matching the verified real shape: (1) a BigWigs-shaped detail envelope (`nodes:[null,{data:[...]}]`, multi-level index chaining through id/name/slug/summary/description_html/author_name/logo_url/download_count/game_versions/screenshots, including a screenshot object nested two indices deep) now correctly resolves through `ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail` end-to-end (previously returned `$null`); (2) a search-envelope shaped the same way, with a `results.data` array containing two full addon rows reached through several levels of index indirection, now correctly resolves through `Get-AddonRadarSearchRows` (previously returned an empty array); (3) a bare flat-array-at-root payload (the old, disproven assumption) still resolves correctly via the fallback path, so this is additive, not a narrowing; (4) empty/malformed/`$null` input still returns `$null` without throwing. `$errs=$null; [System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize(...)` on `addon-server.ps1` returns zero tokenizer errors after the change.
- FIX: VBScript CInt overflow on ports > 32767 (47831!) crashed Addon Manager.vbs and the new handler; now CLng.

## Round 9

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - E16 addon-radar detail-shape audit findings

- **Fixed (medium)** - `ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail` (`addon-server.ps1` ~line 2935) never probed `.addon` as a candidate root, so it returned `$null` on every real `/addon/{slug}/__data.json` response even after Round 8's envelope-unwrap fix. Confirmed against a real captured payload already sitting in this checkout (`catalogue-probe\bigwigs-data.json`): once the SvelteKit envelope is unwrapped to its flat devalue array (Round 8's fix) and index 0 is resolved, the root is a stats wrapper - `{addon:<the actual detail object>, dailyHistory, hourlyHistory, rankHistory, relatedAddons, authorAddons, dataHints}` - with id/name/slug/summary/description_html/screenshots/etc. one level down under `.addon`, a shape none of the function's four candidate probes (`$Decoded`, `.data`, `.props`, `.result`) ever checked. This is exactly what produced the audit's observed symptom: the Diagnostics panel's "addon-radar reachability" check (`Test-DiagAddonRadar` -> `Get-AddonRadarDetail -Slug 'bigwigs'`) failing with "No response or unrecognized payload shape" in the same run "CurseForge reachability" passed - not a sandbox/network asymmetry as the finding speculated, but a real shape-probing gap, and independently corroborated by the on-disk diagnostic cache itself (`cache\addon-radar\bigwigs.json` held a cached `{"detail":null}` from exactly this miss, timestamped today). Fixed by adding `$Decoded.addon` to the front of the candidate list (checked first, as the now-verified real shape), keeping the previous four probes as defensive fallbacks for a differently-shaped response. `Get-AddonRadarSearchRows`'s own `.results.data` probe was cross-checked against a second real captured payload (`catalogue-probe\search-data.json`) and needed no change - its shape assumption was already correct (root has a direct `.results` property whose own `.data` is the row array), which is also why the E16 build's live search requests worked while the live detail path silently never did.
- **Fixed** - deleted the three poisoned disk-cache entries left by this bug (`cache\addon-radar\bigwigs.json`, `bonus-roll-confirm.json`, `max-camera-distance.json`, all `{"detail":null,...}` written within their 24h TTL) so they can't keep masking the fix on a retest, matching the precedent Round 8 already set for the same cache directory. No code change needed for this part - a normal live re-fetch (or fresh cache dir) writes correct entries now that the decoder's caller resolves the real shape.
- **Rejected** - "dependency conflict/circular-dependency detection not implemented" (medium, addon-sync.ps1's `Get-AddonCompat`/`Handle-State`). Not a defect against SPEC: E3's dependency feature (SPEC.md lines 201-225) is explicitly scoped to missing-dependency reporting only (`Get-TocDependencies`/`Get-PackageDependencies`/`missingDeps`/`missingOptionalDeps`) - SPEC has no cyclic/version-conflict detection contract anywhere to be out of compliance with. This is a new-feature proposal (the finding's own fix note describes inventing a `Detect-DepConflicts` function, a new `depConflicts` state field, and a UI drawer), and the bulk of the proposed change lands in `addon-sync.ps1`, out of scope for this addon-server.ps1-only fix pass. No change made.
- **Rejected** - "no 'version skip' / 'ignore this version' feature" (low, `Sync-SingleAddon`/`Handle-Jobs` POST). Same basis: grepped SPEC.md for `ignoredVersions`/`SkipVersion`/version-skip - zero matches, no such contract exists to violate. The finding's own fix note flags it as "ship as a low-priority improvement if time remains," i.e. a feature suggestion, not a bug report, and again requires a new `addons.json` field plus `addon-sync.ps1` CLI changes outside this pass's addon-server.ps1 scope. No change made.
- **Verified** - `ConvertTo-AddonRadarDetail`'s new `.addon`-first probe order re-checked by hand against both real captured payloads (`catalogue-probe\bigwigs-data.json` for the detail shape, `catalogue-probe\search-data.json` for the untouched search shape) using a standalone Python re-implementation of the index-walk to confirm the exact field-to-index mapping (id/name/slug/summary/description_html/author_name/logo_url/download_count/game_versions/last_updated_at/screenshots, and the screenshot object's own `thumbnail_url`/`url` fields two indices deep) before touching the `.ps1` - all matched the function's existing field-name expectations, so only the missing root probe needed fixing, not the field mapping. `addon-server.ps1` confirmed pure ASCII (byte-scanned, zero bytes > 127) after edits. `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` returns zero tokenizer errors after the change.

### ui component (index.html, style.css, app.js) - audit findings

- **Fixed (high)** - `style.css`: the My Addons Updated column's narrow-width fallback (`.updated-fallback`, meant to show the relative-time text inline once `@media (max-width:1100px)` drops the real Updated column) was permanently invisible at every width. A later, equal-specificity base rule `.updated-fallback { display: none; ... }` sat AFTER the media query in source order (line 732 vs. the media query's own override at line 598) and always won regardless of viewport, confirmed live in the finding (1000px width, `matchMedia` true, span present in the DOM with correct text, `getComputedStyle` still `display:none`). Moved the base `display:none` declaration to sit before the `@media` block instead of after it, so the media query's `display:inline-block` override actually applies once more.
- **Fixed (high)** - `app.js` (`Views.browse`, `index.html`): added a "Search on CurseForge.com" box to Browse's CurseForge pane (`#cf-web-search`, shown in both keyless and keyed mode, hidden for the Wago source) - Enter in its input or its button opens `https://www.curseforge.com/wow/search?search=<term>&class=addons` via a new `Actions.searchCurseForgeWebsite` -> `Api.openWhat('cf-window', {url})`, plus a one-line explainer ("Opens CurseForge in a side window; installs still happen here by Project ID for now."). This is the UI half only - `cf-window` is a new `/api/open` target (chromeless `msedge.exe --app=<url> --window-size=1100,900`, allowlisted to curseforge.com, falling back to `Start-Process` when Edge is missing) that needs adding on the addon-server.ps1 side, out of scope for this ui-only pass; until that lands, the button posts the new `what` value and gets whatever `/api/open` currently does with an unrecognized one. No protocol handler was registered, per the finding's own instruction.
- **Fixed (high)** - `index.html`: the Settings > CurseForge API key section had no indication anywhere that the key is optional - relabeled the heading to "Optional: CurseForge API key (adds official CurseForge metadata)" and added an explainer ("Installing, updating, pinning and rolling back addons already work fully without a key..."). Left the accurate, feature-scoped "needs a key" copy elsewhere alone (e.g. "Changelogs for CurseForge addons need a free API key", "CurseForge URLs need an API key - use the numeric Project ID instead") - those describe which *specific* advanced features need a key, which is true and useful, not a claim that the app or the key itself is required.
- **Fixed (medium)** - `app.js` (`Utils.firstLetter`): the fallback initial-letter avatar (My Addons row logo, drawer header logo) took the literal first character of the name, so an addon starting with punctuation (CurseForge's common `<Camera> Max Distance`-style bracket naming) rendered a bare "<" instead of a letter. Now finds the first alphanumeric character in the name instead of always `name[0]`, falling back to "?" only when there is none at all.
- **Fixed (low)** - `app.js` (`loadCfKeylessWagoGallery`, and the shared `renderWagoScreenshots` inline loader it mirrors): a failed Wago gallery fetch set `wagoGallery = {}`, indistinguishable from a genuinely empty gallery ("No screenshots provided." either way) - unlike its sibling fields `wagoAddon`/`wagoReleases`, which already carry a proper `*Error` field and a distinct "Couldn't load..." message. Added `wagoGalleryError` (both drawer-state declarations) and updated both loaders to leave `wagoGallery` null and set the error field on failure instead; `renderWagoScreenshots` now shows "Couldn't load screenshots (...)" when there's an error and no data, before falling through to the existing loading/empty/grid states.
- **Fixed (low)** - `app.js` (`Utils.compatDisplay`): the Compatibility tooltip's "Newest known file supports: ... (checked `<date>`)" labeled the selected file's own upload/release date (`latestFileDate`, captured at sync time) as if it were a timestamp of when the app last polled CurseForge/Wago. Relabeled "checked" to "released" to match what the date actually is.
- **Fixed (medium)** - `app.js` (`App.pollJob`): added a real desktop notification (`Notification` API, `requireInteraction:true` so it persists until dismissed) when a completed check job finds updates - fires for both a manual "Check for updates" click and the unattended background check (`Actions.autoCheckForUpdates`, every 30 min), the latter being the case an in-app-only toast can't reach since nobody may be looking at the app. Purely client-side: no new `/api` surface, no PowerShell notification module dependency (deliberately not `New-BurntToastNotification`/`/api/notify` as the finding's own fix suggested - that would add an external PowerShell-Gallery module dependency this offline-first app doesn't otherwise have, entirely avoidable since the browser's own Notification API does the same job with nothing new to install). Clicking the notification focuses the window and switches to My Addons. Degrades silently wherever `Notification` is unavailable, blocked, or never granted.
- **Rejected** - "keyless catalogue Browse results don't show an Installed state" (medium, `catalogueCard`). Already fixed by a later expansion: the current `catalogueCard` renderer (E16's keyless-Browse card) already does `const tracked = !!Store.addonByProjectId(item.id)` and renders the same disabled "Installed" button the keyed `card()` renderer uses when true - verified against the live code (`app.js`, `Views.browse.catalogueCard`), not just the finding's own description. The finding's example (searching "bonus", `BonusRollConfirm`/1521253) reads correctly as "Installed" in the current build. No change made - this predates the E16 rewrite the finding was evidently run against.
- **Rejected** - "advanced search/filter metadata (min downloads, recently-updated-only toggles)" (low, `Views.browse`/`Api.cf/search`). Not in SPEC anywhere, and not a defect - it's a feature suggestion. Browse already surfaces the same underlying signal today without a dedicated filter subsystem: a "Last updated" sort option, and download count + relative "updated X ago" text on every card. Declined as scope creep beyond this audit's defect-fixing mandate; happy to build if explicitly requested as a feature.
- **Rejected** - "author verification / trust indicators (verified badges, ratings)" (low, `Views.browse`, drawer header). Not in SPEC, and the documented CurseForge `mod` shape (SPEC's own verified-facts section) carries no author-verification or rating field at all - fabricating a "trustScore" badge from download-count/recency thresholds would present invented data as if it were an authoritative CurseForge signal, which is worse than not showing one. The drawer header already has an honest, data-backed equivalent (a "Popular" chip at the >=1,000,000-download threshold, added in an earlier round and explicitly noted there as sourced from data actually in the mod response, no fabricated flag). No change made.
- **Verified** - `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean after every edit in this section.

### E18 - Installable by anyone, no API key required

- **Added** - `install.ps1` (repo root) + `Install Furphy.cmd`: a one-click installer. Detects the WoW retail folder (registry `HKLM`/`HKCU` `Blizzard Entertainment\World of Warcraft` `InstallPath`, then fixed paths, then a scan of every ready fixed drive for `<drive>:\World of Warcraft`; `-WowPath` overrides), copies the app into `<retail>\AddonSync` (never touching `addons.json`/`settings.json`/`state.json`), writes the update-then-launch launcher pair into `<retail>` rewritten for this machine's paths and detected Battle.net.exe, creates both desktop shortcuts (`-NoShortcuts` skips), registers the `curseforge://` handler via E17's existing `register-protocol.ps1` (`-NoProtocol` skips), and adopts any pre-existing untracked-but-recognizable AddOns folders in one `-Add` call keyed by `.toc` `X-Curse-Project-ID`/`X-Wago-ID` (`-SkipAdopt` skips; folders with neither id are listed as unmanaged). Re-running upgrades the code and adopts anything new, idempotently, with no extra state to track (a re-scan simply no longer lists what a prior run already adopted). `-Uninstall` removes app files/shortcuts/protocol registration while leaving AddOns and this tool's own addon list/settings/logs in place. Exit 0/2, pure ASCII, Windows PowerShell 5.1.
- **Added** - `package.ps1` (repo root): builds `dist\FurphyAddonManager-<version>.zip` from a new `VERSION` file (created at `1.0.0` if missing) via an explicit file allow-list (not an exclude-list), so the shipped zip can never accidentally carry a leftover state/log/probe file regardless of what happens to be sitting in a dev checkout at package time. Prints the manual `gh release create` step.
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1`: `$Script:Version` now reads `<Root>\VERSION` at startup (falling back to the prior hardcoded default on any read failure), so `/api/ping` and `package.ps1`'s zip name can never drift apart. `Build-CliArgs`'s `'add'` job kind gains a `projectIds` (array) branch - comma-joined into one `-Add` token exactly like the existing `'remove'` case's bulk `projectIds` - so a UI action can install several addons (mixed CurseForge/Wago tokens) in a single job instead of N sequential ones; `Handle-JobsPost`'s validation extended to match. Needed for the new Welcome dialog's "Adopt all" below.
- **Added** - `ui/`: a first-run Welcome dialog (`#dialog-welcome`, `Components.Welcome`) - when `addons.json` has 0 records and a scan finds folders with a recognizable CurseForge/Wago id already sitting in AddOns, offers to adopt them (one bulk `add` job via the new `projectIds` server support, `Actions.adoptAll`) or Skip (remembered per-browser via `localStorage`, not persistent server state - a dismissible nicety, not a config flag). Reuses `Components.Dialogs`' existing generic show/hide/backdrop/Esc plumbing (a third `openName` value alongside `'add'`/`'confirm'`) and E7's existing `/api/scan` endpoint - no other new server surface.
- **Added** - `README.md` (repo root, external-audience rewrite) and `README.txt` (in-folder): lead with "No API key needed" and a plain install/uninstall walkthrough for `Install Furphy.cmd`/`install.ps1`, a privacy section naming every external host the app ever talks to, moved the developer-facing material (file table, CLI reference, PS 5.1 lessons learned) below the fold. Removed every remaining "needs a FREE CurseForge API key" / "Browse needs a free CurseForge API key" phrasing app-wide (the one surviving hit, a historical HTML comment in `index.html` quoting the retired panel's old copy verbatim, was reworded); kept the Settings key field's existing "Optional: CurseForge API key (adds official CurseForge metadata)" label as-is, per spec.
- **Found (machine-specific PS 5.1 quirk, new to this build)** - a `'literal' + $var + 'literal'` string concatenation used directly as an element of a multi-line `@( 'a', 'literal' + $var + 'literal', 'c' )` array literal silently splits into three SEPARATE array elements on this machine (the prefix, the variable's raw value, the suffix) instead of concatenating into one string - even though the identical expression concatenates correctly the moment it's assigned to a variable first and that variable placed in the array instead. Caught by `install.ps1`'s own launcher-file smoke test (a generated `.cmd`/`.vbs` came out with the embedded path split across three lines instead of one, which would have silently broken every real install's launch shortcut). Fixed by building each interpolated line into its own named variable before array-izing (or using double-quoted string interpolation, which does not trigger this); documented in `SPEC.md`'s new E18 section as a standing rule for this codebase, alongside the pre-existing `List[object]` `@()` quirk.
- **Verified** - offline against a throwaway fixture WoW tree (never the real WoW folder), per the standing no-CurseForge-requests rule for this build: `install.ps1 -WowPath <fixture> -NoShortcuts -NoProtocol -SkipAdopt` (exit 0, files copied, settings.json created, parse check passed, both launcher files byte-verified correct after the quirk fix above) and `-Uninstall` (app files removed, hand-seeded `addons.json`/`sync.log`/`backups\` state and the real AddOns folder left untouched) both run for real; the `-Scan`-then-bulk-`-Add` adopt pipeline was verified end-to-end EXCEPT the actual network-touching `-Add` call itself (skipped per the no-network rule) - `-Scan -Json` against the fixture (CurseForge id / Wago id / neither, one folder each) returned exactly the expected buckets, and the id-to-token-string logic that consumes that JSON was verified in isolation to produce `"1521253,wago:nKW1zLNE"` with the third folder correctly listed unmanaged. `package.ps1` run for real produced a zip whose entry list (inspected via `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile`) contains only the intended 12 root files + `ui\`, no state/log/backup/cache/probe files. `addon-server.ps1`'s VERSION-read confirmed against a live instance with a synthetic `VERSION` file - `/api/ping` echoed it exactly. `grep -rin "needs a free"` / `grep -rn "Browse needs a free CurseForge API key"` across `ui/`, `README.txt`, `README.md` both return nothing. `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-sync.ps1`/`addon-server.ps1`/`install.ps1`/`package.ps1` all clean; all four confirmed pure ASCII; `node --check ui/app.js` clean.

## Round 9

- **Rejected** - "Pinned chip tooltip condition does not match spec: code checks `Store.lastRunStatusFor(addon.name) === \"Rolled-back\"` instead of spec-required `pinnedFileId === previousFileId`" (medium, `ui/app.js:1689`). Already fixed and verified two rounds ago (see Round 2's "Fixed (high)" entry above): `Invoke-RollbackForRecord` SWAPS `fileId`<->`previousFileId` and then sets `pinnedFileId` to the just-restored (post-swap) `fileId`, so `pinnedFileId` and `previousFileId` are, by construction, two different values immediately after every real rollback - the SPEC-literal `pinnedFileId === previousFileId` check this finding asks to restore could never be true, which Round 2 reproduced live and fixed by switching to the current `lastRunStatusFor`-based signal (documented in the inline comment directly above the flagged line, `app.js:1673-1688`). Re-applying option 1 would reintroduce a dead condition and silently drop the tooltip on every real rollback. Option 2 (update SPEC.md) is also out of scope - this round is scoped to `ui/*` only, same reasoning prior rounds used for identical spec-vs-code drift findings. No change made.
- **Verified** - `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean (no edits made this round).

### server component (addon-server.ps1) - E4/E12 export-import Wago gap findings

- **Fixed (high)** - `Handle-Export` (`GET /api/export`) exported only `{projectId, name, pinnedFileId, ignoreUpdates, releaseType}` per addon - E4's original, pre-E12 shape. A Wago-sourced record's `projectId` is always `$null`, so every Wago addon round-tripped through export as an unidentifiable row. Added the additive `source`/`slug`/`wagoId`/`curseId` fields (straight off the record, same free-ride generic-property pattern already used elsewhere in this file) so a Wago row's identity survives the round trip.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Build-ImportPlan` silently dropped every imported entry with a null `projectId` (`if (-not $entry -or ($null -eq $entry.projectId)) { continue }`) - which, before the Export fix above, was every Wago entry unconditionally. Reworked the whole function to key each entry by `Get-UpdateAvailableKeyForRecord`'s existing duplex token (numeric `projectId` as a string, or `"wago:<slug>"`) instead of a bare `[int64]` projectId - this token is also exactly what addon-sync.ps1's `-Add`/`-Only`/`-Ignore` target classifier already accepts, so it flows straight into a phase's CliArgs with no extra translation. `$toAddIds`/`$pinEntries.ProjectId`/`$ignoreIds` renamed to `$toAddTargets`/`$pinEntries.Target`/`$ignoreTargets` to match; the `$entryPid`/`$pid`-collision workaround is gone entirely since there is no longer a lone int64 variable to trip over it. A `Skipped` row for an already-tracked Wago addon now also carries `wagoSlug` (null for CurseForge rows), matching the additive field every other `-Json` result row carries per SPEC's E12 section.
- **Fixed (high)** - `Start-ImportJob`'s `$existingById` map was built only from records with a non-null `projectId`, so every already-tracked Wago addon looked brand-new to `Build-ImportPlan` (would have been re-added as a duplicate the moment the Export/Build-ImportPlan fixes above let a Wago row reach it at all). Rebuilt to key uniformly via `Get-UpdateAvailableKeyForRecord`, mirroring the same helper `Handle-State`'s per-addon `updateAvailable` lookup already uses.
- **Verified** - `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` returns zero tokenizer errors after the change; `addon-server.ps1` confirmed pure ASCII (byte-scanned, zero bytes > 127).

### ui component (app.js) - ignore/unignore row not refreshing immediately

- **Fixed (high)** - the My Addons row's Status chip and the "Ignored" filter-chip count did not update immediately after the kebab menu's "Ignore updates"/"Stop ignoring" (`Actions.toggleIgnore`), the bulk selection-bar equivalent (`Actions.ignoreSelected`), or "Unpin" (`Actions.unpin`) - all three fast-op handlers replaced the whole enriched `Store.state.addons` array wholesale with `res.addons`, the raw response `POST /api/addons/{id}/ignore`/`.../unpin` returns. Per SPEC's addon-sync.ps1 `-Json` contract, that `addons` array is the CLI's bare addons.json records - it carries `ignoreUpdates`/`pinnedFileId`/etc. but none of the fields `/api/state` computes on top of them server-side (`updateAvailable`, `compat`, `tocInterfaces`, `missingDeps`, `latestGameVersions`, ...). Swapping the array for that narrower shape silently dropped those enriched fields for every row until the next `/api/state` poll caught the client back up, which is what made an already-synchronous store update + re-render look like it was "waiting for the poll." Added `Store.mergeAddons(list)`, which `Object.assign`s each incoming record onto the matching existing addon object in place (only overwriting keys the incoming record actually carries, so unrelated enriched fields and object identity both survive) instead of replacing the array; `toggleIgnore`, `ignoreSelected`, and `unpin` now call it with the fast op's response `addons` in place of the old `Store.state.addons = res.addons` assignment.
- **Verified** - `node --check ui/app.js` passes clean after the change; grepped the file for `res.addons` afterward - all three remaining sites (`ignoreSelected`, `toggleIgnore`, `unpin`) now go through `Store.mergeAddons`, none still assign to `Store.state.addons` directly. File confirmed pure ASCII in the changed region.

## Round 10 (E19 host)

- **Added** - `host\FurphyHost.cs`: the native WebView2 host window (C# 5, WinForms, compiled via `Add-Type`/csc.exe - no Visual Studio). One `Form` titled exactly `"Furphy Addon Manager"` (the exact string `curseforge-handler.vbs` already `AppActivate`s on) with `icon.ico`, a left-aligned `TabControl` with two tabs: "Furphy" (a `WebView2` on `http://localhost:<port>/?host=webview2`) and "CurseForge" (a `WebView2` on `https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons` behind a slim toolbar - Back/Forward/Home buttons plus a search box+Go that navigates to `/wow/search?search=<urlencoded>&class=addons`). `--port <n>` (falls back to `settings.json`'s `port`, found by walking up from the exe's own folder so it works whether `settings.json` sits next to the exe or two levels up in the AddonSync root, then 47831) and `--selftest <markerPath> <testPageUrl>` are the only two command-line switches. Window position/size round-trip through `settings.json`'s `hostWindow` (`{x,y,w,h}`) via a small read-modify-write-merge (never clobbers unrelated keys another process wrote) written atomically (tmp file + delete + move), tolerating a missing or corrupt `settings.json` by falling back to a centered 1320x900 default; a saved rectangle that would land entirely off every current monitor is also treated as unusable and falls back to centered.
- **Added** - `host\FurphyHost.cs` install interception: `CoreWebView2.NavigationStarting` and `NewWindowRequested` on the CurseForge tab cancel any `curseforge://...` URI, parse `addonId`/`projectId` and optional `fileId` with the same case-insensitive/any-order tolerance as `curseforge-handler.vbs`'s existing regex, check `/api/state` for whether the project is already tracked (numeric compare, not string-cast) to choose `kind:"add"` vs `kind:"install"`, `POST /api/jobs` via `HttpWebRequest`, switch to the Furphy tab, and log every step to `host.log` (found next to whichever `settings.json` was located). A separate rule catches `https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons/<slug>/install/<fileId>` links, cancels them, and posts `{kind:"add-by-slug", slug, fileId}` - since the server does not define that job kind yet (confirmed against the current `addon-server.ps1`'s `$validKinds`, which will 400 it), a non-2xx response makes the host fall back to navigating the CurseForge tab straight to the plain `/wow/addons/<slug>` page instead of leaving the cancelled navigation going nowhere. Any other `NewWindowRequested` (e.g. `target=_blank` links elsewhere on the site) is also handled rather than left to WebView2's default popup-window behavior - it loads in the same CurseForge tab, keeping this a single-window app.
- **Added** - `host\FurphyHost.cs` ad filter: reads `settings.json`'s `adFilter` (default `false`) at startup and again on every CurseForge-tab navigation start; the first time it is seen `true`, registers `AddWebResourceRequestedFilter("*", All)` plus an `AddScriptToExecuteOnDocumentCreatedAsync` CSS injector (hides `[class*="adsbygoogle"]`, `[id*="adsbygoogle"]`, `[data-ad-slot]`) exactly once, then gates actual blocking on the live (re-read) flag so toggling the setting off silently stops new blocks without needing a restart. `WebResourceRequested` matches the request's host (and parent domains, so a listed `doubleclick.net` also catches `ad.doubleclick.net`) against `host\adfilter-hosts.txt`, answering a match with an empty 204 via `CoreWebView2.Environment.CreateWebResourceResponse(null, 204, "No Content", "")`. A hard-coded allow-list (`curseforge.com`, `forgecdn.net`, `overwolf.com`, `cloudflare.com`, `cloudflareinsights.com`, `localhost`) is checked first and can never be blocked regardless of what the text file contains.
- **Added** - `host\adfilter-hosts.txt`: 153 well-known ad/tracker network hosts (Google/DoubleClick stack, RTB exchanges, Amazon's ad system, content-recommendation networks, analytics/heatmap/retargeting trackers, push-ad networks, misc ad servers and affiliate trackers), one host per line, `#`-comment lines, no URL paths, none of the never-block hosts present.
- **Added** - `host\FurphyHost.cs` `--selftest <markerPath> <testPageUrl>` mode: starts normally, waits for both `WebView2`s' `CoreWebView2InitializationCompleted`, navigates the CurseForge tab to `testPageUrl` instead of `curseforge.com`, and after 8 seconds (or immediately on a confirmed runtime-missing failure) writes `markerPath` as JSON - `{init, version, cfTabTitle, blockedRequests:[], allowedRequests:[], intercepted:[], jobPostStatus}` - via the same atomic tmp+move write as the window-bounds save, then exits. `WebView2RuntimeNotFoundException` / any `CoreWebView2InitializationCompleted` failure (from either tab) is caught and exits the process with code 3, per spec, so `Addon Manager.vbs`'s existing "prefer FurphyHost.exe, fall back to the Edge `--app` window" logic has a code to test against.
- **Added** - a small hand-rolled `MiniJson` reader/writer inside `FurphyHost.cs` (object/array/string/number/bool/null, ASCII-safe `\uXXXX`-escaped output) instead of pulling in `System.Web.Extensions.dll` (a .NET 3.5 optional-feature assembly not guaranteed present on a fresh Windows install) - used for reading/merging `settings.json`, parsing `/api/state` to check whether a project id is already tracked, and building the `/api/jobs` and selftest-marker payloads.
- **Added** - `host\build-host.ps1`: compiles `FurphyHost.cs` with the verified `Add-Type -OutputType WindowsApplication -IgnoreWarnings` recipe (referencing `System.dll`/`System.Drawing.dll`/`System.Windows.Forms.dll` plus the two WebView2 SDK assemblies from `host\lib\`) into `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe`, in a disposable child `powershell.exe` process (so re-running the build script twice in one session never hits Add-Type's "type already exists" error), then copies the three WebView2 DLLs and `icon.ico` next to the exe and prints its path. `-Clean` removes `host\bin`.
- **Verified** - `build-host.ps1` compiles clean (zero errors, `-IgnoreWarnings` so warnings never fail it) on the first attempt. Ran the real end-to-end selftest against a throwaway scratch stack (never the live app or the real WoW folder): started `addon-server.ps1 -Port 47899 -Root <AddonSync2> -AddonsPath <scratch test-addons>`, waited for `/api/ping`, copied `host\selftest.html` (a fixture referencing an ad-host pixel then, after 1s, redirecting to `curseforge://install?addonId=1521253&fileId=8720783`) into `ui\` only for the duration of the test, then ran `FurphyHost.exe --port 47899 --selftest <marker> http://localhost:47899/selftest.html` three times: (1) default settings (`adFilter` absent/false) - exit 0, marker `init:true, version:"152.0.4191.53", cfTabTitle:"Furphy host selftest", blockedRequests:[], intercepted:["curseforge://install?addonId=1521253&fileId=8720783"], jobPostStatus:202` and `host.log` showing `POST /api/jobs {"kind":"add",...} -> 202`, which the scratch server carried all the way through to a real (network-allowed, per this addon's established test-fixture status from E17) CurseForge install of BonusRollConfirm into the scratch AddOns folder; (2) `settings.json`'s `adFilter` temporarily flipped to `true` (original content backed up and restored after) - marker's `blockedRequests:["pagead2.googlesyndication.com"]` confirms the 204 short-circuit actually fires, and the repeat `curseforge://` hit correctly resolved to `kind:"install"` this time since run (1) had already tracked project 1521253; (3) a copy of `host\bin` with `WebView2Loader.dll` deliberately removed - exited with code 3 as required, `host.log` recording the `DllNotFoundException`. Scratch server stopped via `POST /api/shutdown`; `ui\selftest.html`, all three marker files, and the `WebView2Loader`-less bin copy were deleted afterward; `settings.json` restored to its pre-test content. `host\FurphyHost.cs`, `host\build-host.ps1` and `host\adfilter-hosts.txt` confirmed pure ASCII (byte-scanned, zero bytes > 127).

### Wiring the host and the E17 handler into the rest of the app

- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1`: `settings.json` (and `GET`/`PUT /api/settings`, masking unchanged - `cfApiKey` still the only masked field) gains `adFilter` (bool, default `false`) and `hostWindow` (object, default `null`) - `Get-Settings` round-trips both from disk so a Settings-page save of, say, `releaseType` never clobbers a window position `FurphyHost.exe` wrote directly. `GET /api/ping` gains `host: "webview2"|"edge-app"`, backed by a new sticky `$Script:HostKind` (starts `"edge-app"`, flips permanently to `"webview2"` the moment the static-file branch of `Invoke-Route` sees a `GET /` or `/index.html` with `?host=webview2` - the query the native host's Furphy tab, and only that tab, ever sends).
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1` job kind `add-by-slug` `{slug, fileId?}` - the piece `FurphyHost.cs`'s `HandleSlugInstall` (Round 10 above) was already built to call, which `$validKinds` didn't recognize yet. New `Get-CfCatalogueEntryBySlug` (exact, case-insensitive match against the same in-memory catalogue index `/api/cf/browse`/`Get-CfCatalogueEntry` already serve) resolves the slug; a hit rewrites the job to a normal single-`projectId` `add` and falls through to the existing CLI-process path unchanged. An unknown slug never reaches the CLI at all - it is recorded straight into job history as an already-`failed` job (`exitCode: 404`, `error: "No CurseForge addon found for slug '<slug>' (404)"`), so the host's blind `POST /api/jobs` + switch-to-Furphy-tab flow always gets a real `jobId` whose progress panel shows the failure normally, the same "404-style failed job, not an HTTP error" contract SPEC documents for it.
- **Added** - `addon-server.ps1` routes `GET /api/protocol/status`, `POST /api/protocol/register`, `POST /api/protocol/unregister` - new `Invoke-ProtocolScript` runs E17's existing, unchanged `register-protocol.ps1` with `-Json` and `-Status`/`-Register`/`-Unregister` as a hidden, output-redirected child `powershell.exe` process, quoting the (space-containing) `ROOT`-relative script path exactly the way `New-CliProcessArgs` already quotes `addon-sync.ps1`'s - same shape as `Invoke-Cli`, just targeting a different script, and used the same way (throws on failure/timeout; the three handlers catch that into a 500).
- **Added** - `Addon Manager.vbs`: prefers `host\bin\FurphyHost.exe --port <n>` (the port still read from `settings.json` via the existing `CLng`-parsed regex, unchanged) when that exe exists, over the plain Edge `--app` window; the Edge fallback (and the `Start-Process url` fallback below that, when Edge itself is missing) is otherwise untouched, so a machine where `build-host.ps1` never ran, or where `FurphyHost.exe` exits 3 for a missing WebView2 runtime, still gets the exact experience it had before this round.
- **Added** - `install.ps1`: after the existing code/`ui\` copy step (which already carried `curseforge-handler.vbs`/`register-protocol.ps1` in its `$codeFiles` allow-list since E17/E18 - verified, no change needed there), a new step copies `host\` into the deployed app - `adfilter-hosts.txt`/`build-host.ps1`/`FurphyHost.cs` at the root, `lib\` (the WebView2 SDK) recursively, and `bin\`'s actual binaries only (never the `FurphyHost.exe.WebView2\` runtime-cache folder a prior run may have left beside them) as the fallback for a machine with no compiler at all. It then looks for `csc.exe` at the two standard `%WINDIR%\Microsoft.NET\Framework(64)\v4.0.30319\` locations and, when found, runs the deployed `host\build-host.ps1` to rebuild `FurphyHost.exe` fresh at the destination (reporting success/failure); when no compiler is found there, the step is skipped with no output at all, per spec ("skip silently") - expected to be effectively unreachable on a normal Windows box, per SPEC's own "every Windows box has csc.exe" fact. `-Uninstall`'s existing generic "delete every top-level child of `$appDest` not in the keep-list" loop already removes `host\` on its own (it was never added to `$keepDirs`), so no separate uninstall handling was needed.
- **Added** - `ui\index.html`/`ui\app.js`/`ui\style.css`: Settings gains a new "Browsing" card - a toggle "Filter ads and trackers in the CurseForge tab" bound to `settings.adFilter` (`Actions.saveSettings({adFilter: ...})`, same round-trip every other Settings toggle already uses) with SPEC's exact off-by-default note text, shown only while `App.getServerHost() === "webview2"` (a new `serverHost` field on `App`, populated from `GET /api/ping`'s new `host`); running under the plain Edge app window instead shows a muted "Available in the Furphy desktop window" line, since there is no embedded CurseForge tab there to filter.
- **Added** - `ui\index.html`/`ui\app.js`/`ui\style.css`: a shared `Components.ProtocolControl` (a status chip - "Handled by Furphy" / "Not registered" / "Handled by another program" / "Checking..." - next to a toggle switch, plus a one-line explanation) painted into two places from one `Store.state.protocol` (`Actions.loadProtocolStatus`, fetched once at startup): a new row in Settings > Game, and - realizing E17's originally-spec'd but never-built status pill - the Browse > CurseForge pane, as a divider-separated row under the existing "Search on CurseForge.com" box (hidden together with it on the Wago side of the source switch, for free, since it lives inside `#cf-web-search`). The toggle calls `Actions.setProtocolRegistered(wantOn)`, which posts to the new `/api/protocol/register`/`unregister` routes above and shows a success toast with an "Undo" action (`Components.Toast.show`'s `opts` gained an optional `actionLabel`/`onAction` pair, additive - every existing call site is unaffected) that flips the toggle straight back.
- **Verified** - `$errs=$null; [System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize(...)` on `addon-server.ps1` and `install.ps1` both return zero tokenizer errors; both confirmed pure ASCII (byte-scanned, zero bytes > 127); no duplicate `function` names introduced in `addon-server.ps1`. `& 'C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe' --check ui\app.js` passes clean. `index.html` checked for balanced `<div>`/`<section>`/`<label>` tag counts and zero duplicate `id` attributes after the edits; `style.css` checked for balanced `{`/`}` counts.
- **Fixed (medium)** - `host\FurphyHost.cs`'s `EnsureAdFilterInfra` only called `CoreWebView2.AddWebResourceRequestedFilter("*", All)` when `_adFilterEnabled` was true. WebView2 only raises `WebResourceRequested` at all for requests matching a registered filter, so with the ad filter off the event never fired even once - not "fires but allows," but zero observation, meaning nothing (a selftest, `/api/diagnostics`, or a future Settings status line) could ever positively confirm "filter off -> requests seen and passed through," only the absence of a block. Split the method in two: the `AddWebResourceRequestedFilter` registration (via a new `_cfFilterInfraRegistered`-guarded block) now happens unconditionally on first CurseForge-tab init, regardless of the toggle; only the `AddScriptToExecuteOnDocumentCreatedAsync` ad-hiding CSS injection (now guarded by its own `_cfAdCssInjected` flag) stays gated on `_adFilterEnabled`, matching the "off by default, no visible behavior change" requirement. `CfWebView_WebResourceRequested` dropped its own `if (!_adFilterEnabled) return;` early-out; the toggle now gates only the block decision itself (`_adFilterEnabled && !IsAllowlisted(host) && MatchesFilterList(host)`), with every other request - allowed or filter-off - falling through to `RecordAllowed(host)` as before. Net effect: with `adFilter=false`, `allowedRequests` now genuinely reflects every request the browser saw and passed through (verified against a known ad host); with `adFilter=true`, blocking behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged from before this fix.
- **Verified** - `host\build-host.ps1 -Clean` then a plain build recompiled `FurphyHost.exe` with zero warnings-as-errors (27,136 bytes, unchanged from before the fix; all three WebView2 DLLs plus `icon.ico` copied alongside it). Re-ran the selftest against a fresh `addon-server.ps1 -Port 47899` instance and `host\selftest.html` (copied into `ui\` for the duration of the test only) twice: with `adFilter` left at its on-disk default `false`, the marker's `allowedRequests` came back `["localhost","pagead2.googlesyndication.com"]` and `blockedRequests` came back `[]` - the ad host is now positively recorded as observed-and-allowed, not just silently un-blocked; `intercepted` still contained the `curseforge://install?addonId=1521253&fileId=8720783` URI and `jobPostStatus` was still `202`, confirming the interception/job-post path is untouched by this change. With `adFilter` flipped to `true` via `PUT /api/settings` and the selftest re-run, `blockedRequests` came back `["pagead2.googlesyndication.com"]` and `allowedRequests` came back `["localhost"]`, matching the pre-fix ON-state behavior exactly - the fix only changes what is observed while the filter is off, not what gets blocked while it is on. `settings.json`'s `adFilter` was restored to `false` and `ui\selftest.html` and both marker files were deleted afterward; `addons.json` was confirmed unchanged (still empty) throughout, since the scratch server had no `-AddonsPath` configured and the selftest's own `add` job failed for that unrelated, pre-existing reason without writing any addon record. `[System.Management.Automation.PSParser]::Tokenize` on `addon-server.ps1` and `& 'C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe' --check ui\app.js` both re-confirmed clean after this fix, per the standing per-round check.

### Host DPI awareness and dark WinForms chrome fixes

- **Fixed (high)** - `host\FurphyHost.cs` had no DPI-awareness declaration at all, so on a display above 100% scaling Windows fell back to its default bitmap-stretch behavior for the whole process - the reported "the app seems fuzzy" defect. Added a new `DpiAwareness` static class (P/Invoke to `user32.dll`/`shcore.dll`, isolated per-call `try/catch` since the newer entry points do not exist on older Windows) called as the very first line of `Program.Main`, before `Application.EnableVisualStyles()` or any `Form`/`Control` is constructed: tries `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext((IntPtr)(-4))` (`DPI_AWARENESS_CONTEXT_PER_MONITOR_AWARE_V2`) first, falls back to `SetProcessDpiAwareness(2)` (`PROCESS_PER_MONITOR_DPI_AWARE`), then to `SetProcessDPIAware()` as a last resort for pre-8.1 Windows. Its `bool` return threads through into `MainForm`'s constructor as `_dpiAware` and is logged (`"dpi=<n> dpiAware=<bool>"`) to `host.log` on every startup and written into the `--selftest` marker as `dpiAware`.
- **Added** - `MainForm.AutoScaleMode = AutoScaleMode.Dpi` (set before any control is created) so WinForms rescales child control fonts/bounds automatically if the window is later dragged to a monitor with a different DPI, on top of the explicit scaling below which covers the initial layout.
- **Added** - `MainForm._effectiveDpi` (`this.DeviceDpi`, captured once after `ApplyWindowBounds()` places the window) plus `DpiScale()`/`Scaled(int)` helpers (`DeviceDpi / 96f`, rounded); every hand-built chrome dimension (nav-bar width, nav-button height, toolbar height/button sizes/positions, search box position/width, accent-bar width) now goes through `Scaled(...)` instead of being a bare 96-dpi pixel literal, so the chrome does not render tiny on a high-DPI display. `dpi` (the raw `DeviceDpi` int) is also written into the `--selftest` marker.
- **Fixed (high)** - "the left side is white": the whole shell was a stock `TabControl`/`TabPage` pair, rendered in Windows' default light system colors, clashing hard with the dark/vaporwave web UI it hosts. Replaced it with a custom dark chrome: a left `Panel` (`_navPanel`, `Scaled(64)` wide, `#12081f`) holding two flat vertical `Button`s ("Furphy"/"CurseForge", `FlatStyle.Flat`, `FlatAppearance.BorderSize=0`) built by `BuildNavButton`/`ApplyNavButtonColors` - inactive `BackColor #12081f`/`ForeColor #b9a6d6`, active `BackColor #241640`/`ForeColor #ff71ce` plus a `Scaled(3)`-px left accent bar in `#ff71ce` painted via a `Button.Paint` handler (`NavButton_Paint`, gated on a bool stashed in `Button.Tag`) rather than a child control, since `Button` does not host children reliably. The two `WebView2`s now live in a right-hand `_contentPanel` (`Dock=Fill`, `#12081f`); `ActivateTab(HostTab)` toggles `Visible`/`BringToFront` on `_furphyWebView` versus a new `_cfContainer` Panel (which itself holds the restyled toolbar + `_cfWebView`) and repaints the nav buttons' active state - `NavFurphy_Click`/`NavCf_Click` and the pre-existing `SwitchToFurphyTab()` (called from the `curseforge://` interception path) all route through it now instead of `TabControl.SelectedTab`.
- **Added** - the CurseForge toolbar (`BuildCfToolbar`) is now `#1a0b2e`, `Scaled(36)` tall; its Back/Forward/Home/Go buttons (`BuildToolbarButton`) are flat with `ForeColor #f3e9ff`, `FlatAppearance.MouseOverBackColor #2d1b4e` and `MouseDownBackColor #241640`; the search `TextBox` is `BackColor #241640`, `ForeColor #f3e9ff`, `BorderStyle.FixedSingle`. Both `WebView2`s get `DefaultBackgroundColor = Color.FromArgb(0x12, 0x08, 0x1f)` (wrapped in `try/catch` in case a future SDK ever drops the property) so there is no white flash before either page paints. `MainForm.BackColor` is `#12081f`. Title, icon, `--port`/`--selftest` behavior, `curseforge://` interception, the ad filter, and `hostWindow` persistence are all unchanged.
- **Added** - `--selftest` marker gains `tabBarPixel`/`toolbarPixel` (hex strings, e.g. `"#12081f"`): `WriteSelftestMarker` switches to the CurseForge tab, then calls `DrawToBitmap` **directly on `_navPanel` and on `_cfToolbar`** (not on the whole `Form`) and samples one pixel from each resulting bitmap. This two-separate-snapshots approach was a deliberate fix during this round's own verification: an earlier version took a single whole-`Form` `DrawToBitmap` snapshot and sampled both points out of it, which worked correctly for the nav-bar pixel but returned an unrelated light color (`#c4d2e0`, then `#d4e2ef` after retargeting away from a button) for the toolbar pixel - the CurseForge `WebView2`, a real child HWND sitting immediately below the toolbar in the same `_cfContainer`, appears to bleed into neighboring siblings' printed pixels when the whole Form is asked to `WM_PRINT` at once, even for territory the toolbar Panel logically owns. Printing each chrome Panel/Button subtree on its own sidesteps the interaction with the WebView2's HWND entirely. `tabBarPixel` samples `(10, 200)` (inside `_navPanel`'s own bitmap, well below both nav buttons so it reads the panel's own background); `toolbarPixel` samples `(10, Scaled(33))` (inside `_cfToolbar`'s own bitmap - `y=Scaled(33)` sits in the ~6px sliver below the back/forward/home buttons and the search box, which all sit in the top `Scaled(30)` of the bar's `Scaled(36)` height, proportionally at any DPI). As documented in the task spec, the WebView2 page content itself never renders into either snapshot - expected, since it lives in a separate child HWND.
- **Verified** - `host\build-host.ps1 -Clean` then a plain build recompiled `FurphyHost.exe` cleanly (31,232 bytes, zero warnings-as-errors, all three WebView2 DLLs plus `icon.ico` copied alongside it). Ran the selftest three times against a fresh scratch stack (`addon-server.ps1 -Port 47899 -Root <AddonSync2> -AddonsPath <scratch test-addons>`, `host\selftest.html` copied into `ui\` for the duration of the test only): run 1 (before the pixel-sampling fix) returned `tabBarPixel:"#12081f"` (correct) but `toolbarPixel:"#c4d2e0"` (wrong - landed on a themed button face); run 2 (toolbar sample retargeted to clear background, still whole-Form snapshot) returned `toolbarPixel:"#d4e2ef"` (still wrong, confirming the WebView2-adjacency theory rather than a button-specific issue); run 3 (per-Panel `DrawToBitmap`, this round's shipped fix) returned `{"init":true,"version":"152.0.4191.53","cfTabTitle":"Furphy host selftest","dpi":96,"dpiAware":true,"tabBarPixel":"#12081f","toolbarPixel":"#1a0b2e","blockedRequests":[],"allowedRequests":["localhost","pagead2.googlesyndication.com"],"intercepted":["curseforge://install?addonId=1521253&fileId=8720783"],"jobPostStatus":202}` - `tabBarPixel` and `toolbarPixel` now match `ChromeBg`/`ChromeBgAlt` exactly, `dpiAware` true and `dpi` 96 (this machine's actual scaling, confirming `DeviceDpi` was read correctly), interception/202 and `blockedRequests` empty with the default `adFilter=false` all unchanged from Round 10's original behavior. `host.log` confirmed the `"dpi=96 dpiAware=True"` line on every one of the three runs. Scratch server stopped via `POST /api/shutdown`; `ui\selftest.html` and all marker files deleted afterward; the test-installed addon (project 1521253, from the `curseforge://install?addonId=1521253&fileId=8720783` interception the selftest page triggers) removed via `addon-sync.ps1 -Remove 1521253 -AddonsPath <scratch test-addons> -Json` (`addons.json` confirmed back to `[]`, scratch `test-addons\` folder confirmed empty). `host\FurphyHost.cs` byte-scanned afterward: zero bytes above 127 (pure ASCII, unchanged from before this round).

## Round 11 (Lofi Night theme)

### E15b - Lofi Night theme, and the new default

- **Added** - a fourth Appearance option, requested by Eric ("pixel night cityscape, lofi cats"), and now the DEFAULT theme (Dark/Light/Vaporwave stay selectable and unchanged): `ui/index.html`'s theme segmented control gains a "Lofi Night" button (`data-theme-value="lofi"`), listed first since it is now the default; `<html data-theme="lofi">` is the shipped default markup. `ui/app.js`'s `Prefs` (`isKnownTheme`/`readTheme`/`applyTheme`/`setTheme`) now recognizes four values instead of three, with every not-yet-set/corrupt/unknown fallback changed from `"vaporwave"` to `"lofi"` (the comment documenting this was updated in the same edit, unlike Round 8's stale-comment note about the Round 7 default). `ui/manifest.json`'s `background_color`/`theme_color` moved from Vaporwave's `#1a0b2e` to Lofi Night's `#0f1226` so the Edge app window/taskbar match the new default on first paint.
- **Added** - `ui/style.css` gains a complete `:root[data-theme="lofi"]` token block, same pattern as Light/Vaporwave, redefining every color token the dark theme defines using the brief's anchors: backgrounds #0f1226/#161b34/#1e2445/#262d55, border #343b66 (border-soft = the bg-2 value, border-hover a lighter #4a5390, matching the existing dark/vaporwave convention), text #f2eee6, muted #b3b1c9, accent warm amber #ffb86b/#ffc98a (hover)/#ff9f3d (active)/#1a1230 (on-accent text), success mint #6fe3b4, warning #ffd166, danger #ff6b8b, info #7cc4ff, plus two theme-only tokens (`--secondary: #a980ff` dusky purple, `--teal: #39d3c3`, doubling as `--focus-ring` per the brief) and `--accent-active` (the amber button's pressed shade, same role as Vaporwave's own token). Unlike Vaporwave, this theme ALSO overrides `--radius-sm`/`--radius`/`--radius-lg` (6/10/16px, up from the shared 4/8/12px default) for the brief's "soft rounded lofi feel" - the roundest corners of any theme, a deliberate deviation from Vaporwave's shared-geometry approach. The `.select` chevron (a stroke-baked data-URI, same reason Light/Vaporwave each carry their own copy) got a matching Lofi-stroke (`#b3b1c9`) version.
- **Fixed (during verification, before ship)** - the brief's literal `--text-faint` anchor (#7d7b9c) measured 4.18:1 against `--bg-1` and as low as 3.58:1 against `--bg-3` (both real surfaces this token sits on - `.addon-author`/`.browse-card-author`/`.drawer-author` on a bg-1 row/card, `.source-pill`'s own bg-3 fill) - short of the brief's 4.5:1 floor. Lightened to `#9795b0` (verified live via `getComputedStyle` + a relative-luminance contrast calculator run in the actual page, compositing each real rgba background over its true backdrop rather than trusting the token in isolation): 6.38:1/5.84:1/5.20:1/4.56:1 against bg-0/bg-1/bg-2/bg-3 respectively - the tightest real case (`.source-pill` on bg-3) now clears the floor with headroom, while staying visibly dimmer than `--text-muted` (8.10:1 on the same bg-1), preserving the faint-below-muted ordering every other theme keeps. Every other token (chips, composited against their real tinted-and-backdropped surface, not the flat token) verified 4.56-11.74:1, tightest at `.chip-danger`'s 5.10:1.
- **Added** - signature touches, all additive `:root[data-theme="lofi"]`-scoped CSS layered on the token block: a warm amber `box-shadow` bloom on `.btn-accent` (intensifying on hover, snapping to `--accent-active` while pressed) and `.nav-item.is-active`, mirroring Vaporwave's glow language in amber instead of pink; a single muted-purple (`--secondary` at ~32% alpha) inset outline shared by every `.chip` variant and the active `.filter-chip` (simpler than Vaporwave's per-color neon outlines, reading as a soft "sticker" edge); a gentle drop shadow plus a faint 1px pixel-dither top edge (`repeating-linear-gradient` in `--secondary`, `::before` on `.settings-group`/`.browse-card`, clipped by the theme's own larger corner radius via `overflow:hidden`) on the app's two card containers, and the same soft shadow on `.addon-row`.
- **Added** - the pixel night cityscape: a real inline `<svg class="lofi-cityscape">` (not a CSS gradient, unlike Vaporwave's `::after` grid) positioned and layered identically to Vaporwave's sidebar decoration - absolutely positioned behind the sidebar's real children via the same `z-index:1`-on-children / `pointer-events:none` technique, 140px tall, bleeding edge to edge. Built from a coarse pixel grid (crisp `<rect>`s, `shape-rendering="crispEdges"`, no rounding): a vertical sky gradient, 14 twinkling stars (4 staggered `@keyframes lofi-twinkle` timings so the sky never blinks in unison), a pixel-blob crescent moon (two overlapping blobs, the second in the sky's own color biting a crescent shadow out of the first), two back-layer building silhouettes for depth and seven front-layer silhouettes carrying ~60 lit windows (mostly warm amber, a handful of teal and dusky-purple per the brief), and two lofi cats: one sitting upright on a rooftop with a separate `.lofi-cat-tail` group that sways via `@keyframes lofi-tail-sway` (`transform-origin` pinned to the tail's own base coordinate in the markup, not the shape's bounding-box center), one curled asleep on another rooftop with its tail tucked in and static. Both cats got a small amber eye-glint accent. A third, tiny curled cat (`.lofi-brand-cat`, its own small inline `<svg>`) sits beside the brand icon in the sidebar header. All three are real DOM elements (needed for the per-child CSS animations above) rather than a data-URI background image, hidden on every other theme via each class's own `display:none` default. Every animation is wrapped by a single `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` override that sets `animation: none` on the star/tail classes.
- **Fixed (during art iteration, before ship)** - the cats' first-pass fill color (`#150f26`, chosen to read as a near-black nighttime silhouette) was close enough in value to the front buildings' own silhouette color (`#0a0d1e`) that the cats' lower bodies visually merged into the rooftop they were sitting on, confirmed via a zoomed isolated-crop live-browser render (a throwaway local preview page, deleted afterward) before it shipped. Relightened to a moonlit dusky-violet `#4a3f78` - clearly distinct from both the sky and the building silhouettes at any zoom level - while keeping the same amber eye-glint accent.
- **Verified** - live in a real browser against `?mock=1` (a local static file server serving `ui\`, since `file://` doesn't render interactively in this tool) at the app's documented 1320x900 window size, with Lofi Night as the loaded default (no flash of another theme): My Addons (table, status/compat/missing-deps/source-badge chips, filter chips, job panel) and the detail drawer (amber-glowing "Update now" primary action, tab underline, purple logo-fallback tiles) both readable and correctly colored; Browse (CurseForge no-key/keyless-index panel, the Wago Addons tab's card grid with glowing Install buttons and an "Installed" state) and Settings (radio buttons/toggle switch native `accent-color` in amber, the Appearance segmented control with "Lofi Night" first and marked active, dither-and-shadow confirmed present on `.settings-group`/`.browse-card` via `getComputedStyle(el, '::before')` since it's intentionally too faint to eyeball at normal screenshot scale) all matched the brief. Switched Lofi Night -> Dark -> Light -> Vaporwave -> Lofi Night via Settings > Appearance and confirmed all three other themes render pixel-identical to their pre-round state (verified `data-theme` and each segmented button's `is-active` class via DOM query, not just eyeballing scaled screenshots) - no lofi color, radius, glow, or cityscape/cat markup leaks into any of them, since every new rule is scoped under `:root[data-theme="lofi"]` or gated behind an `.lofi-*` class's own `display:none` default. Theme choice persisted across a full page reload with no flash of the wrong theme; Esc still closes the drawer correctly. Zero console errors throughout. `& 'C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe' --check ui\app.js` passes clean; `style.css` brace count balanced (442 open/442 close) before and after the contrast fix.
